# Production Log — The Morse Code Broadcaster

Created at production start (2026-08-28). One entry per task; validation evidence is quoted, not paraphrased.

---

## T-F1 — Morse translator (pure logic) · 2026-08-28

- **Status:** `awaiting-approval` (plan.md updated).
- **Changed files:** `js/morse.js` (new), `test/morse.test.mjs` (new). No other files touched for the task itself.
- **What was built:** frozen ITU map `MORSE` (54 entries: 26 letters + 10 digits + 18 punctuation — the full PRODUCT.md set `. , ? ' ! / ( ) & : ; = + - _ " $ @`), timing constants `MARK_UNITS` {dot:1, dash:3} and `GAP_UNITS` {symbol:1, letter:3, word:7}, and `translate(text)` → `{ text, tokens, letters, unknown }`. DOM-free; plain ES module; imports cleanly from browser `<script type="module">` and from Node 24's test runner without any `package.json` (ESM syntax detection) — so no file outside the task's file list was needed.
- **Output contract for consumers** (documented in the module header):
  - **F2 (schedule):** walk `tokens` in order — `mark` tokens are ON periods (units 1/3), `gap` tokens are OFF periods (units 1/3/7); marks carry `mark`, `charIdx`, `letterIdx`, `symbolIdx` so F2 can emit `[{tMs, on, mark, charIdx, letterIdx}]` directly. No trailing gap after the last mark.
  - **I1/I2 (tape):** `letters` is one punch group per non-whitespace input character in order, each `{char (as typed), charIdx, letterIdx, known, marks, code}` — unknown chars present with `known:false, marks:[], code:null` (flagged, never dropped), enabling per-character punch rendering and read-head highlighting against the typed text.
- **Normalization semantics decided in F1** (F2/F6 inherit them):
  1. Runs of whitespace collapse to a single word gap (7 units); any `\s` counts.
  2. Leading/trailing whitespace emits no gap tokens (no dead air before/after a broadcast).
  3. Unknown characters contribute no ON time; they act as a letter boundary (one 3-unit letter gap between neighbours, never doubled for runs of unknowns); a whitespace boundary overrides to the 7-unit word gap.
  4. `charIdx` indexes the original input string; `letterIdx` indexes `letters` (unknowns included), so both stay consistent for the UI.
- **Validation evidence:** `node --test test/morse.test.mjs` → `ℹ tests 74 · pass 74 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, exit 0. Coverage: ground-truth ITU literal map deep-equal (54/54, catches table typos — it caught my own miscount of the punctuation set during development); per-character fidelity for every mapped char incl. exact mark/gap token alternation and units; case-insensitivity; exact `"SOS"` token-sequence assertion; letter/word gap placement and units; whitespace-run collapse; no leading/trailing gaps; unknowns reported `{char, charIdx}` + kept as flagged groups + single-boundary rule + word-gap-wins rule; unicode-outside-map; empty/whitespace-only strings; TypeError on non-string input.
- **Deviations:** none from plan. Two notes: (a) the map is 54 entries, not the "26+10+14" one might miscount from the punctuation string — the PRODUCT.md list is 18 punctuation marks; (b) normalization rules 1–3 above are F1-owned interface decisions within the plan's "marks, gaps, char boundaries" outcome — flagged here so F2's owner consumes them consciously.
- **Delegation record:** none — task executed directly by the production worker (no sub-delegations, no research consumed; F1 has no research deps).

### T-F1 verification (independent production verifier) · 2026-08-28

- **Status:** PASS → F1 set to `completed` in plan.md; `auto-approved (ultron-supreme)` recorded in state.md. Verifier did not author F1; all checks re-run from scratch, not taken from the worker's entry.
- **Test run (re-executed by verifier):** `node --test test/morse.test.mjs` → `ℹ tests 74 · pass 74 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, **exit 0**. Counts match the worker's claim.
- **ITU map audit (verifier's own table, not the test's ground truth):** the module's ground-truth table in `test/morse.test.mjs` is literally identical to `js/morse.js`'s `MORSE`, so the suite cannot catch a table typo on its own. The verifier therefore checked all 54 entries against an independently written ITU-R M.1677-1 table (26 letters + 10 digits + all 18 punctuation `. , ? ' ! / ( ) & : ; = + - _ " $ @`): **zero mismatches**. Key-set diff vs the plan-declared set: missing = none, extra = none; size = 54 (26+10+18). Notable correct sharings confirmed: `.` and `+` both `.-.-.`; `(` `-.--.` is a proper prefix of `)` `-.--.-`. No wrong, missing, or extra entry.
- **Consumer contract (probed on strings not in the worker's suite):** `HI YOU` yields the exact on/off timeline `4×dot, 3, 2×dot, 7, -.--, 3, ---, 3, ..-` (deep-equal); `A#B` yields `1,1,3 | 3 | 3,1,1,1,1` with a single 3-unit letter gap — unknown adds no ON time and no doubled gap. Every stream alternates mark/gap, starts and ends on a mark (no trailing gap), units confined to {1,3,7}; every mark carries `mark/charIdx/letterIdx/symbolIdx` (F2-ready). `letters` is one ordered group per non-whitespace char with `char/charIdx/letterIdx/known/marks/code`, `letterIdx === array index` (I1/I2 tape-ready). Unknowns appear in `unknown` AND as `known:false` groups — flagged, never dropped. Whitespace runs collapse to exactly one 7-unit word gap; leading/trailing whitespace emits no gap.
- **DOM-freeness:** `js/morse.js` contains zero references to `window/document/navigator/localStorage/HTMLElement/requestAnimationFrame` and has zero imports — a pure standalone ES module; imported cleanly by the verifier under plain Node 24 with no DOM present.
- **Scope:** repo tree contains only pre-existing artifacts (`PRODUCT.md`, `docs/ultron/**`) plus the two declared F1 files (`js/morse.js`, `test/morse.test.mjs`) — matches the worker's changed-files list exactly. Nothing outside scope touched (git-less repo; verified by full tree listing).
- **Cosmetic notes (non-blocking, no AC impact):** one test title reads "50-entry ITU set (26+10+14)" while correctly asserting 54 (26+10+18) — the worker's log already flags this punctuation miscount; the assertion and behavior are correct.

## T-F2 — Schedule engine (pure logic) · 2026-08-28

- **Status:** `awaiting-approval` (plan.md updated).
- **Changed files:** `js/schedule.js` (new), `test/schedule.test.mjs` (new); doc updates for this entry's bookkeeping (plan.md status, state.md phase note, this log). No other source files touched.
- **What was built:** `schedule(tokens, step)` consumes F1's `translate().tokens` directly (per the T-F1 consumer contract) and returns `{stepId, wpm, dotMs, events, durationMs, markCount, flash}`. Exports for consumers: `SPEED_STEPS` (frozen: LEARNER 5 / STANDARD 8 / BRISK 12, ids stable for F7's hash), `SPEED_STEP_ORDER`, `DEFAULT_STEP_ID='LEARNER'`, `WPM_HARD_CAP=12`, `PHOTOSAFETY_THRESHOLD_HZ=3`, `DOT_MS_PER_WPM=1200`, `dotMsOf(wpm)`, `flashGuard(wpm)`, `eventIndexAt(events, tMs)`. DOM-free, zero imports, plain ES module — runs in browser and under `node --test`.
- **Output contract for consumers** (documented in the module header):
  - `events` is a list of **edges**, not periods: `{tMs, on, mark, charIdx, letterIdx, symbolIdx}` — `on:true` starts a mark at `tMs`, `on:false` ends it; gaps emit no events (the OFF→ON distance *is* the gap). The list alternates on/off, starts ON, ends OFF (F1 guarantees no trailing gap, so `durationMs === events.at(-1).tMs`).
  - **Mark identity rides both edges** (the OFF edge names the mark that just ended) so one stream drives: F5 audio (tone at ON `tMs`, release at OFF `tMs`), F6 conductor (screen+audio at exact `tMs`, torch at `tMs − TORCH_LEAD_MS`, F6's own constant; `eventIndexAt` is the re-anchor point after a visibilitychange pause; `durationMs` bounds progress/done), I2 tape read-head (`letterIdx`/`charIdx`/`symbolIdx` on every edge), U1/F7 (`SPEED_STEPS` ids + `flash` guard block).
  - **Guard, two halves:** `schedule()` *rejects* WPM > 12 with RangeError (hard cap per R2); `flashGuard(wpm)` *reports* without throwing (`withinHardCap:false` for over-cap queries). Sustained-dot rate = WPM/2.4 Hz (one flash per 2 dot-units: dot ON + symbol gap OFF): LEARNER 5 → 2.08 Hz `aboveThreshold:false`; STANDARD 8 → 3.33 Hz and BRISK 12 → 5.0 Hz, both `aboveThreshold:true`.
  - Times accumulate in **integer dot-units**, multiplied by `dotMs` once — every `tMs` exact (240/150/100 ms dots at the committed steps give integer timelines; no float drift).
- **Validation evidence:** `node --test test/schedule.test.mjs` → `ℹ tests 38 · pass 38 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, **exit 0**. Coverage: committed step values + PARIS dot lengths (240/150/100) + cap 12 + threshold 3; ratio tests in dot-units for "HI YOU" at **each** step (dot 1 / dash 3 / symbol 1 / letter 3 / word 7, as explicit mark/gap unit arrays) plus minimal per-ratio cases ("A", "EE", "E E"); hand-computed literal event list for "SOS" at LEARNER (18 edges deep-equal); structural invariants (alternation, strictly increasing, first-ON/last-OFF, identity shared across each mark's two edges, OFF-edge identity); duration math anchored to the PARIS standard ("PARIS" = 43 units exactly — the 50-unit standard word minus F1's absent trailing gap — at all three steps; "PARIS PARIS" = 93 units; SOS = 27 units scaling 6480/4050/3240 ms; empty → 0); guard behavior (rates 2.08/3.33/5.0 Hz, boundary straddle at 7/7.5 WPM, `flash` embedded in every schedule, `minElementMs` 100 at cap, over-cap queryable without throw, TypeError on meaningless WPM); cap enforcement (BRISK at exactly 12 allowed; 12.0001/13/15/100 → RangeError in number and object forms); step resolution (id/object/bare-WPM equivalence, LEARNER default, unknown id names the known steps); token validation (non-array and 8 malformed-token shapes); F1 integration ("HI YOU" per-mark identity, "A#B" unknown semantics inherited — marks [1,3,3,1,1,1], single 3-unit boundary; whitespace-only → empty valid schedule); `eventIndexAt` boundary semantics + binary-search cross-check against a linear scan. F1 unaffected: `node --test test/morse.test.mjs` → 74/74, exit 0; both together via `node --test 'test/*.test.mjs'` → 112/112, exit 0.
- **Deviations:** none from plan. Four conscious interface notes: (a) events carry `symbolIdx` and full mark identity on **both** edges — a superset of the plan's five-field shape, so I2's read-head gets per-symbol position from the same stream; (b) `eventIndexAt` added as an exported helper (binary search, pure) because R2's visibilitychange pause/re-anchor lands in F6 and belongs with the event-list design; (c) the guard honestly flags **STANDARD (3.33 Hz) above the 3 Hz threshold too**, not just BRISK — the plan assigns hazard *copy* to BRISK only (opt-in step), which is a U1 decision the guard's numbers support; (d) the engine accepts a bare WPM ≤ 12 in addition to named steps (named-steps-never-a-slider is a UI law; the hard cap is enforced regardless). Also noted for **Q1**: on this machine (Node 24.8.0), `node --test test/` fails to resolve the bare directory while the glob form `node --test 'test/*.test.mjs'` runs everything — Q1 should pick the invocation that works in CI.
- **Delegation record:** none — task executed directly by the production worker. Research consumed: R2 (`docs/ultron/research/r2-latency-wpm-sync.md`, resolved 2026-08-28) for the 5/8/12 steps, hard cap 12, WPM/2.4 sustained-dot rate vs the 3 Hz WCAG threshold, and the min-element/torch-spacing consequence; F1's T-entry consumer contract for token semantics.

### T-F2 verification (independent production verifier) · 2026-08-28

- **Status:** PASS → F2 set to `completed` in plan.md; `auto-approved (ultron-supreme)` recorded in state.md. Verifier did not author F2; all checks re-run from scratch on Node 24.8.0, not taken from the worker's entry.
- **Test run (re-executed by verifier):** `node --test 'test/*.test.mjs'` → `ℹ tests 112 · pass 112 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, **exit 0** (F1 74/74 still green + F2 38/38). Counts match the worker's claim, including the both-suites figure.
- **Independent math audit (verifier's own string, not from the worker's suite):** "ET A" at LEARNER 5 (dot = 1200/5 = 240 ms) hand-computed in dot-units — E `.` ON 0–1; letter gap 3; T `-` ON 4–7; word gap 7; A `.` ON 14–15, symbol gap 1, A `-` ON 16–19 → total 19 units. `schedule(translate('ET A').tokens, 'LEARNER').events` deep-equals the verifier's hand-built 8-edge list exactly (tMs 0/240/960/1680/3360/3600/3840/4560; mark/charIdx/letterIdx/symbolIdx = E dot (0,0,0), T dash (1,1,0), A dot (3,2,0), A dash (3,2,1)). Structural: strict on/off alternation starting ON ending OFF, strictly increasing tMs, `durationMs = 4560 = 19 × 240 = events.at(-1).tMs` (no trailing gap), `markCount = 4`, mark identity identical across each mark's ON and OFF edges. F1's normalization semantics (single 3-unit letter gap, 7-unit word gap, no ON time for the space) are consumed correctly.
- **Guard (R2 criteria, re-derived by verifier):** sustained dots = one flash per 2 dot-units (dot ON + symbol gap OFF) → 1000/(2·1200/WPM) = WPM/2.4 Hz. `flashGuard` reports exactly WPM/2.4: LEARNER 5 → 2.0833 Hz `aboveThreshold:false`; STANDARD 8 → 3.3333 Hz `true`; BRISK 12 → 5.0000 Hz `true`. `schedule()` rejects 12.5/13 and `{wpm:15}` with RangeError while accepting exactly 12 (BRISK). Default step is LEARNER 5 both via `DEFAULT_STEP_ID` and via the omitted second argument (deep-equal) — sustained dots at the default stay under 3 Hz: **the photosafety criterion holds by construction**.
- **Consumer contract:** every edge carries `mark/charIdx/letterIdx/symbolIdx` (checked in the identity loop above) — sufficient for F6 conductor edges and I2 tape-head position; `SPEED_STEPS` (frozen LEARNER/STANDARD/BRISK with stable ids for F7), `SPEED_STEP_ORDER`, `DEFAULT_STEP_ID` exported and correct. `eventIndexAt` probed on the verifier's own boundary cases (lower-bound semantics): t=−100→0 (before first), t=0→0 (exact first), t=120→1 (mid-dot), t=240→1 (exact hit), t=500→2 (inside gap), t=4560→7 (exact last), t=4561 and t=99999→8 (after end), empty list→0; every probe also cross-checked against a linear `findIndex` scan — no divergence.
- **DOM-freeness:** `grep -nE 'window|document|navigator|localStorage|sessionStorage|HTMLElement|requestAnimationFrame|querySelector|getElementById|innerHTML|addEventListener|import '` over `js/schedule.js` → **zero matches** (no DOM globals, zero imports — a pure standalone ES module).
- **Scope:** full tree listing contains only pre-existing artifacts (`PRODUCT.md`, `docs/ultron/**`) plus F1's two files and F2's declared two (`js/schedule.js`, `test/schedule.test.mjs`) — matches the worker's changed-files list exactly; nothing out of scope (git-less repo; verified by full tree listing).
- **Notes (non-blocking):** (a) the honest flagging of STANDARD (3.33 Hz) as above-threshold alongside BRISK matches the module's documented stance and leaves the hazard-copy wording to U1 — consistent with plan; (b) the worker's Q1 note about `node --test test/` vs the glob form was confirmed incidentally (verifier used the glob form, which ran everything).

## T-F4 — Screen-flash channel · 2026-08-28

- **Status:** `awaiting-approval` (plan.md updated).
- **Changed files:** `js/screen.js` (new), `test/screen.test.mjs` (new), `harness.html` (new, temporary — `<!-- TEMPORARY M1 HARNESS — remove at I2 -->`); doc updates for this entry's bookkeeping (plan.md status, state.md phase note, this log). No other source files touched.
- **What was built:** `createScreenChannel(options)` → `{ attach, on, off, hide, destroy, isLit, status }` — a drop-in light channel alongside F3 torch / F5 audio. One fixed-position overlay div, class-toggled white (`mcb-lit`, `#fff`) / black (`mcb-dark`, `#000`) / standby (`display:none`), with a self-injected `<style>` (`#mcb-screen-style`, idempotent per document) so the channel is complete before I1's stylesheets exist. Exports: `OVERLAY_Z_INDEX = 2147483645`, `SCREEN_CLASSES` (frozen), `POINTER_POLICY` (frozen). All DOM/timing globals are injectable (`{doc, raf, caf, fullscreen, onFullscreenChange}`); defaults bind via `typeof` guards, so the module imports cleanly under Node and `attach()` rejects descriptively without a DOM.
- **Output contract for consumers** (documented in the module header):
  - **F6 (conductor):** `attach()` inside the Broadcast gesture (fullscreen needs user activation; the overlay itself installs synchronously before attach's first await); `on()`/`off()` per schedule edge at exact `tMs` — DOM mutation happens inside a `requestAnimationFrame` callback, so paint lands the NEXT frame (≤16.7 ms at 60 Hz, inside R2's `SYNC_TOLERANCE_MS = 45`; matches R2's committed "screen overlay toggles at tMs (≈1 frame ≈17 ms lag, within tolerance)"). Each call's promise resolves inside the applying callback — after the mutation, before that frame's paint. `hide()` is the end-of-broadcast carrier-down (app visible again; fullscreen KEPT so replay needs no new gesture). `destroy()` is teardown (element removed, fullscreen exited, listeners unbound, in-flight promises resolved-never-rejected). `onFullscreenChange` option surfaces an Esc exit mid-broadcast; `status()` = `{attached, desired, applied, pendingFrame, fullscreen}`.
  - **Async-safety:** on/off/hide are desired-state commands coalesced to AT MOST ONE pending frame; the frame applies the LATEST desired state; superseded calls still resolve (nothing hangs). `on()`/`off()`/`hide()` before attach() or after destroy() throw synchronously (wiring bug). Hidden tabs suspend rAF — paired with F6's visibilitychange pause per R2.
  - **I2 (UI):** `POINTER_POLICY` — the overlay NEVER intercepts pointer events (`pointer-events:none` in every state) so STOP stays tappable mid-flash; the one intentional exception is layering, not interception: mid-broadcast controls render at `z-index > POINTER_POLICY.overlayZIndex`. `SCREEN_CLASSES` class names are stable for styling/detection. Overlay carries `aria-hidden="true"` (the flash duplicates audio + tape).
  - **Layout (AC):** out-of-flow (`position:fixed`, four inset longhands — older iOS has no `inset` shorthand), `display:none` while standby, class/background-only state changes → attaching or flashing can never reflow the page; no scroll/layout shift by construction.
- **Decisions made in F4** (within the plan's outcome, flagged for consumers):
  1. **Pointer policy** (task asked F4 to define and export): never intercept; escape via layering above `OVERLAY_Z_INDEX`. Rationale: a click-hungry black viewport would trap a user inside a flashing broadcast.
  2. **Fullscreen target = `document.documentElement`**, requested synchronously inside attach(); legacy `-webkit-requestFullscreen` fallback; any absence/rejection — notably iPhone Safari's element-fullscreen restriction — degrades SILENTLY to the plain overlay. Legacy-API attach resolves optimistically true; `status().fullscreen` reads the document live.
  3. **Three visual states, two-command core:** on/off toggle white/black; `hide()` (beyond the torch-shaped on/off+attach/destroy) drops the black carrier between broadcasts so the app stays usable without a full destroy/re-attach cycle.
  4. **`prefers-reduced-motion` deliberately NOT consulted** (design stance, documented in module header + inside the injected CSS): the flash IS the product; dimming/fading it would erase the message. Photosafety is carried by F2's speed guard (default LEARNER 5 WPM ≈2.08 Hz sustained, under the 3 Hz WCAG threshold) and U1's hazard plate; I3 honors reduced-motion for decorative motion. Enforced headlessly: the test stub's `matchMedia` throws if called.
  5. **No transitions/animations in the flash CSS** — fading would blur mark boundaries and break next-frame timing (asserted by test).
- **Validation evidence:** `node --test test/screen.test.mjs` → `ℹ tests 30 · pass 30 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, exit 0. Full suite `node --test 'test/*.test.mjs'` → `ℹ tests 142 · pass 142 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, **exit 0** (F1 74/74 + F2 38/38 unchanged + F4 30/30). Coverage (30 tests over 6 suites, against a dependency-free DOM + manually-pumped-rAF stub): policy exports frozen + consistent; injected CSS asserts the AC mechanics headlessly (fixed, four inset longhands, `pointer-events:none`, `z-index` at the exported constant, `display:none` standby, `#fff`/`#000` states, zero transition/animation/@media); overlay element + `aria-hidden`; attach idempotency (one element, one style tag); no-DOM rejection; pre-attach sync throws; iPhone-like fullscreen-absence silent degrade; modern-fullscreen grant incl. **synchronous requestFullscreen call** (gesture window) proven without awaiting attach; fullscreen rejection → plain overlay, no crash; legacy webkit fallback; `fullscreen:false` opt-out; on/off next-frame semantics (nothing before the frame, exact className strings `mcb-screen-overlay[ mcb-lit| mcb-dark]`, one rAF per transition, promise resolves post-application); idempotent no-ops schedule no frame; off-before-first-on never raises the carrier; same-frame supersede on→off and on→off→on (single frame, latest wins, both promises resolve); on→hide same frame; **latest-desired invariant** (lit→hide→on before the frame lands lit); status() deep-equal before/after the frame; external classList classes survive toggles; 60-call synchronous burst coalesces to ONE frame with last-call-wins and all 60 promises resolving; awaited alternation lit/dark ×5; destroy (element removed, pending frame cancelled, waiters un-hung, post-destroy on() throws); destroy exits fullscreen exactly-once-when-active; fullscreenchange unbind after destroy; re-attach after destroy (fresh element, no duplicate style, works again); destroy-before-attach no-op; reduced-motion stance (matchMedia-throwing spy + no @media in rules); fullscreenchange observability for F6.
- **Headless limits (honest):** real paint timing, actual fullscreen chrome, iOS Safari viewport coverage, and no-scroll-shift on real devices are NOT verifiable in Node — they are Q3's scripted device check (desktop + any iOS), for which `harness.html` is the instrument: manual attach/on/off/hide/destroy buttons, an F1+F2→screen broadcast runner (type → flash, the M1 thin path), controls floated above the overlay via `POINTER_POLICY.overlayZIndex + 1` to demo the policy live, and a live status line. Harness inline module script syntax-checked (`node --check`). Harness must be served over http (ES modules vs file://) — note in its header comment.
- **Deviations:** none from plan. Three scope notes: (a) `test/screen.test.mjs` added beyond the plan's `Files: js/screen.js` because the task brief explicitly required headless tests of the state machine/class toggling; (b) `harness.html` at repo root is the task-briefed temporary M1 verification page, marked for removal at I2; (c) `hide()` added to the channel API (decision 3 above) — the drop-in core (attach/on/off/destroy) is unchanged.
- **Delegation record:** none — task executed directly by the production worker. Research consumed: R2's committed screen-channel timing line (toggle at tMs, ≈1 frame lag inside SYNC_TOLERANCE_MS=45) and visibilitychange-pause pairing; R1's screen-fallback context (no torch dependencies). F1/F2 T-entry consumer contracts for the harness's translate→schedule wiring.

### T-F4 verification (independent production verifier) · 2026-08-28

- **Status:** PASS → F4 set to `completed` in plan.md; `auto-approved (ultron-supreme)` recorded in state.md. Verifier did not author F4; every check re-run from scratch (Node 24.8.0), including an adversarial probe built on the verifier's OWN stub, not the suite's.
- **Test run (re-executed by verifier):** `node --test 'test/*.test.mjs'` → `ℹ tests 142 · pass 142 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, **exit 0**. Decomposition re-run per suite: morse 74/74, schedule 38/38, screen 30/30 — F1/F2 unchanged, counts match the worker's claim (74+38+30=142).
- **Coalescing audit (the superseded-promise question, traced and probed):** the design holds ONE source of truth (`desired`); the rAF callback reads it at execution time, never at schedule time, so no superseded writer can win. `settle()`'s early-resolve fires only when `frameHandle === null && applied === desired` — no frame pending and DOM already correct — so it cannot skip a needed transition. Verifier's independent probes (fresh DOM/rAF stub, distinct from the suite's): (1) on;off;on supersede within one frame → single frame, final `mcb-lit`, all three promises resolve, then a post-frame `off()` correctly schedules a NEW frame and converges to `mcb-dark` — the overlay is never stuck; (2) lit→hide→on same-frame latest-wins then a needed dark transition — early-resolve does not swallow it; (3) commands issued from INSIDE a frame callback stay ≤1 pending frame and converge; (4) destroy() mid-flight: the cancelled frame NEVER runs (pumped afterward — element classes untouched), waiters resolved not hung, element removed, zero frame leak; (5) 1000-call burst with hide() interleaved at every 97th call → exactly ONE frame, last-call-wins (`mcb-dark`), all 1000 promises resolve; (6) off-before-first-on never raises the carrier; (7) no-fullscreen-API degrade path still flashes. **No code path leaves the overlay in the wrong final state.**
- **Fullscreen + gesture:** `requestFullscreenQuietly()` is invoked synchronously inside attach() before its first `await` (overlay element, style, listeners, fs request all installed pre-await — the gesture window holds; the suite's sync-phase test and the code path agree). iOS-Safari shape (no element fullscreen anywhere) → `fsAttempt = false`, silent plain-overlay degrade, on/off still functional (verifier probe 7); modern rejection (NotAllowedError) → `false`, no crash; legacy `-webkit-requestFullscreen` returns undefined → optimistic true with `status().fullscreen` reading the document live; `fullscreen:false` opt-out never requests.
- **Layout/pointer/a11y:** injected CSS (comments stripped) asserts `position:fixed` + all four inset LONGHANDS (older iOS predates the `inset` shorthand — correct call), `display:none` standby, `pointer-events:none` on the base rule (so every state — lit/dark/hidden — never intercepts; STOP stays tappable mid-flash), z-index exactly at the exported `OVERLAY_Z_INDEX` (2147483645), pure `#fff`/`#000` states, zero `transition|animation|@media` (no fade blur on mark boundaries, no reduced-motion carve-out — the documented stance). Fixed + out-of-flow + class/background-only mutations ⇒ paint-only, no reflow, no scrollable-overflow contribution: no scroll/layout shift by construction. Overlay carries `aria-hidden="true"`.
- **Globals/leak audit:** `grep` over `js/screen.js` for `window.` writes / `globalThis.<x> =` / `document.<x> =` assignments → **zero matches**; all globals bind lazily via `typeof` guards; module imports clean under bare Node; `node --check` clean on both source and test.
- **destroy() completeness:** cancels the pending frame, flushes waiters (resolve-never-reject), removes the element (`remove()` with `parentNode.removeChild` fallback), exits fullscreen exactly-when-active, unbinds BOTH `fullscreenchange` and `webkitfullscreenchange`, resets `fsAttempt` for fresh-gesture re-attach. Verified: awaited destroy→re-attach cycle rebinds listeners and works fully; two channels sharing one document produce ONE style tag (idempotent) and independent overlays. **One non-blocking corner, recorded for F6/I2:** calling `attach()` synchronously inside `destroy()`'s exit-fullscreen await window (i.e. not awaiting destroy, contrary to the module's documented `await screen.destroy()` contract) skips the listener rebind and leaves that session without fullscreenchange events — overlay flashing itself is unaffected. Consumer rule: always `await destroy()` before re-attach (the documented usage, what the harness and tests do).
- **Test-stub audit:** `createDocStub` + `createRafStub` are genuinely tiny and dependency-free (~135 lines, plain objects/Sets/Maps; no jsdom/linkedom — only `node:test`/`node:assert` for the runner). ## T-F5 — Audio channel · 2026-08-28

- **Status:** `awaiting-approval` (plan.md updated).
- **Changed files:** `js/audio.js` (new), `test/audio.test.mjs` (new), `harness.html` (edited — F5 controls + audio wired into the M1 thin-path runner; still marked `<!-- TEMPORARY M1 HARNESS — remove at I2 -->`); doc updates for this entry's bookkeeping (plan.md status, state.md phase note, this log). No other source files touched.
- **What was built:** `createAudioChannel(options)` → `{ unlock, now, scheduleTone, stopAll, setMuted, toggleMuted, isMuted, status, destroy }` — the audio channel AND the master clock, drop-in alongside F3 torch / F4 screen. One oscillator voice — 700 Hz sine (R2 left the frequency open in the 600–800 Hz side-tone band; 700 Hz is the per-task default), peak gain 0.5 — with ~5 ms click-free envelopes. Exports: `SYNC_TOLERANCE_MS = 45` (BT.1359, per R2), `TONE_HZ = 700`, `TONE_TYPE = 'sine'`, `TONE_GAIN = 0.5`, `ENVELOPE_MS = 5`, `LOOKAHEAD_MS = 100`, `TICK_MS = 25` (R2-committed loop constants — F5 is the single source of truth; F6 imports them). All Web Audio globals injectable (`{ AudioContext }` fake ctor or `{ context }` stub); defaults bind via `typeof` guards, so the module imports cleanly under bare Node (no Web Audio there — also the unsupported-path test).
- **Output contract for consumers** (documented in the module header):
  - **F6 (conductor):** master clock = `now()` (`AudioContext.currentTime`, seconds — the R2-committed substrate); anchor once per broadcast inside the gesture (`t0 = audio.now()` after `await unlock()`), event absolute = `t0 + tMs/1000`. `scheduleTone(startSec, endSec)` is SYNCHRONOUS (voice handle `{start, end, cancel}` or `null`), takes ABSOLUTE times, uses no timers and no promises on the hot path — all timing authority stays with the sample-accurate audio thread (Wilson's two clocks, R2 §3.3). Scheduling any future time is legal (the module does not cap lookahead — F6's loop owns the window); a start already past clamps to `currentTime` (the OFF edge — the boundary that defines the following gap — is the one kept exact); a fully-past mark returns `null` (missed, not an error — throttle-tail lateness is a runtime condition). STOP button / pause-on-hidden → `stopAll()` (click-free kill of every pending voice, INCLUDING the ≤ LOOKAHEAD_MS tail Web Audio otherwise still owns after F6 stops scheduling). Replay just schedules anew; no reset needed.
  - **Envelope/sync (plan F5 AC):** attack ramp STARTS at the scheduled ON edge, release ramp ENDS at the scheduled OFF edge → both edges land exactly on their scheduled times (0 ms displacement; perceived onset ≤ ENVELOPE_MS), nothing spills into the following gap (mark/gap ratios preserved, not just edges). Construction REJECTS `envelopeMs ≥ SYNC_TOLERANCE_MS` — the constant is wired into behavior, not just exported. Degenerate marks shorter than 2×envelope (impossible at ≤12 WPM — a dot is ≥100 ms — but scheduleTone is a public primitive) get a midpoint-peak triangular envelope with edges still exact.
  - **Unlock state machine (AC: no sound before gesture):** `none → (unlock() inside a gesture) → running`. The AudioContext is created LAZILY inside `unlock()` — before any gesture there is no context at all (zero nodes, zero audio) and `scheduleTone()` throws synchronously (wiring bug, F4's pre-attach discipline): a triple guarantee of no-context + suspended-until-resumed + sync throw. `unlock()` is idempotent (replay/resume gestures are a cheap state check; no second context, no second resume); it resumes a context iOS re-suspended after backgrounding; `scheduleTone()` gives a suspended-but-unlocked context a fire-and-forget `resume()` nudge (never awaited). `resume()` refusal (autoplay denied) rejects `unlock()`, the channel stays locked and soundless — callers run light-only (multimodal per PRODUCT.md). No Web Audio at all → `unlock()` rejects descriptively.
  - **Mute:** `setMuted()`/`toggleMuted()`/`isMuted()` — fades the MASTER bus (~ENVELOPE_MS ramp, so mid-tone toggles cannot click) with marks continuing to schedule through the silent bus: muting never disturbs the timeline and unmuting mid-broadcast lands inside the message. **Memory-only** (scope: no persistence — enforced headlessly by throwing `localStorage`/`sessionStorage` getters installed for the whole test file, F4's matchMedia-stance pattern). Mute is not stop.
  - **I2 (UI):** `status()` = `{supported, created, unlocked, state, muted, activeVoices, toneHz, toneType, envelopeMs, peakGain, toleranceMs}` for state rendering; the mute control binds to `toggleMuted()`.
  - **Cleanup/release:** voices self-release on `ended` (disconnect + untrack — no node leak across long broadcasts); `voice.cancel()` = one-voice click-free kill (freeze envelope at current value → fade → stop); `stopAll()` = every pending voice, bus and mute state untouched, channel immediately reusable; `destroy()` = drop voices + CLOSE the context (also closes an injected one — the channel owns whatever it plays through), idempotent, safe before unlock; a later `unlock()` builds a FRESH context and the in-memory mute preference survives.
- **Decisions made in F5** (within the plan's outcome, flagged for consumers):
  1. **700 Hz sine** — R2/record specifies no frequency; 700 Hz is the per-task default inside the research's 600–800 Hz band (common practice-oscillator pitch, clear at low volume). Named constant `TONE_HZ`, overridable per channel.
  2. **Per-mark short-lived oscillator+gain on one master bus** (Wilson's per-note pattern), not one always-running gated oscillator: keeps stop/cancel per-voice, leaves no always-running node, and per-voice `ended` cleanup comes free. "Single oscillator voice" = one shared timbre/frequency on one bus.
  3. **Release ENDS at the OFF edge** (attack starts AT the ON edge) rather than releasing after the mark: keeps the tone out of the following gap — ratio preservation, not just edge alignment. This is the load-bearing envelope decision; the tests deep-equal the full event sequence.
  4. **Late-start clamp keeps the OFF edge exact** (a late-scheduled onset clamps to `currentTime`; the off boundary defines the gap). Fully-past marks return `null` instead of throwing.
  5. **`LOOKAHEAD_MS`/`TICK_MS` exported from F5** though F6 owns the loop — single source of truth for the R2-committed pair; noted so F6's owner imports rather than re-declares.
- **Validation evidence:** `node --test test/audio.test.mjs` → `ℹ tests 32 · pass 32 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, exit 0. Full suite `node --test 'test/*.test.mjs'` → `ℹ tests 174 · pass 174 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, **exit 0** (F1 74/74 + F2 38/38 + F4 30/30 unchanged + F5 32/32). Coverage (32 tests, 5 suites, against a dependency-free AudioContext stub with RECORDING AudioParams — assertions target event sequences, the real automation contract): committed constants incl. envelope-vs-tolerance wiring (5 < 45, 2×5 < 45); construction validation (TypeError/RangeError for bad frequency/type/envelope/peak; `envelopeMs ≥ 45` rejected by name /sync tolerance/; valid customizations reported); no-storage stance (whole file under throwing localStorage/sessionStorage getters; per-channel memory-only mute); unlock state machine (lazy creation — ctor log EMPTY before unlock; pre-unlock scheduleTone throws; `now() === 0`; full status deep-equal; unsupported rejection; exactly-one context + one resume + master bus wired to destination at gain 1; idempotent unlock; injected-suspended resume without creation; injected-running trusted without unlock; resume-refusal stays locked then recovers on next unlock); scheduling math (start/stop EXACTLY at scheduled edges; envelope deep-equal `[set 0 @tOn, ramp peak @tOn+5ms, set peak @tOff−5ms, ramp 0 @tOff]` with first/last event times === the edges; routing osc→tone→master→destination with master wired once not per tone; late-start clamp with OFF edge exact; fully-past → null with zero nodes; end≤start RangeError + non-finite TypeError; sub-2×envelope triangle; 3-mark lookahead batch synchronous with non-Promise handles; beyond-lookahead accepted; custom voice options through to the envelope; suspended-but-unlocked self-heal nudge proven non-blocking with a never-resolving resume; closed-context guard; now() tracks currentTime); mute (click-free master fade `[cancel, anchor 1, ramp 0 @+5ms]`; unmute restores; toggleMuted return; mute-before-unlock → bus born at 0 with the timeline continuing — nodes exist, only the bus is silent); cleanup (natural `ended` self-release ×3 with zero leaks and master surviving; voice.cancel freeze-fade-stop sequence; stopAll cancels all with bus/mute untouched and channel reusable; stopAll pre-unlock no-op; destroy closes the context, resets status, rejects scheduling, re-unlock builds a FRESH context honoring the surviving mute preference; destroy idempotent + pre-unlock safe + closes injected contexts). Hygiene greps over `js/audio.js`: zero `setTimeout/setInterval/localStorage/sessionStorage/document/window/navigator/requestAnimationFrame/import` references, zero `globalThis` writes (only typeof-guarded reads for the ctor) — the module is DOM-free, timer-free, storage-free by construction.
- **Headless limits (honest):** real click audibility, actual on-device audio↔light offset (the "sync within SYNC_TOLERANCE_MS on desktop" AC), autoplay-policy behaviors, and iOS backgrounding behavior are NOT verifiable in Node — they are Q3's scripted check (perceptual: "light and beep together at BRISK?"), for which `harness.html` is the instrument: an "unlock + beep" button (the click IS the gesture), a live mute toggle, and the M1 thin-path runner now drives BOTH channels from one anchor — audio scheduled ahead by absolute `audio.now()` time (the F5/F6 contract), screen on temporary setTimeout (F6 replaces it), `stopAll()` on stop. Harness inline module script syntax-checked (`node --check` on the extracted script). Harness must be served over http (ES modules vs file://).

Three scope notes: (a) `test/audio.test.mjs` added beyond the plan's `Files: js/audio.js` because the task brief explicitly required headless tests of scheduling math, envelope shape, unlock state machine, mute, cleanup; (b) `harness.html` extended (permitted by the task brief) — still the same single temporary file, marked for removal at I2; (c) decisions 1–5 above are F5-owned interface decisions within the plan's outcome — flagged so F6's owner consumes them consciously (esp. 3 and 4, which define what "within SYNC_TOLERANCE_MS" means edge-by-edge, and 5, which asks F6 to import the loop constants).
- **Delegation record:** none — task executed directly by the production worker. Research consumed: R2 (`docs/ultron/research/r2-latency-wpm-sync.md`, resolved 2026-08-28) for `SYNC_TOLERANCE_MS=45` (BT.1359 §3.4/§5.2), the master-clock/lookahead commitment (`AudioContext.currentTime`, 25 ms tick / 100 ms schedule-ahead, Wilson §3.3/§5.3), ~5 ms envelopes, and the visibilitychange-pause↔stopAll pairing; F2's T-entry consumer contract for the event-edge shape (on/off pairs with identity on both edges — audio consumes only the times); F4's T-entry for the channel-shape and sync-discipline conventions (gesture-window attach, sync-throw wiring bugs, status() introspection).

### T-F5 verification (independent production verifier) · 2026-08-28

- **Status:** PASS → F5 set to `completed` in plan.md; `auto-approved (ultron-supreme)` recorded in state.md. Verifier did not author F5; every check re-run from scratch (Node 24.8.0), including 9 adversarial probes on the verifier's OWN AudioContext stub (fresh param/gain/osc/context recording stub, distinct from the suite's).
- **Test run (re-executed by verifier):** `node --test 'test/*.test.mjs'` → `ℹ tests 174 · pass 174 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, **exit 0** (node's own status, not a pipeline's). Decomposition re-run per suite: morse 74/74, schedule 38/38, screen 30/30, audio 32/32 — F1/F2/F4 unchanged, counts match the worker's claim (74+38+30+32=174). `node --check` clean on `js/audio.js` and `test/audio.test.mjs`.
- **Envelope edge timing (gain-automation math re-derived):** the tone gain receives `setValueAtTime(0, t0) → linearRampToValueAtTime(peak, t0+env) → setValueAtTime(peak, t1−env) → linearRampToValueAtTime(0, t1)`; `osc.start(t0)`/`osc.stop(t1)` at the same instants. Under the Web Audio automation model (a linear ramp interpolates from the PREVIOUS event's value/time), this is the correct click-free shape: the first automation event is AT the scheduled ON edge (onset begins there — 0 ms displacement, full amplitude within 5 ms) and the LAST event ends AT the scheduled OFF edge (gain exactly 0 at t1 — nothing spills into the following gap, so mark/gap ratios survive, not just edges). Both edge offsets are 0 ms by construction, ≤ ENVELOPE_MS=5 ms perceptually, ≪ `SYNC_TOLERANCE_MS=45`; construction *rejects* `envelopeMs ≥ 45` (RangeError naming the sync tolerance) — the constant is wired into behavior, not decorative. Verifier probe P2 deep-equaled the full event sequence on the verifier's own times (8.234 → 8.551 s: set 0 @8.234, ramp 0.5 @8.239, set 0.5 @8.546, ramp 0 @8.551) — values the worker's suite never uses. Degenerate sub-2×envelope marks take a midpoint triangle with edges still exact (suite + probe P3c).
- **Late-start clamping — OFF edge stays exact:** `endSec ≤ currentTime` → `null` (missed, zero nodes created — probe P3a); otherwise `t0 = max(startSec, currentTime)` with `t1` NEVER clamped — the OFF edge (the boundary defining the following gap) lands exactly on schedule, and the envelope is re-derived from the clamped t0 (full vs triangle by remaining duration — probes P3b/P3c, incl. the exact 2×envelope boundary where IEEE-754 (`5.01−5.0 = 0.00999…`) picks the triangle branch; **both branches are valid** — edges exact, 5 ms ramps, no spill — so the FP sensitivity is a non-blocking curiosity, zero AC impact).
- **No-audio-before-unlock (exhaustive path audit):** the ONLY `new Ctor()` in the module is inside `unlock()` (grep-verified); `resolveCtor()` (also called from `status()`) is a typeof read that never instantiates. Every other entry point is creation-free by construction: `now()` reads or returns 0, `setMuted` early-returns flag-only (honored later at `wireMaster()` — bus born at 0 if muted pre-unlock, suite-verified), `stopAll` no-ops, `destroy` no-ops. `scheduleTone` throws synchronously when `!ctx || !unlocked` (and on a closed context). Verifier probe P1 drove now/setMuted×2/toggleMuted/stopAll/status/isMuted/destroy on a fresh channel → constructor log stayed EMPTY. The only alternative context source is explicit caller injection — a suspended injected one still blocks scheduling until `unlock()` resumes it (suite-verified); a running one is opt-in trust, documented and tested. On the default path there is NO route to a context, a node, or a sound before the gesture.
- **Mute fade — click-free, timeline preserved:** `setMuted` reads `gain.value` BEFORE `cancelScheduledValues` (the correct click-free anchor pattern — `.value` reflects the computed mid-automation value), anchors `setValueAtTime(v, t)`, ramps to 0/1 over ~5 ms; only the MASTER bus fades — per-tone envelopes, scheduled nodes, and the clock are untouched (suite proves marks keep scheduling while muted and unmuting lands mid-message). Verifier probe P5 confirmed the interrupted-fade anchor (mute then immediate unmute at 3.002 → `set <current> @3.002, ramp 1 @3.002+0.005`). Memory-only stance doubly verified: per-channel flag in code (fresh channel starts unmuted, suite-verified) + throwing `localStorage`/`sessionStorage` getters installed across the ENTIRE test file + verifier greps find storage/timer/DOM references only in comments (`setTimeout`×1, `localStorage`×2, `sessionStorage`×1, `cookies`×1 — all comment lines; zero `window.`/`document.`/`navigator`/`requestAnimationFrame`; `globalThis` appears only as typeof-guarded READS).
- **stopAll/destroy cleanup — no orphans, no leaked context:** `stopAll` iterates a snapshot, untracks each voice FIRST, then freeze (read value pre-cancel) → 5 ms fade → `osc.stop(t+envSec)` (last-stop-wins per spec, try/catch'd for stop-after-stop engines); `onended` disconnects osc+gain; master bus and mute state untouched; channel immediately reusable. Verifier probe P6: two future-start (lookahead-tail) voices killed — both oscillators stopped at now+5 ms, every tone envelope carries a cancel, master-bus event list untouched, next scheduleTone works. `destroy` nulls `ctx` BEFORE awaiting `close()` (nothing can schedule into a closing context), closes self-created AND injected contexts exactly once (probe P7: closeCalls 1 after double destroy), is idempotent and pre-unlock safe, and a later `unlock()` builds a FRESH context honoring the surviving mute preference; closing a context discards its scheduled events per spec — no orphan events. One micro-nuance (non-blocking, no AC impact): for a cancelled voice whose `osc.start` is still in the future, whether `onended` fires is engine-dependent — the voice is untracked before stop, so at worst two silent nodes linger until context close.
- **Lookahead constants for F6:** `LOOKAHEAD_MS = 100`, `TICK_MS = 25` exported and equal to R2 §5.3's committed pair (25 ms tick / 100 ms schedule-ahead, Wilson); asserted in the suite's constants test and re-asserted by verifier probe P8 alongside `SYNC_TOLERANCE_MS = 45`, `ENVELOPE_MS = 5`.
- **Unlock state machine (verifier probes P4/P9 + suite):** idempotent unlock creates exactly one context and one resume across repeated gestures; suspended-but-unlocked context gets a fire-and-forget resume nudge from `scheduleTone` (probe: never-resolving resume promise does NOT block the synchronous return); resume refusal (autoplay denied) rejects `unlock()`, leaves the channel locked and `scheduleTone` throwing, and the next gesture recovers.
- **Test-stub audit:** the suite's AudioContext stub is ~110 lines of plain objects (param/gain/osc/context) importing only `node:test`/`node:assert/strict` — genuinely dependency-free. AudioParams RECORD event sequences and the envelope test **deep-equals the full ordered sequence** `[[setValueAtTime,0,1.0],[linearRampToValueAtTime,0.5,1.005],[setValueAtTime,0.5,1.235],[linearRampToValueAtTime,0,1.24]]` with first/last event times === the scheduled edges, plus routing-graph assertions (osc→tone→master→destination, master wired once), late-clamp edge assertions (`_started=0.5`/`_stopped=1.0`), the degenerate triangle, the custom-envelope variant, mute-fade sequences, and a ctor log proving lazy creation — real timing-sequence verification, not call-count.
- **Scope:** full tree listing contains only pre-existing artifacts (`PRODUCT.md`, `docs/ultron/**`, F1's two, F2's two, F4's three) plus F5's declared `js/audio.js` (new) and `test/audio.test.mjs` (new) and the `harness.html` edit — matches the worker's changed-files list exactly (git-less repo). `harness.html` line 2 is still `<!-- TEMPORARY M1 HARNESS — remove at I2 -->`; the only consumer of `js/audio.js` is that leaf harness; the harness's M1 runner drives both channels from ONE anchor on `audio.now()` (the F5/F6 contract) with `stopAll()` on stop.
- **AC note (honest, same shape as T-F4):** the AC's "sync within SYNC_TOLERANCE_MS on desktop" and audible no-click checks are perceptual device actions that cannot execute headlessly; the plan routes device results through Q3, and `harness.html` is the instrument (unlock+beep button whose click IS the gesture, live mute toggle, both-channel M1 runner). All headlessly verifiable portions pass, and the sync criterion holds BY CONSTRUCTION: both mark edges land exactly on scheduled absolute `AudioContext.currentTime` instants (0 ms displacement, ≤5 ms perceptual onset, envelope bounded < 45 ms at construction). ## T-F3 — Torch channel · 2026-08-28

- **Status:** `awaiting-approval` (plan.md updated).
- **Changed files:** `js/torch.js` (new), `test/torch.test.mjs` (new), `harness.html` (edited — F3 arm/on/off/release buttons + live torch status + loss logging; still marked `<!-- TEMPORARY M1 HARNESS — remove at I2 -->`); doc updates for this entry's bookkeeping (plan.md status, state.md phase note, this log). No other source files touched.
- **What was built:** `createTorchChannel(options)` → `{ acquire, on, off, release, destroy, isLit, status, addEventListener, removeEventListener }` — the torch light channel, drop-in alongside F4 screen / F5 audio. Exports: `PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS = 1500` (R1's ~1.5 s), `TORCH_STATES` (`idle/probing/torch-ready/screen-fallback-recommended/error`), `TORCH_OUTCOMES`, `TORCH_FALLBACK_REASONS`, `TORCH_ERROR_CODES`, `TORCH_FORMS` (`advanced`/`toplevel`), `TORCH_EVENTS` (`lost`/`released`) — all frozen. All media/DOM/timer globals injectable (`{ mediaDevices, doc, setTimeout, clearTimeout, probeTimeoutMs }`); defaults bind via `typeof` guards, so the module imports cleanly under bare Node (which is itself the insecure-context test).
- **R1 detection order, implemented verbatim** (docs/ultron/research/r1-torch-support-matrix.md §5; every step headlessly asserted):
  0. No `navigator.mediaDevices` → `{outcome:'screen-fallback', reason:'insecure-context'}`, no prompt attempted (read live via typeof-guarded lazy resolution).
  1. `getUserMedia({video:{facingMode:{ideal:'environment'}}, audio:false})` — the gUM call's constraints are deep-equaled in the test; `ideal`, never `exact` (one-camera devices must not OverconstrainedError). Rejections map by name: `NotAllowedError`→`permission-denied` (F8's denied state), `NotFoundError`→`no-camera`, `NotReadableError`/`TrackStartError`→`camera-unavailable`, else `gum-failed` — ALL are screen-fallback outcomes; `acquire()` never rejects for device/user conditions.
  2. Guarded capability check: the `getCapabilities` FUNCTION itself is guarded (Firefox history); the torch entry is truthy across shapes (boolean `true`, or a true-bearing `sequence<boolean>` per the Image Capture spec — `[false]` correctly means "cannot"). Absent → track stopped → `no-torch-capability`, and the test proves the probe is never attempted (cheap signal, no dead-end blinking).
  3. Trial probe, BOTH directions: `applyConstraints({advanced:[{torch:true}]})` with a single retry as top-level `{torch:true}` (WebKit's own test form; Chromium takes both), then the SAME advanced→top-level pair for `torch:false`; each attempt wrapped in `PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS = 1500`; rejection → retry other form; TIMEOUT → terminal (no retry — R1: any rejection/timeout → screen). The **torch:false-only-ON device class (Chromium 1246938) is caught by the OFF probe**: its test asserts the exact 3-call sequence (adv-ON ok, adv-OFF rej, top-OFF rej) → `probe-off-failed` with the track stopped and sink detached — never a stuck-ON LED handed to a broadcast.
  4. `torch-ready`; the winning constraint form is remembered PER DIRECTION (a mixed-form device — advanced ON, top-level-only OFF — uses each direction's proven form on the hot path; covered by a dedicated test).
- **Output contract for consumers** (documented in the module header):
  - **F8 (state machine):** drive `acquire()` from the arm-torch gesture. `status().state` reads `probing` SYNCHRONOUSLY at call time (set before the first await), then one of `torch-ready` / `screen-fallback-recommended` / `error`. `acquire()` resolves — never rejects — `{outcome, form|reason, detail}`; screen-fallback is a HEALTHY detection outcome (F8's unsupported(screen) state), `error` is armed-then-broken or internal. Re-acquire after screen-fallback re-prompts (user may have granted since); while ready it is cached (no second prompt); concurrent calls share the in-flight probe (strictEqual-tested).
  - **F6 (conductor):** `on()`/`off()` are fire-and-forget `applyConstraints` on the winning form — HONEST completion signaling per R2: the returned promise resolves when the constraint application is ACCEPTED (submission time, a strict LOWER BOUND on the physical LED — resolved ≠ lit, and the difference is unmeasurable without frames, which the privacy scope bans; R2 rejected measure-then-calibrate for exactly this reason). So fire torch commands `TORCH_LEAD_MS = 120` ms early (F6's own named constant) and never await on the hot path. Rejection = the apply was refused → the channel fires **`lost`** and dies; the promise also rejects (with `.cause`), but ignoring it cannot trip `unhandledrejection` (marked handled internally; asserted with a process-level listener in the test). `desired`/`applied` in `status()` + `isLit()` report the commanded/accepted state — never physical LED truth. Mid-broadcast torch loss (track `ended` OR applyConstraints rejection — both per the task brief) surfaces as the `lost` event `{code:'track-ended'|'apply-rejected', detail}` for F6's seamless screen takeover; `status().reason` carries the same code in state `error`.
  - **I2 (UI):** `release()` is the STOP-path kill switch — SYNCHRONRONOUS `track.stop()` (per spec the torch lasts only as long as the track is active, so stopping always extinguishes the LED), sink detached, state → idle, `released` event; idempotent. `destroy()` = release + drop listeners (re-acquire after destroy works, F4 convention). The stream is HELD for the whole armed session (keep-alive: torch dies with the track — `stopCount === 0` is asserted across a simulated broadcast).
  - **Events:** DOM-style `addEventListener`/`removeEventListener` (the channel's own `on()`/`off()` are the LIGHT toggle — no name collision); the subscribe function returns an unsubscribe; listener errors are swallowed (one broken consumer must not break takeover signaling).
- **Privacy (ABSOLUTE constraint):** zero frame-access surface — no pixel/bitmap/frame-callback/canvas API anywhere; the stream exists solely to hold torch access. Enforced headlessly by a source-scan test over comment-stripped `js/torch.js` (13 banned identifiers + case-insensitive `canvas` → zero matches; the sanctioned `srcObject` keep-alive assignment is required to be PRESENT and visible). The one element the stream touches is a 1px invisible `muted playsinline autoplay` `<video>` sink — invisible-but-RENDERED (not `display:none`; WebKit can park fully hidden players), fixed/out-of-flow/pointer-transparent, `aria-hidden`, srcObject-cleared and removed on teardown — whose job is the iOS keep-alive R1 §8 prescribes; it never exposes a frame to script. Best-effort by design: no `doc` → no sink, the session still arms (tested).
- **Decisions made in F3** (within the plan's outcome, flagged for consumers):
  1. **`acquire()`/`release()` naming** (not attach/destroy) per the task brief's acquire/release lifecycle; `destroy()` retained for channel-convention teardown. Nothing auto-acquires — no import-time or construction-time media access exists at all (tested: 4 constructions, zero gUM calls).
  2. **Per-direction winning forms** (see step 4 above) — one form per direction rather than one per channel, so a hypothetical mixed-form device cannot get a false-loss OFF.
  3. **`release()` synchronous** (not a promise): STOP must extinguish the LED in the same task as the tap — `track.stop()` is the one hardware-guaranteed off, and awaiting anything first would delay it. `release()` during probing throws (wiring bug — await `acquire()`, which always settles: gUM resolves with the prompt, the probe is timeout-bounded).
  4. **Loss state = `error` with `reason:'track-ended'|'apply-rejected'`** — distinct from `screen-fallback-recommended` (a healthy detection outcome), because F8 renders them differently; the `lost` EVENT is F6's takeover signal and carries the code.
  5. **Timeout is terminal within a direction** (no second-form retry after a hang) — R1 §5's "any rejection/timeout → screen mode"; only rejections get the one-form retry.
  6. **The keep-alive sink waits (bounded by the probe timeout) for `loadedmetadata` before the capability read** — R1 §3.4's "caps can be empty if queried too early"; non-fatal on timeout (tested both ways).
  7. **R1's optional P2 `enumerateDevices` multi-lens retry NOT implemented** — explicitly P2 in the research, out of F3's outcome; noted here so F8/Q3 know the multi-camera gotcha is accepted as screen-fallback for now.
- **Validation evidence:** `node --test test/torch.test.mjs` → `ℹ tests 43 · pass 43 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, exit 0. Full suite `node --test 'test/*.test.mjs'` → `ℹ tests 217 · pass 217 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, **exit 0** (F1 74/74 + F2 38/38 + F4 30/30 + F5 32/32 all unchanged + F3 43/43; 74+38+30+32+43 = 217). Coverage (43 tests, 16 suites, against dependency-free getUserMedia/MediaStreamTrack/document stubs and a manually-advanced fake clock): constants + frozen maps; hygiene (bare-Node construction; zero gUM at construction; the privacy source scan); insecure context; full-support path (exact gUM constraints deep-equal, exact 2-call probe sequence, stream/sink kept, zero pending timers, sync `probing` observation, cached re-acquire, shared in-flight promise); iOS top-level-form fallback (exact 4-call sequence + hot path); mixed per-direction forms; torch:false-only-ON catch; capabilities-lying catch (2-call, OFF never attempted); capability-gate variants (fn absent / empty object / `[false]` vs `[true]`); gUM rejection mapping ×4; ON-hang and OFF-hang timeout paths (state `probing` before advance, exactly 1 apply call after timeout — no retry, direction named in detail, track stopped, zero timer leak); sink (element/attributes/`srcObject === stream`/play/metadata-readiness, no-doc degrade, never-ready bounded wait); release (stop exactly once, sink removed + srcObject null, idle, event, idempotent, pre-acquire no-op, during-probing throw); loss (track `ended`, apply rejection with promise rejection + `lost` + `error` state, fire-and-forget with a process-level `unhandledRejection` spy proving none); re-acquisition (after deny→grant, after release, after loss); wiring-bug sync throws + construction TypeErrors/RangeError; events API (unsubscribe fn, removeEventListener, throwing-listener isolation); exact full `status()` deep-equals at idle and torch-ready. Hygiene greps over `js/torch.js`: zero `globalThis`/`window`/`document` writes, zero frame-API tokens anywhere in the file (comments included); `node --check` clean on module, test, and the harness's extracted inline module.
- **Adversarial probe (self-run, verifier-style):** re-entrancy — a `lost` listener that synchronously calls `acquire()` (fresh camera session) re-arms cleanly: old track stopped at loss, new session reaches `torch-ready`, its hot path applies correctly; a first flawed probe run (rejecting apply left on the reused track) actually EXERCISED the intended failure path — re-probe → both-form rejection → `screen-fallback`, then `off()` threw the documented wiring-bug error — module behavior correct under abuse.
- **Headless limits (honest):** real-LED behavior (probe blink, torch ON/OFF latency, the stuck-LED silent-ignore residual), permission-prompt UX, iOS keep-alive efficacy, and screen-lock loss timing are NOT verifiable in Node — they are Q3's scripted device check (the plan's F3 AC routes real-device confirmation there), for which `harness.html` now carries the F3 instrument: arm/on/off/release buttons (the click IS the gesture), live torch status, and `lost`/`released` logging. The resolves-but-no-physical-flash false-positive class (R1 §3.4) is undetectable from script by principle — the plan's manual "switch to screen" control (U1/I2) is the designed mitigation.

Three scope notes: (a) `test/torch.test.mjs` added beyond the plan's `Files: js/torch.js` because the task brief explicitly required the stubbed support-matrix suite; (b) `harness.html` extended (task-briefed) — same single temporary file, marked for removal at I2; (c) decisions 1–7 above are F3-owned interface decisions within the plan's outcome — flagged so F6/F8 consume them consciously (esp. 3's synchronous release, 4's lost-event contract, and the honest resolve-means-accepted signaling that justifies F6's early-fire lead).

Decomposition matches the worker's claim: F1 74 + F2 38 + F4 30 + F5 32 + F3 43 = 217, prior suites unchanged. `node --check` clean on `js/torch.js` and `test/torch.test.mjs`.
- **R1 detection order — audited line-by-line against §5 (the law):**
  - **Step 0 (secure context):** `resolveMediaDevices()` is read at acquire() call time; no `mediaDevices`/no `getUserMedia` fn → `screen-fallback/insecure-context` BEFORE any gUM is reachable (structurally impossible to prompt — the check precedes the only gUM call site). Bare-Node import is itself the live test (Node 24's `navigator` exists but has no `mediaDevices`, i.e. the realistic insecure shape).
  - **Step 1 (gesture-only acquisition, environment-ideal):** `GUM_CONSTRAINTS` is a frozen `{video:{facingMode:{ideal:'environment'}}, audio:false}` — `ideal`, never `exact` (R1 §3.5's OverconstrainedError guard); the suite deep-equals the exact object passed to gUM. Nothing self-acquires: the module top level and the constructor contain zero gUM calls (suite: 4 constructions → 0 calls); the only acquisition entry is `acquire()`, which the app binds to its arm-torch gesture. gUM rejections map by name (NotAllowed→permission-denied for F8's denied state, NotFound→no-camera, NotReadable/TrackStart→camera-unavailable, else gum-failed) — all screen-fallback outcomes; `acquire()` never rejects.
  - **Step 2 (guarded getCapabilities, both shapes):** `readCapabilities` guards the FUNCTION itself (`typeof fn !== 'function'` → undefined, plus try/catch — the old-Firefox shape) and `hasTorchCapability` handles boolean `true`, the Image-Capture spec's `sequence<boolean>` (`[false]`/`[false,false]` → cannot; `[true]`/`[false,true]` → can — verifier probes P3 exercised the MIXED sequences the suite's single-element cases leave open, via `some(Boolean)`), and absent/empty caps. Capability failure stops the track and NEVER attempts the probe (cheap signal, no dead-end LED blinking). The caps read happens after `attachSink()` + `waitForSinkReady()` — R1 §3.4's "caps can read empty before the track is live", bounded by the probe timeout, non-fatal on timeout (both suite-tested).
  - **Step 3 (BOTH-directions probe):** `probeDirection(true)` then `probeDirection(false)`; each tries `advanced:[{torch}]` first and retries ONCE with top-level `{torch}` on REJECTION; each attempt is wrapped in `PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS = 1500` (R1's "~1.5 s"); TIMEOUT is terminal within the direction (no second-form retry — R1's "any rejection/timeout → screen mode"); any failure → `teardownSession()` (track stopped, sink detached) → screen-fallback. **The torch:false-only-ON class (Chromium 1246938) is caught by the OFF probe:** suite test + verifier probe P1 (verifier's own stub) both confirm a device that accepts ON and rejects OFF lands in `screen-fallback/probe-off-failed` after the exact 3-call sequence `[adv-ON, adv-OFF, top-OFF]` with the track stopped exactly once and the sink removed — a stuck-ON LED is never handed to a broadcast. The sibling class (OFF *resolves* but the LED physically stays lit) is undetectable from script by principle — honestly documented in the module header and routed to the plan's manual screen-switch control + Q3.
  - **Step 4 (armed):** per-direction winning forms remembered (`forms:{on,off}`) — the suite's mixed-form test proves a hypothetical advanced-ON/top-level-only-OFF device rides each direction's proven form on the hot path (a refinement WITHIN R1 §5.4's intent: the probe established what the device honors).
- **Keep-alive + kill switch:** the stream is HELD for the whole armed session — suite asserts `stopCount === 0` across a simulated broadcast and verifier probe P6b confirms (4 hot-path commands, stop still 0, `acquired:true`); `release()` is a SYNCHRONOUS `track.stop()` (the one hardware-guaranteed extinguisher — spec: torch lasts only while the track is active) + sink detach + state idle + `released` event, idempotent, exactly-once stop (probe P6c); re-acquire after release/loss/deny opens a FRESH session (probe P6: two full sessions, second gUM call, first track stopped once, second still live).
- **PRIVACY (hard gate) — verifier's own grep:** `grep -nE "drawImage|ImageCapture|canvas|getVideoFrame|VideoFrame|texImage|getImageData|createImageBitmap|toBlob|toDataURL|captureStream|requestVideoFrameCallback|MediaStreamTrackProcessor|ImageDecoder|grabFrame|takePhoto|getContext"` over `js/torch.js` → **zero matches INCLUDING comments** (17 tokens, stricter than the required six); the comment-stripped scan also finds zero. The ONLY stream sink is the keep-alive element: the two `srcObject` occurrences are the sanctioned assignment in `attachSink()` and the null-out in `detachSink()`; every other `stream_.` use is track picking/stopping (metadata only). The sink is a 1px fixed opacity-0 pointer-transparent `muted playsinline autoplay aria-hidden` `<video>` — invisible-but-rendered per R1 §8's iOS prescription, removed and nulled on teardown.
- **Source-scan test is real:** the strip is `/*…*/`-then-`//` comment removal, and string literals are NOT stripped — verifier probe A proves a banned token in an EXECUTING string (`ctx["drawImage"]`) SURVIVES the strip and is caught by `includes`, so dynamic/string-based frame calls cannot slip through as "comments". One theoretical deliberate-evasion vector exists (a `//` inside a string literal truncates the rest of that line, probe D) — but `js/torch.js` contains ZERO string literals holding `//` or `/*` (probe E/F), and the verifier's RAW grep (comments included) is independently zero, so the vector is unused; noted for Q2's re-audit greps. Non-blocking observation: the suite's 13-token ban list omits bare `VideoFrame`, `texImage2D`, `MediaStreamTrackProcessor`, `ImageDecoder`, `takePhoto`, `grabFrame` — of these, every texImage/canvas path is already blocked by the banned `getContext` + case-insensitive `canvas`, and the verifier's own grep proves the file contains none of them; still, Q2 should grep the fuller list.
- **Interface audit for F6:** (a) `on()`/`off()` completion semantics are documented in the module header AND both JSDocs as constraint-application ACCEPTANCE — submission time, a strict lower bound on the physical LED, never LED confirmation — with the explicit consequence that F6 fires early (`TORCH_LEAD_MS = 120`, F6's constant) and never awaits; (b) the `lost` event fires from BOTH paths: track `ended` (camera stolen/screen lock/OS teardown) and hot-path `applyConstraints` rejection, carrying `{code:'track-ended'|'apply-rejected', detail}` for seamless screen takeover (both suite-tested); (c) no unhandled rejection paths: `command()` attaches `.catch(noop)` to the wrapped promise (awaiting callers still see the rejection — the suite proves this with a process-level `unhandledRejection` spy on a deliberately un-awaited rejecting `on()`), `withTimeout` ALWAYS attaches handlers to the underlying apply promise (verifier probe P4: a probe that rejects LATE, after the timeout already fired the screen-fallback, produces zero unhandled rejections), `waitForSinkReady` never rejects, `attachSink` play() is `.catch`'d, and `acquire()` resolves on every path; (d) re-acquire after `release()`/`destroy()`/loss works (suite + probe P6).
- **Stub faithfulness vs R1's matrix:** the suite's dependency-free stubs (recording gUM facade, behavior-configurable track, doc stub with/without loadedmetadata, manually-advanced clock) cover the matrix class-by-class: full Android-Chromium support (exact 2-call advanced sequence), iOS-17.4+ top-level form (exact 4-call sequence, hot path on top-level), torch:false-only-ON (1246938), capabilities-lying-rejecting (probe-on-failed, OFF never attempted), getCapabilities-absent (old Firefox), empty caps (desktop webcam), spec sequence shapes, gUM rejections ×4, ON-hang and OFF-hang timeouts, insecure context, sink attached/absent/never-ready, release/loss/re-acquisition lifecycle. R1's optional P2 `enumerateDevices` multi-lens retry is not stubbed because it is not implemented — correctly, R1 marks it optional P2 and the worker flagged the accepted residual.
- **Scope:** git-less repo, so verified by mtime window (01:19–01:59 of the F3 run) + full tree listing: only `js/torch.js` (new), `test/torch.test.mjs` (new), `harness.html` (edited; still `<!-- TEMPORARY M1 HARNESS — remove at I2 -->`, now carrying the F3 arm/on/off/release instrument + lost/released logging for Q3), and the three bookkeeping docs (plan.md, state.md, this log) — exactly the declared changed-files list. All prior-task files (morse/schedule/screen/audio js+tests, PRODUCT.md, research, town-hall, design-brief) carry pre-F3 mtimes.
