## T-F8 — App state machine · 2026-08-28

- **Status:** `awaiting-approval` (plan.md updated).
- **Changed files:** `js/states.js` (new), `test/states.test.mjs` (new); doc updates for this entry's bookkeeping (plan.md status, state.md phase note, this log). No other source files touched.
- **What was built:** `createStateMachine()` → `{ dispatch, state, can, snapshot, addEventListener, removeEventListener }` — the explicit app state machine as a pure, synchronous transition table (no DOM, no timers, no promises; driven entirely by dispatched events, so every cell is headlessly pinnable). **15 states** (`idle / preflight / arming / awaiting-permission / permission-denied / unsupported / torch-ready / screen-mode / ready / broadcasting / broadcasting-on-screen / paused / paused-on-screen / done / error`), **21 events** (`STATE_EVENTS`: user intent REQUEST_BROADCAST/ACK/CANCEL/GO/PAUSE/RESUME/STOP/REPLAY/ACCEPT_SCREEN/SWITCH_CHANNEL/RETRY_ARM/RESET + environment PERMISSION_PROMPTED/PERMISSION_GRANTED/TORCH_ACQUIRED/TORCH_PERMISSION_DENIED/TORCH_UNSUPPORTED/TORCH_ERROR/TORCH_LOST + conductor BROADCAST_COMPLETE/BROADCAST_FAILED), **2 effects** (`STATE_EFFECTS`: `notice-required`, `release-torch`). Exports: `STATES`, `STATE_LIST` (lifecycle order — I1's static snapshots iterate it), `STATE_EVENTS`, `STATE_EFFECTS`, the frozen `TRANSITIONS` table, **`ARM_MATRIX` + `resolveArmMatrix()`** (the permission×support matrix AS DATA, 4 support values × 4 permission values, every cell total incl. documented defensive ones; cell = `{state, medium, framing, offerScreen, prompts}`), `ARM_SUPPORT`, `ARM_PERMISSION`, `ARM_MEDIUMS`, `ARM_FRAMINGS`, `SUPPORT_BY_FALLBACK_REASON` (F3 fallback reason → support), **`eventFromTorchResult()`** (one F3 `acquire()` result → the exact event+payload to dispatch — I2 never interprets F3 internals). The only import is `TORCH_FALLBACK_REASONS/TORCH_ERROR_CODES/TORCH_OUTCOMES` from `js/torch.js` (plan dep F3; keeps the two vocabularies one source).
- **The matrix, owned here** (R1 committed into F8): supported×granted → `torch-ready` (iOS 17.4+ / Chromium-Android torch path); supported×denied → `permission-denied` with `offerScreen:true` (the ONLY cell that offers a choice — never a dead end); any unsupported → `unsupported` = screen with POSITIVE framing (`screen-positive`); no-camera×(not-asked/pending) and insecure×everything → screen WITHOUT prompting (`prompts:false` — the desktop/no-camera row). Desktop-with-camera and all-Firefox reach `unsupported` through the live caps/probe path (matrix cell no-torch×granted) — never UA sniffing (R1 §8 explicitly out). R1's reference flow IDLE→ARMING→TORCH_READY|SCREEN_MODE→BROADCASTING is the table's spine; `preflight` wraps it in the notice gate, `awaiting-permission` refines the prompt window, the screen side lands in `screen-mode` via `unsupported`→ACCEPT_SCREEN.
- **Output contract for consumers** (documented in the module header):
  - **F6 (conductor):** dispatches `GO` (from `ready` — entering `ready` is the app's GO-commit; `ready`'s GO starts the broadcast), `PAUSE` on visibilitychange→hidden (R2) and `RESUME` on re-anchor, `BROADCAST_COMPLETE` when the schedule runs out, `BROADCAST_FAILED` on conductor failure, and `TORCH_LOST` (translating F3's `lost` event payload `{code}`) for the seamless screen takeover — `broadcasting→broadcasting-on-screen` (and `paused→paused-on-screen`); the machine records `takeover {cause:'torch-lost'|'manual-switch', code}` for I2's explanatory note. Manual SWITCH_CHANNEL rides the same transitions, cause-tagged (R1 §5.6's mitigation for the resolves-but-no-flash class).
  - **I2 (UI):** subscribe to `change` `{from,to,via,payload,effects,snapshot}` (frozen) and render per `snapshot.state` — every state renders distinctly, with `noticePending` (hazard plate in preflight), `fallbackReason/fallbackDetail` (denied/unsupported copy; ALSO the last arm resolution for screen-mode's "why screen" line), `takeover`, `doneReason ('complete'|'stopped')`, `error {kind:'arm'|'broadcast', reason, detail}`, `channel ('torch'|'screen'|null)` as the per-state render carriers. `invalid` events are soft rejections (races); `release-torch`/`notice-required` are host actions (call `torch.release()` / show the hazard plate). The whole wiring is ten lines (header sketch): translate one `await torch.acquire()` with `eventFromTorchResult()`; dispatch `PERMISSION_PROMPTED` right before it ONLY when a prompt is actually expected (remembered grants and desktops skip it — awaiting-permission is an optional refinement; every TORCH_* outcome is legal from both `arming` and `awaiting-permission`).
  - **U1 (copy):** one framing per matrix cell (`torch` / `screen-positive` / `denied-offer-screen` / `why-camera` / `arming`) — the copy pack can be written against `ARM_MATRIX` directly; why-camera precedes the prompt (`awaiting-permission`), the hazard notice precedes the first broadcast (`preflight`, once per session).
- **Decisions made in F8** (within the plan's outcome, flagged for consumers):
  1. **State set reconciliation:** plan.md listed 10 states, the production brief 13 (adding arming/torch-ready/screen-mode); +2 screen-variant broadcast states (`broadcasting-on-screen` per the brief's takeover transition, `paused-on-screen` for pause/resume symmetry — resume must know which channel to re-drive) = 15. Every state reachable; no sinks.
  2. **`unsupported` vs `screen-mode`:** unsupported = the positive-framing reframe moment ("this device broadcasts by screen light"), screen-mode = armed-with-screen standby. Distinct renders; both exit forward (ACCEPT_SCREEN/RETRY_ARM/RESET).
  3. **`ready` = the GO-choreography state:** armed + committed; I2's standby→caution→GO frames render there; `ready`+GO starts the broadcast (branching on `context.channel`). CANCEL from ready returns to the armed standby.
  4. **Privacy-scope channel drop:** entering `done` from a torch broadcast sets channel null + emits `release-torch` (camera track released after broadcast — the fixed-by-scope privacy line); a held screen channel persists through done (F4 keeps fullscreen — replay needs no new gesture), so `done.REPLAY` branches: screen → `ready` directly, torch → `preflight` re-arm. REPLAY never re-notices (hazard once per session).
  5. **Rejection semantics:** KNOWN event in a wrong state = soft rejection (runtime race — a late channel outcome after the user moved on: no state/context change, `invalid` emitted, `{accepted:false}` returned); UNKNOWN event name = TypeError (wiring bug, the F3/F4/F5 discipline). Duplicated/racy TORCH_* outcomes after a completed arm reject softly (tested).
  6. **Effect placement:** `change` fires first (state authoritative), then each row effect as its own event; `TORCH_LOST` carries NO release effect (F3 already stopped the track on loss) while SWITCH_CHANNEL does (F3 won't self-release on a manual switch).
  7. **Phase-scoped context:** `takeover`/`doneReason`/`error` live only in their states (+takeover legitimately survives into done/error for explanatory copy) and are cleared by their state's exits; `fallbackReason` is deliberately the LAST arm resolution (screen-mode copy wants the why). The fuzz + hand-probes caught two real leaks during development (screen channel surviving `error→RETRY_ARM` into arming; takeover surviving the same path) — both fixed and pinned by tests.
  8. **No CANCEL/RESET during arming/awaiting-permission:** the OS prompt owns the screen mid-acquire and `acquire()` always settles (F3 contract), so the four TORCH_* outcomes always arrive; the machine cannot get stuck.
  9. **Screen switch is one-way** (torch-ready→screen-mode, no row back): re-arming torch is a fresh journey (RESET→REQUEST_BROADCAST), keeping the arming story single-entry.
- **Validation evidence:** `node --test test/states.test.mjs` → `ℹ tests 49 · pass 49 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, exit 0. Full suite `node --test 'test/*.test.mjs'` → `ℹ tests 266 · pass 266 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, **exit 0** (F1 74/74 + F2 38/38 + F4 30/30 + F5 32/32 + F3 43/43 all unchanged + F8 49/49; 74+38+30+32+43+49 = 266). Coverage (49 tests, 8 suites): constants/table shape (15 committed states unique+frozen+STATE_LIST order; 21 unique events; TRANSITIONS frozen at every level; table targets ⊆ STATES and effects ⊆ STATE_EFFECTS incl. branch-function evaluation over all three channel shapes; cold-open snapshot deep-equal+frozen); the matrix (all 16 cells deep-equal an INDEPENDENT hand-written literal via resolveArmMatrix AND via ARM_MATRIX; no-camera/insecure no-prompt cells; offerScreen true only in denied cells; screen-positive framing everywhere unsupported; every distinct matrix target state driven at runtime; carrier equivalence — dispatch sequences landing exactly where the cells say; TypeErrors incl. prototype-key probes 'toString'); F3 glue (SUPPORT_BY_FALLBACK_REASON covers every fallback reason except the denial; eventFromTorchResult over all outcomes × reason classes with support values; round-trip dispatch(acquire-result) lands per the matrix; malformed-result TypeErrors); journeys (cold-open torch with full state/via/effects sequences; remembered-grant skip; cold-open screen with replay-without-re-arm and zero release effects; no-torch-after-grant; denied→accept/retry-grant/reset; mid-broadcast TORCH_LOST takeover incl. paused variant and no-release-on-loss; manual switch standby+mid-broadcast with release; pause/resume/stop both channels with doneReason/channel asserts; torch-done replay re-arms through preflight without re-noticing; error recovery both kinds incl. error-REPLAY channel branch; cancel paths; notice exactly once per acknowledged session); exhaustive table cells (**441 probes: 21 state-variants × 21 events — every defined cell accepts and moves, every undefined cell rejects with frozen descriptive result, unchanged state+context, and an `invalid` event**; branch rows pinned per channel for ready.GO/CANCEL and done/error.REPLAY; release-torch asserted on exactly the torch-abandoning rows via before/after deltas; racy duplicate outcomes rejected); no dead ends (every state has outgoing; terminal-ish states recover within 1–2 hops; idle reachable from every variant ≤3 hops); dispatch/emitter semantics (TypeError wiring bugs vs soft races; change payload shape+freeze+effect order; payload pass-through untouched; frozen results/snapshots; throwing-listener isolation; unsubscribe both ways; distinct-render carriers scoped); purity + fuzz (comment-stripped source scan: zero DOM/timer/storage/globalThis tokens, only import is './torch.js'; **5 seeded storms × 3000 random events never leave the state set, the per-state channel invariant, or the phase-scoping invariants** — the fuzz caught the error→RETRY_ARM channel leak live). Adversarial hand-probes beyond the suite: symbol event names TypeError; takeover→BROADCAST_FAILED→RETRY_ARM full-scope retirement; replay-into-preflight hazard re-notice suppression; `can()` honesty on branch rows.
- **Headless limits (honest):** none for the logic — the module is synchronous and pure, so the whole surface IS headlessly verified. What remains for others: the rendered distinctness itself (I1/I2 static snapshots + wiring), the copy per framing (U1), and the real permission-prompt UX (Q3 device script). The `awaiting-permission` refinement's prompt-expectation heuristic (permissions-API vs first-run guess) is I2's to implement against `torch.status().supported`; the machine stays correct either way by design.
- **Deviations:** none from plan. Three scope notes: (a) the state set is the production brief's 13 + `broadcasting-on-screen` (the brief's own example transition) + `paused-on-screen` (symmetry so resume knows the channel) — a superset of plan.md's 10, blessed by the brief; (b) `test/states.test.mjs` is beyond the plan's `Files: js/states.js` per the task brief's explicit validation requirement; (c) `js/states.js` imports three frozen constant maps from `js/torch.js` — the plan's declared dep "F3 (detection)", kept to vocabulary only (no channel behavior), so states.js still imports cleanly anywhere torch.js does.

## T-F6 — Broadcast conductor · 2026-08-28

- **Status:** `awaiting-approval` (plan.md updated).
- **Changed files:** `js/conductor.js` (new), `test/conductor.test.mjs` (new); doc updates for this entry's bookkeeping (plan.md status, state.md phase note, this log). No other source files touched — `harness.html` deliberately NOT extended (its M1 runner keeps the temporary setTimeout path until I2 wires the real conductor; this task's brief scoped the files to the conductor + its test).
- **What was built:** `createConductor(options)` → `{ start, pause, resume, cancel, replay, status, destroy }` — runs an F2 schedule against the armed channels. One edge stream, three offset channels, ONE master clock: **audio at exact tMs** (`scheduleTone(anchorSec + tMs/1000, …)` — absolute AudioContext seconds, sample-accurate on the audio thread), **screen at exact tMs** (loop-dispatched, next-frame paint within R2's `SYNC_TOLERANCE_MS`), **torch at `tMs − TORCH_LEAD_MS`** (fire-and-forget `on()`/`off()`, never awaited — F3's resolve-means-accepted contract). ONLY the torch leads, so no early fire can ever bleed light/sound into a following gap on screen/audio. Exports: `TORCH_LEAD_MS = 120` (range 0–200 enforced at construction, per R2 "tuned once via Q3 — never at runtime"), `TORCH_LEAD_MIN_MS/MAX_MS`, `START_PAD_MS = TORCH_LEAD_MS + TICK_MS` (=145: runway so the first torch command, due at −120, fires at its exact early time instead of clamping), `CONDUCTOR_STATES`, `PAUSE_REASONS`. Imports: `eventIndexAt` (js/schedule.js — the committed re-anchor), `LOOKAHEAD_MS`/`TICK_MS` (js/audio.js — single source of truth per F5's note), `TORCH_EVENTS`/`TORCH_STATES` (js/torch.js — vocabulary only). DOM-free and state-machine-agnostic: clock (`now`), tick pair (`setTimeout`/`clearTimeout`), all three channels, and the visibilitychange port (`{addEventListener, removeEventListener, isHidden}`; default the global document via typeof guard) are injectable; machine transitions surface ONLY through injected callbacks.
- **The loop (R2 §5.3 implemented):** anchor once per broadcast inside the GO gesture — `screen.attach()` AND `audio.unlock()` are both initiated in `start()`'s synchronous prefix (fullscreen + context-resume need the user-activation window), then awaited; anchor = `now() + START_PAD_MS/1000`. Each wakeup dispatches, in order: (1) due torch commands, (2) due screen commands + per-mark progress (progress rides the exact-tMs pass in torch mode too — the tape head moves with the schedule, not the LED), (3) audio tones for marks entering the 100 ms lookahead window, (4) completion when every edge is dispatched and `t ≥ durationMs`. The wakeup is **due-aware with `TICK_MS` as the sleep CAP** (a conscious refinement, documented in the header): sleep = min(next torch due, next edge, next tone-window opening, durationMs, now + 25 ms) — R2's 25 ms cadence is preserved as the maximum sleep (the lookahead window can never starve), while screen edges land on their exact tMs instead of quantized to the tick grid; audio is still dispatched a full window ahead from one place, Wilson's shape intact. Position math is quantized to the microsecond so float noise in `(now − anchor)` can never flip an exact-boundary due check. NO self-calibration anywhere (banned: camera frames — R2 §4b).
- **Pause/resume (R2 visibilitychange):** `pause()` freezes the exact position P, kills the tick timer, `audio.stopAll()` (including the ≤ LOOKAHEAD_MS tail Web Audio still owns), and commands the ACTIVE light out. `resume()` re-anchors (`anchor = now − P/1000`) with the committed `eventIndexAt(events, P)` (max'd with the conductor's own cursor so a pause coinciding exactly with a dispatched edge can never double-fire); everything with `tMs < P` is elapsed and skipped; a mark mid-flight at P **completes with its remainder** — light restored at the resume instant, its tone re-scheduled from the (F5-clamped, past) onset to the EXACT OFF edge, so the boundary defining the following gap never moves; audio is never re-scheduled into the past (every post-resume tone call ends after its scheduling instant — asserted). Hidden wall-time is discarded (media-player semantics — the schedule position is what's preserved). Resuming on visible is deliberately NOT automatic (F8's RESUME is user intent; a self-resuming conductor would desync from the machine) — the host decides; `onPause({reason:'visibility'})` is the machine's PAUSE signal. Concurrent `resume()` calls re-anchor exactly once (`resuming` guard, same for `starting`); a cancel landing during resume's unlock-await wins.
- **Torch loss → seamless screen takeover:** the screen overlay is attached (standby, carrier down) at EVERY start — inside the gesture, so the takeover costs one class toggle and no mid-broadcast permission. F3's `lost` event switches the channel to screen WITHOUT losing position: mid-mark → `screen.on()` at the loss instant and the running mark completes on screen; every later edge (incl. its OFF) stays on its exact tMs; the torch cursor is retired and the listener unbound (a second loss is a no-op — there is no torch left to lose). Loss while PAUSED switches the channel only (no light action; resume then drives screen). The conductor never commands a dead torch and never calls `torch.release()` — the machine's `release-torch` effect is the host's hardware kill.
- **Output contract for consumers** (documented in the module header):
  - **F8/I2 (machine map — F6 emits, never drives):** `onPause({reason:'user'|'visibility', positionMs})` → dispatch `PAUSE`; `onComplete({durationMs, channel, markCount, positionMs})` → `BROADCAST_COMPLETE`; `onError({phase:'start'|'broadcast', error})` → `BROADCAST_FAILED`; `onTakeover({code, detail, positionMs, from:'torch', to:'screen', midMark, mark})` → `TORCH_LOST` (the `code` passthrough matches F3's `'track-ended'|'apply-rejected'`); `onResume({positionMs})` after the host calls `resume()` on machine RESUME.
  - **I2 (tape read-head):** `onProgress({tMs, mark, charIdx, letterIdx, symbolIdx, channel})` fires per mark at its exact ON tMs, plus once at resume (head re-reported at the frozen position).
  - **Lifecycle:** `start(sched)` from the GO gesture (replays the remembered schedule when called bare); `pause()/resume()/cancel()/replay()` map to machine PAUSE/RESUME/STOP/REPLAY — replay is a clean restart of the same schedule (mid-broadcast replay cancels first; F4 keeps fullscreen across `hide()`, so screen-path replay needs no new gesture); `destroy()` tears the conductor down without owning channel lifetimes. `status()` exposes the whole observable state (state, channel, positionMs, anchorSec, clockSource, cursors, tickPending…).
  - **Light-off guarantees:** pause/cancel/fail command the ACTIVE channel off (torch off is best-effort fire-and-forget; the hardware guarantee remains the host's `torch.release()` via the machine effect); cancel/fail also drop the screen carrier. Natural completion only drops the carrier — the schedule's own last edge is an OFF (F2 invariant) already dispatched at its exact time.
- **Decisions made in F6** (within the plan's outcome, flagged for consumers):
  1. **Due-aware wakeup, TICK_MS as the sleep cap** — the one deliberate refinement of R2's loop shape (see above). Without it, screen edges land at `ceil(t/25)*25` — up to 24 ms of avoidable quantization INSIDE the 45 ms budget; with it, mock-clock exactness is literal and real-browser skew reduces to timer jitter alone.
  2. **Mid-flight mark completes with its remainder** on resume/takeover (position-exact media-player semantics), rather than skipping to the next boundary — the OFF edge (the gap boundary) stays exact either way; F5's late-start clamp exists for exactly this.
  3. **Manual resume after visibility-visible** (decision explained above); the conductor's visibility port pauses on hidden only.
  4. **Clock fallback chain: injected `now` > audio clock (when unlock succeeds) > `performance.now()/1000` for light-only broadcasts** — R2 calls performance.now "required but not sufficient"; with no audio to sync against, it is sufficient. Selected once per broadcast, never mixed mid-flight.
  5. **`onProgress` fires on ON edges** (head moves onto a mark as it plays) — one event per mark, identity straight from the F2 edge.
  6. **Empty schedule (whitespace-only message) = instantly-complete broadcast** (channels armed in-gesture, carrier dropped, `onComplete`) rather than an error — F2 allows empty schedules; I2 should still disable GO on empty input.
  7. **Environment failures report via `onError` + state FAILED; wiring bugs throw** (start-while-running, malformed schedules/options) — the F3/F4/F5 discipline. Consumer-callback exceptions are swallowed (a broken UI must not stop a broadcast); channel sync throws on the hot path DO fail it.
- **Validation evidence:** `node --test test/conductor.test.mjs` → `ℹ tests 43 · pass 43 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, exit 0. Full suite `node --test 'test/*.test.mjs'` → `ℹ tests 309 · pass 309 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, **exit 0** (F1 74 + F2 38 + F4 30 + F5 32 + F3 43 + F8 49 all unchanged + F6 43; 266+43 = 309). Coverage (43 tests, 11 suites, against a manually-advanced FakeClock that fires each timer AT its exact due time + dependency-free recording channel stubs): committed constants (lead 120 ∈ [0,200], pad = lead+tick, 25/100 imported from F5); construction TypeErrors/RangeErrors (missing channels, bad callbacks/clock/visibility, lead out of range incl. both endpoints OK); **screen-mode timing at EVERY WPM step** ("SOS" @ LEARNER/STANDARD/BRISK: every on/off deep-equal to `A + tMs`; tones deep-equal `A/1000 + tMs/1000` pairs AND their scheduling instants deep-equal `A + tMs − 100`; progress deep-equal per-mark identity + channel; hide + onComplete at exactly `A + durationMs`; master clock = audio clock, anchored after unlock); torch-mode (cmds deep-equal `A + tMs − 120` incl. the first at exactly `A − 120`; screen stays standby; listener bound then unbound; custom lead 200 with widened pad shifts ONLY torch); **takeover at four probed positions** (during a dot t=100 / a dash t=2200 / a gap t=1500 / the final mark t=6300: exact expected torch-cmd prefix by due-time filter, screen restore only when mid-mark, all later edges exact, audio untouched, onTakeover payload deep-equal incl. mark identity and code passthrough; loss while PAUSED; double-loss ignored); pause/resume exactness (mid-mark remainder at exact resumed offsets, elapsed-marks-skipped, no double-fires — ON count = markCount + exactly one restore, pause-on-an-edge-coincidence never re-fires the loop edge, gap pause without restore, concurrent resume once, no-op guards, clock-stall catch-up fires every edge in order then completes); visibilitychange (hidden → paused at exact position with reason 'visibility' + stopAll + lights-out; visible does NOT auto-resume; port unbound on destroy); replay (after completion: identical relative timing from a fresh anchor, attach idempotent, no leaked timers/tones; mid-broadcast: cancels first — stopAll, hide, no first-run onComplete; replay-before-start throws); cancel (screen: off+hide at the instant, audio killed, dead quiet after, no onComplete; torch: last command is OFF and nothing follows — the LED is never left commanded-ON; after takeover only the live channel is commanded; idempotent; destroy resets to a clean conductor); failures (attach refusal → onError(start)+FAILED; mid-broadcast sync throw → onError(broadcast)+lights-out+loop dead; async channel rejections produce ZERO process-level unhandled rejections with a spy; start-while-active + five malformed-schedule shapes reject; broken onProgress consumer cannot stop the broadcast); clock sources (light-only via injected clock with zero tones; whitespace-only instant completion with channels armed; **real-clock smoke** of the performance.now fallback on real setTimeout at ±80 ms; audio re-unlock on resume — the iOS re-suspension path); hygiene (comment-stripped source scan: zero storage/navigator/window/DOM/frame tokens; the only document reference is the lazy typeof-guarded visibility default).
- **Self-audit finds during development (each now pinned by a test):** a TDZ shadowing bug in the takeover payload (`const positionMs` vs the `positionMs()` helper — caught by the first takeover probe); the resume partial-tone double-schedule (resume scheduled it, then the next tick's lookahead re-scheduled it — cursor now skips past the restored mark's OFF edge); the concurrent-resume re-anchor race (`resuming` guard, mirroring `starting`); and the pause-lights-out coinciding with an edge OFF at the same ms (correct behavior, test documents it).

The real-clock smoke test covers the performance-fallback path only, loosely (±80 ms).
- **Deviations:** none from plan. Three scope notes: (a) `test/conductor.test.mjs` is the plan-declared second file; (b) `harness.html` untouched (see Changed files) — I2 swaps its temporary runner for the conductor; (c) decisions 1–7 above are F6-owned interface/timing decisions within the plan's outcome, flagged so I2/Q3 consume them consciously (esp. 1's due-aware cap, 2's remainder semantics, and 3's manual-resume-after-visible, which the machine's paused states already model).

### T-F6 verification (independent production verifier) · 2026-08-28 — **FAIL** (F6 returned to `in-progress`)

- **Status:** FAIL. Two reproducible torch-mode pause/resume defects (below) unmeet the plan's pause/replay outcome and R2 §5.3's committed "torch commands are emitted early … (re-anchor on resume)" behavior. F6 set to `in-progress` in plan.md. Verifier did not author F6; probes written independently (mock clock + recording channel stubs + the REAL `js/schedule.js`/`js/morse.js`), script deleted after the run.
- **Test run (re-executed by verifier):** `node --test 'test/*.test.mjs'` → `ℹ tests 309 · pass 309 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, **exit 0** — matches the worker's claim exactly. The suite is green; the defects are OUTSIDE its probe placements (its pause/resume probes never land inside a TORCH_LEAD window — see below).
- **What passed (verifier's own 48-check probe battery):** committed constants (`TORCH_LEAD_MS=120`, bounds `0–200` RangeError-enforced, `START_PAD_MS=145=lead+tick`, `TICK_MS=25`/`LOOKAHEAD_MS=100` imported from `js/audio.js`); torch-mode baseline timing ("EE" @ BRISK: torch cmds exactly `tMs−120` = `[on−120, off−20, on280, off380]`, first at exactly `A−120` via the 145 ms pad; tones exact `[0,100],[400,500]`; screen standby `attach`+`hide` only; completion at exactly `durationMs`; listener bound→unbound; zero pending timers); the early torch fire does NOT bleed into following gaps in an unpaused run (every edge command is the exact stream shifted −120 wholesale — spacing ≥100 ms preserved at BRISK, no reordering, screen/audio never lead); **torch-loss takeover** (mid-final-mark loss @450: `screen.on()` at the loss instant, final OFF exact @500, dead torch never commanded again, `onTakeover` payload incl. `code` passthrough + mark identity, listener unbound, second loss ignored; replay after loss re-resolves to screen and replays 0/100/400/500 byte-exact); pause-at-exact-edge (discharged + undischarged: each mark lit exactly once, no double-fire of the loop edge); rapid GO→STOP→GO (cancel mid-mark: last torch cmd OFF + `stopAll` + `hide` + 0 pending timers + nothing fires after; immediate replay: clean full run from fresh anchor); long-dash-final message @ BRISK ("ET": dash ON@400, final OFF@exact 700, dark after, completion at `durationMs`); visibilitychange port (hidden during a TORCH_LEAD window pauses at exact position with reason `visibility` + lights out; visible does NOT auto-resume); destroy unbinds everything; screen-mode pause/resume in the SAME windows is immune (OFF at exact 100, ON at exact 400 — no gap bleed, no missing mark); DOM-freeness (the only `document` references are the lazy typeof-guarded visibility default at lines 810–816 — injectable port confirmed; no window/navigator/storage/frame tokens); no state-machine import (only `eventIndexAt` from schedule.js, `LOOKAHEAD_MS`/`TICK_MS` from audio.js, `TORCH_EVENTS`/`TORCH_STATES` from torch.js); scope exact (files newer than `harness.html` (01:41): only `js/conductor.js` + `test/conductor.test.mjs` in the F6 window — `harness.html` and all prior-task files untouched).
- **Defect 1 — torch stuck ON through the following gap after a mid-mark pause inside the OFF edge's lead window.** Repro ("EE" @ BRISK — events `on@0 off@100 on@400 off@500`; lead 120): pause (user OR visibilitychange — same path) at P=60, i.e. inside `[offT−120, offT) = [−20,100)` where the mark's early torch-OFF (due −20) has ALREADY been dispatched; `pause()`'s `lightsDown` countermands with an OFF. `resume()` restores the light (`torchCommand(true)`) — but `torchIdx = Math.max(resumeIdx, torchIdx)` (js/conductor.js:533) keeps the cursor PAST the OFF edge, so the resumed mark's end is never commanded out. Post-resume torch cmds: `on@60 (restore), on@280 (redundant), off@380` — the commanded LED is ON continuously from the resume instant through the ENTIRE letter gap `[100,400)` to 380: "E E" renders as one ~320 ms flash. Violates the module's own header invariant ("the boundary that defines the following gap never moves") and R2 §5.3's per-edge torch emission. A correct resume must re-command the pending OFF (at latest at the exact `offT`).
- **Defect 2 — upcoming mark never lit after a mid-gap pause inside the ON edge's lead window.** Same repro, pause at P=340 (inside `[onT−120, onT) = [280,400)`): the next mark's early torch-ON (due 280) already fired; pause's `lightsDown` countermands it with an OFF. `resume()` re-anchors `lightIdx`/`toneIdx` to `resumeIdx` but clamps `torchIdx` only UPWARD (line 533), so the mark's ON is never re-issued. Post-resume torch cmds: `off@380` only — mark `[400,500)` is DARK on the LED while its tone still plays (`[400,500]` scheduled, verified) and progress still reports it: a dropped Morse element on the light channel, audio↔light desync. Root cause of both: the torch cursor is treated as "already done" for commands whose lead-shifted due passed pre-pause, even though pause actively destroyed their effect (`lightsDown` OFF) — `lightIdx`/`toneIdx` are re-anchored DOWN to `resumeIdx` but `torchIdx` is not.
- **Blast radius:** every torch-mode pause→resume whose freeze point falls within `TORCH_LEAD_MS` (120 ms) before ANY edge. At BRISK dot sequences (min element 100 ms < 120 ms) the union of these windows covers effectively the WHOLE timeline — on a real torch device, essentially any pause/resume during dot-dense BRISK content corrupts the remaining broadcast (gap-merge via Defect 1 or missing marks via Defect 2). At LEARNER/STANDARD each edge contributes a 120 ms window — the R2-mandated `visibilitychange → hidden` auto-pause lands there with high probability. Screen mode is immune (verified). Audio is immune (F5's late-start clamp; the past-onset remainder request `[0,100]` at resume position 60 is the documented F5 contract — onset clamps, OFF edge exact).
- **Why the suite missed it:** its torch-mode pause/resume probes froze OUTSIDE the 120 ms lead windows, and its "no double-fires — ON count = markCount + exactly one restore" assertion counts total calls rather than post-resume coverage per edge, so a countermanded-then-skipped command still satisfies the count.
- **Fix direction (for the worker, not prescriptive):** on resume, re-anchor `torchIdx` DOWN to `resumeIdx` (mirroring `lightIdx`/`toneIdx`) so lead-shifted commands whose due already passed re-fire at the resume tick (a redundant command is safe — fire-and-forget, spacing preserved); alternatively explicitly walk `[resumeIdx, torchIdx)` and re-issue the pending command(s). Pin with probes at `offT−lead+ε` and `onT−lead+ε` at BRISK (dot followed by symbol gap is the worst case, as R2 §5.1's spacing analysis implies).
- **Non-blocking observations (recorded for the worker, none gating):** (a) pausing exactly at an undischarged ON edge makes `resume()` emit `onProgress` for that mark twice (resume head-report + the immediate tick dispatch — same identity, same/advancing `tMs`; benign for a read-head, monotone); (b) `resume()`'s mid-mark `screenCommand(true)` is not exception-wrapped, so a synchronously-throwing screen channel rejects the `resume()` promise instead of routing through `onError` (tick-path sync throws DO fail correctly via `fail()`); (c) an injected `torchLeadMs` > 120 with the default `startPadMs` (computed from the DEFAULT lead) under-pads the first command (fires at −145, not `−lead`) — the suite covered the widened-pad case only.
- **Evidence:** verifier probe battery (48 checks; 39 pass, 6 fail on the two defects above, 3 harness artifacts resolved as benign and cross-checked: `attach` position is logged before any anchor exists; the past-onset tone request is F5's clamp contract; the same-ms double-OFF at pause is the documented idempotent coincidence) + the minimal repro above; probe script deleted after the run (zero repo residue).

### T-F6 retry — torch-mode pause/resume re-anchor fix (production worker) · 2026-08-28

- **Status:** `awaiting-approval` (plan.md updated; F6 was returned to `in-progress` by the FAIL verification above).
- **Changed files:** `js/conductor.js` (edited), `test/conductor.test.mjs` (edited — +11 tests); doc updates for this entry's bookkeeping (plan.md status, state.md phase note, this log). No other files touched.
- **Root cause, confirmed as diagnosed:** `resume()` clamped the torch cursor only upward (`torchIdx = Math.max(resumeIdx, torchIdx)`), so commands whose lead-shifted due (`tMs − 120`) had already fired pre-pause — and been countermanded by `pause()`'s lights-out OFF — were never re-issued: the resumed mark's OFF skipped (Defect 1, LED commanded ON through the following gap) or the upcoming mark's ON skipped (Defect 2, dark mark against its tone).
- **The fix (two parts, one invariant):**
  1. **Down-anchor:** `torchIdx = resumeIdx` on resume (mirroring `lightIdx`/`toneIdx`). Re-firing a countermanded command is safe (fire-and-forget, idempotent dark — the verifier's own note).
  2. **Catch-up shaping via `torchDueMs(i)`** (new helper, used by BOTH `dispatchDues` and `scheduleNextWake`): after a resume, an edge whose lead due already passed at the freeze point (`tMs ≤ pausedAtMs + leadMs`, tracked as `torchCatchUpT`, reset at every `start()`/`destroy()`) fires — **ON immediately at the resume tick** (the mark must be lit ASAP, never dark against its tone), **OFF at its EXACT edge time**. The OFF is deliberately NOT fired at the resume instant: whenever an OFF's lead due passed, its mark's ON was necessarily also (re)issued at that same instant (the mid-mark restore, or an immediate catch-up ON — an OFF's due passing implies its ON's passed earlier), so a same-instant OFF would zero-length the just-lit mark. That is Defect 2 again — the naive `torchIdx = resumeIdx` alone reproduces it for pauses landing in `[nextOffT − lead, nextOnT)` or exactly on an ON edge (pinned by tests: pause @390 and @400 in "EE" @ BRISK both render the full mark `[400,500)` as `on@390, off@500` / `on@400, off@500`). Firing the OFF exactly AT `offT` is the verifier's stated bound verbatim ("re-command the pending OFF (at latest at the exact offT)"), keeps the gap-start boundary exact in command space (mirroring the screen channel's resume), avoids the same-instant on/off command pair (the W3C #275 rapid-toggle class R2 §3.1b warns about), and never commands light into a gap beyond the existing lead design. Every future edge from `resumeIdx` gets exactly one torch command post-resume; no edge fires twice post-resume (edges dispatched by the loop before the pause sit before `resumeIdx` and are skipped — exact-edge pauses keep this true).
- **Verifier's non-blocking observations, addressed:** (b) `resume()`'s mid-mark restore is now exception-wrapped — a synchronously-throwing channel fails the broadcast via `onError({phase:'broadcast'})` + state FAILED (the tick-path discipline) instead of rejecting the `resume()` promise (pinned by test); (c) the default `startPadMs` now tracks the EFFECTIVE lead (`options.startPadMs ?? leadMs + TICK_MS`), so an injected `torchLeadMs: 200` without an explicit pad anchors at 225 and the first command fires at its exact `−200` (pinned by test; the existing injects-both test is unchanged). (a) — the duplicate `onProgress` when pausing exactly on an undischarged ON edge — left as-is: same identity, same/advancing `tMs`, monotone for a read-head (documented benign by the verifier; the retry's exact-ON-edge test pins the pair explicitly).
- **Validation evidence:** `node --test 'test/*.test.mjs'` → `ℹ tests 320 · pass 320 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, **exit 0** (309 pre-existing all green — F1 74 + F2 38 + F4 30 + F5 32 + F3 43 + F8 49 + F6's original 43 — plus the 11 new; 309+11 = 320). `node --check` clean on module and test. **The new tests catch the original defects:** with the old `Math.max(resumeIdx, torchIdx)` line temporarily reintroduced, 7 of the 11 fail (both verifier repros, the @390 and exact-ON-edge follow-ons, repeated pauses, the visibilitychange variant, the post-resume takeover); fix restored → all green.
- **Regression tests added (all hand-computed FULL command sequences, never call counts; "EE" @ BRISK — edges `on@0 off@100 on@400 off@500`, the verifier's own repro schedule, where the 120 ms lead windows cover effectively the whole 100 ms-dot timeline):** pause @60 inside the mark's final lead window (post-resume `on@60 (restore), off@100 (pending OFF re-commanded at its exact edge), on@280, off@380` + tones `[clamped,100] [400,500]` + completion payload); pause @340 inside the gap's lead window (post-resume `on@340 (immediate), off@380`); pause @390 with BOTH upcoming dues passed (`on@390, off@500` — mark not zero-lengthened); pause exactly ON the ON edge @400 (`on@400, off@500` — full mark); pause exactly ON the OFF edge @100 (edge never re-fired; `on@280, off@380`); repeated pause/resume across both windows (every cycle complete, one completion); visibilitychange-hidden inside the lead window (reason `visibility`, same repaired stream — the R2 auto-pause path); takeover after a lead-window resume (dead torch frozen, screen finishes at exact tMs, takeover payload deep-equal) and torch lost while PAUSED inside the gap's lead window (resume drives screen `on@400, off@500` exact); resume-restore sync throw → `onError(broadcast)` + FAILED; injected lead 200 widens the default pad (anchor 225, first command at exact `−200`).
- **Verifier's repros re-run against the fixed conductor** (standalone mock-clock script, deleted after the run — zero repo residue; schedule-ms command streams): Repro 1 "EE" @ BRISK paused @60 → post-resume torch cmds `on@60, off@100, on@280, off@380` (was `on@60, on@280, off@380`); Repro 2 paused @340 → `on@340, off@380` (was `off@380` only). Both complete `{durationMs:500, channel:'torch', markCount:2, positionMs:500}`.
- **Passing behaviors re-verified unchanged:** exact `tMs` for screen/audio and `tMs − 120` for torch in unpaused runs (the full "SOS" torch deep-equal test untouched and green); no gap bleed (the lead-shifted stream fires wholesale; catch-up OFFs land ON the boundary, never after it); master-clock anchoring (`AudioContext.currentTime`, pad runway — the pad default now tracks an injected lead, a strict improvement); LED-off invariant at cancel (last torch command is OFF, nothing after — test green); takeover at four probed positions + paused-loss + double-loss (green); replay resets cleanly (no leaked timers/tones — `torchCatchUpT` reset at `start()`); leak-freedom (`driveToIdle` asserts zero pending timers in every new test); DOM-freeness hygiene scan green.
- **Deviations:** none from plan or from the verifier's fix direction (down-anchor + re-fire; the OFF's fire time is the verifier's own "at latest the exact offT" bound, chosen exactly AT `offT` for the zero-length and rapid-toggle reasons above — the reasoning is documented in the module header and here for the re-verifier).

When the inter-mark gap g satisfies `onT − 120 < offT` (g < TORCH_LEAD_MS + slack), the index-ordered dispatch loop (`while … torchDueMs(torchIdx) <= t`) cannot fire the ON before the pending OFF, so both fire in the SAME tick at `offT` — 0 ms command spacing.   - **Blast radius:** every torch-mode pause→resume whose freeze point lands in `[offT−120, offT)` of a NON-FINAL mark followed by a gap < ~153 ms — at BRISK that is every symbol-gap-adjacent mark (the entire dot, since dot 100 < lead 120); at STANDARD every symbol-gap-adjacent mark (30 ms dark). The R2-mandated `visibilitychange → hidden` auto-pause hits the same windows (verified: identical stream via the visibility port). Screen mode is immune. This is inherent to the previous verifier's "at latest at the exact offT" bound and matches that verifier's own hand-expected `on@60, off@100, on@280` stream; it is reported for completeness, not as a FAIL trigger. The FAIL triggers are the 0 ms same-instant pair + merged marks (BRISK) and the sub-frame spacing (STANDARD).
  - **Root cause:** `torchDueMs()` (js/conductor.js:361–365) treats catch-up timing per-edge in isolation; the interaction — a catch-up OFF's exact time falling at/after the NEXT ON's normal due when gap < lead — was not considered. The retry entry's justification ("an OFF's due passing implies its ON's passed earlier") reasons only about the OFF's own mark.
  - **Why the suite missed it:** all 11 new regression tests probe "EE" @ BRISK (letter gap 300 > lead — collision impossible) or single-mark/edge-coincidence cases; `SOS_BRISK` exists in the suite but no pause probe lands in a symbol-gap window; no assertion examines inter-command spacing or commanded-dark between consecutive marks.

### T-F6 attempt 3 — the spacing-floor catch-up derivation (Defect 3 fix, production worker) · 2026-08-28

- **Status:** `awaiting-approval` (plan.md updated; F6 was returned to `in-progress` by the re-verification FAIL above).
- **Changed files:** `js/conductor.js` (edited), `test/conductor.test.mjs` (edited — +11 tests); doc updates for this entry's bookkeeping (plan.md status, state.md phase note, this log). No other source files touched; `TORCH_LEAD_MS`, the WPM steps, and every other module untouched.
- **Root cause, confirmed as diagnosed:** `torchDueMs()` shaped each catch-up edge in isolation — a catch-up OFF at its EXACT edge time while the FOLLOWING mark's ON kept its normal lead due (`onT − 120`). When the inter-mark gap < `TORCH_LEAD_MS` (every BRISK symbol gap 100 < 120; STANDARD's gap−lead = 30 < one 33 ms frame), the next ON's due PRECEDED the OFF's exact time, so the index-ordered dispatch loop held the ON until the OFF's tick and fired both in the SAME wakeup — commanded dark 0 (BRISK) or 30 ms (STANDARD): adjacent marks merged into one flash while both tones played.
- **THE INVARIANT (established, generalizing the verifier's fix direction):** in torch COMMAND space, after any pause/resume sequence, between any commanded OFF and the next commanded ON there is ALWAYS a dark gap ≥ `TORCH_SPACING_FLOOR_MS = 33` ms (new named export; ⌈1000/30⌉ = one 30 fps camera-preview frame — R2 §3.1c: the HAL applies torch requests per preview frame, so a shorter commanded dark can be skipped entirely, merging marks; the W3C #275 / html5-qrcode #634 rapid-toggle class R2 §3.1b spaces commands to avoid. An unpaused run holds the floor by construction — consecutive torch commands are spaced by a Morse element, ≥100 ms at every committed step — so resume catch-up is the only path that can compress it, and the floor is what the catch-up scheduler is derived FROM).
- **The fix — re-derivation, not a branch patch:** resume() now builds the ENTIRE post-resume torch due schedule once (`shapeTorchDues()`, a monotone array over the pending edges; `torchDueMs(i)` reads it, and both `dispatchDues` and `scheduleNextWake` consume the same array — the wake can never target a due that a later edge has already passed):
  1. **Base dues:** a countermanded ON whose lead due passed → the resume instant (a mark never plays dark against its tone); a countermanded OFF → its EXACT edge time (gap boundary re-commanded no later than offT, never at the resume instant where it would zero-length the restored mark); everything else → the normal lead due.
  2. **Dark floor, two-sided:** a pending OFF is PULLED to `min(its due, nextOnDue − floor)` when that stays strictly after the previous command — the restored mark renders slightly short in command space, the accepted trade (dark always exists, marks stay distinct, information preserved). When the pull would land at/before the resume instant (or invert the just-restored mark), the OFF KEEPS its exact edge and the FOLLOWING ON is advanced to `prevDue + floor`, capped at the mark's own tMs (an ON is never commanded later than its true edge — hence never after its own OFF's due). Either way commanded dark ≥ floor.
  3. **Monotone:** dues never decrease — dispatch order = edge order, times strictly increase at every committed step.
  Ordering classes checked in the derivation and pinned by tests: catch-up OFF vs catch-up ON (both dues passed — the pull is `R − floor < R`, always infeasible → OFF exact + ON advanced), catch-up OFF vs normal-due ON (pull when `on2Due − floor ≥ R`, else OFF exact + ON advanced), normal OFF vs catch-up ON (vacuous — a catch-up ON's preceding OFF is necessarily catch-up too — but handled by the same generic walk), pauses exactly ON an ON edge / OFF edge (dispatched-first semantics preserved), repeated pause/resume cycles (recomputed per resume), mid-dash at BRISK, symbol/letter/word gaps at every step.
- **Verifier repros re-run against the fixed conductor** (standalone mock-clock script, deleted after the run — zero repo residue; schedule-ms post-resume command streams): Repro A "I" @ BRISK paused @50 → `on@50 (restore), off@100 (EXACT edge), on@133 (advanced to offT+floor), off@180` — was `on@50, off@100, on@100, off@180` (off/on same tick, LED continuously ON 50→180); dot 1 keeps its FULL 50 ms remainder, commanded dark [100,133] = 33. Repro B "N" @ BRISK @250 → `on@250, off@300, on@333, off@380` — was `on@250, off@300, on@300, off@380`. Repro C "I" @ STANDARD @90 → `on@90, off@147 (PULLED — feasible), on@180, off@330` — was `on@90, off@150, on@180, off@330` (30 ms sub-frame dark). Also pinned: "S" @ BRISK @45 (feasible pull: `on@45, off@47, on@80, off@180, on@280, off@380`), "I" @ BRISK @99 (both dues passed, max compression: `on@99, off@100, on@133, off@180`), the visibilitychange-hidden variant of Repro A (identical stream), and an injected floor 60 run proving the knob drives the derivation and that a pull EQUAL to the restore instant is refused (strict inequality — a zero-length mark is never produced).
- **THE SWEEP (would have caught all three defects — demonstrated by mutation):** a systematic pause-position sweep steps through pause positions every 5 ms across a whole schedule (BRISK "SOS N I" = 980 positions, STANDARD "SOS" = 810, LEARNER "EE" = 240) and asserts the commanded-stream invariant at EVERY position: off→next-on spacing ≥ floor; full coverage (exactly one command per pending edge + the restore); no double-fires (strict alternation from ON); nothing fires before the resume instant; times strictly increase; every pending mark's tone re-scheduled with its EXACT OFF edge; exactly one completion; zero leaked timers. Mutation checks (script deleted after the runs): reintroducing the attempt-2 per-edge shaping fails 9 of the 11 new tests (all three repros + pull branch + max compression + visibilitychange + floor-60 + the BRISK and STANDARD sweeps) while all 54 pre-existing tests stay green (the fix changes nothing they pin); reintroducing the ORIGINAL defect-1/2 clamp (`torchIdx = max(resumeIdx, torchIdx)`) fails all three sweeps plus the attempt-2 regression tests.
- **Validation evidence:** `node --test 'test/*.test.mjs'` → `ℹ tests 331 · pass 331 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, **exit 0** (320 pre-existing all green — F1 74 + F2 38 + F4 30 + F5 32 + F3 43 + F8 49 + F6's 54 — plus the 11 new; 320+11 = 331). `node --check` clean on module and test. Every previously-verified invariant re-validated green: master clock anchored once per GO; screen/audio at exact tMs; torch at `tMs − 120` unpaused with no gap bleed (the full "SOS" torch deep-equal untouched); Defects 1 and 2 repros (@60 → `on@60, off@100, on@280, off@380`; @340 → `on@340, off@380`) keep their corrected streams byte-exact; takeover mid-mark/final-mark/paused with the dead torch never commanded; cancel mid-mark → last command OFF; replay leak-free (`torchDues` reset at start/destroy/takeover); DOM-free (hygiene scan green); no state-machine import; `TORCH_LEAD_MS` bounds 0–200 unchanged.
- **Design notes for the re-verifier:** (a) the spacing floor is an F6-owned constant `TORCH_SPACING_FLOOR_MS = 33` with an injectable `torchSpacingFloorMs` option (validated positive, exposed in `status()`), the same injectability discipline as tickMs/lookaheadMs; (b) the pull-vs-advance decision follows the verifier's own two suggestions in order — pull `min(offT, nextOnDue − floor)` when feasible "(still ≥ the resume instant, still no gap-stuck-on)", else "advance the ON to max(nextOnDue, offT + minSpacing)"; (c) the accepted-bound consequence the re-verifier noted (a catch-up OFF shortens the FOLLOWING commanded gap by TORCH_LEAD_MS when no pull/advance binds, e.g. letter gap at BRISK 300→180 commanded) is unchanged and still ≥ floor everywhere; when the rules DO bind, commanded dark is exactly the floor; (d) sub-floor gaps (< 33 ms between consecutive Morse elements) cannot occur at any committed step (min element = 100 ms at BRISK) — for pathological injected schedules with gap < floor the derivation degrades by capping the advanced ON at its exact tMs (floor temporarily unsatisfiable), never by inverting or reordering commands.
- **Deviations:** none from plan, from R2's committed constants, or from the re-verifier's fix direction. The conductor's file list is unchanged (js/conductor.js + test/conductor.test.mjs).
- **Delegation record:** none — task executed directly by the production worker. Inputs: the T-F6 re-verification entry (Defect 3 repros, sweep-flagged positions, fix direction, oracle), R2 §3.1b/§3.1c (the ≥33 ms frame-quantization spacing argument and the W3C #275 rapid-toggle class), R2 §5.3 (unchanged lead/loop commitments), and the attempt-2 entry's pinned regression streams (kept byte-exact).

Defects 1/2 repros still corrected byte-exact: "EE"@BRISK@60 → `on@60, off@100, on@280, off@380`; @340 → `on@340, off@380`. Worker pins reproduced: "S"@BRISK@45 → `on@45, off@47, on@80, off@180, on@280, off@380` (feasible pull; restored dot 2 ms in command space, dark exactly 33); "I"@BRISK@99 → `on@99, off@100, on@133, off@180` (max compression, pull correctly refused at `resumeMs − floor < resumeMs`); exact-OFF-edge pause @100 (dispatched-first) → `on@280, off@380` — the edge never re-fired.
- **The sweep (verifier's own, stride 7 ms — a different phase than the suite's 5 ms):** pause positions every 7 ms across BRISK "I" (42 positions), "N" (71), "SOS" (399) and STANDARD "I" (64) = 576 single-pause/resume runs, each asserting the FULL battery on the post-resume commanded stream: sequence coverage (the mid-mark restore + exactly one command per pending schedule edge), no command before the resume instant, monotone dues, strict alternation, NO zero-length mark (every commanded ON–OFF pair length > 0), commanded dark ≥ `TORCH_SPACING_FLOOR_MS` = 33 between every OFF→ON, no ON at/after its own OFF's edge time, no OFF after its own edge time, last command = the final OFF at ≤ `durationMs` (final mark never lost), every pending mark's tone re-scheduled exactly once with its EXACT OFF edge and never ending in the past, exactly one completion, torch silent while paused, zero pending timers at end, nothing fires after completion. **All 576 positions pass every check.**
- **Pause-during-catch-up (double pause/resume):** every 7 ms first-pause position on "I"/"N"/"SOS"@BRISK × second pause at +7 ms and +21 ms (≈ 990 sequences, second pause landing inside the first resume's shaped catch-up region, incl. before a pending pulled/advanced OFF): epoch-1 prefix holds alternation/floor/mark-length/no-early; the recomputed epoch-2 stream passes the full battery at its own freeze point; exactly one completion per run; leak-free. Repeated catch-up shaping is correctly re-derived per resume (`torchCatchUpT`/`torchDues` recomputed, reset at start/destroy/takeover).
- **Takeover during catch-up:** torch `lost` fired at resume+35 ms for every 7 ms pause position on "I"/"N"@BRISK (+ "SOS" every 35 ms), and while PAUSED after a catch-up resume (SOS every 35 ms): the dead torch is NEVER commanded again (running loss or paused loss, including after the post-loss resume); the screen completes every remaining edge at EXACT `tMs` (+ the restore exactly at the loss instant when mid-mark, incl. final-mark losses); `onTakeover` fires once with the code passthrough; completion lands on the screen channel; leak-free.
- **The trade (judged, accepted):** when the floor is feasible the pending OFF is PULLED to `min(offT, nextOnDue − floor)` — the restored mark renders short in command space (worst observed commanded remainder 1–2 ms: `on@99/off@100`, `on@45/off@47`) but never zero (strict `pullTo > prevDue`), and the commanded dark is exactly the floor; when the pull would invert the just-restored mark the OFF keeps its exact edge and the FOLLOWING ON advances to `offT + floor`, capped at its own `tMs` (e.g. `on@133` for a mark at 200 — still 67 ms inside the lead design; the cap never binds at committed steps since every inter-mark gap ≥ 100 > 33). Across all ~1,870 probe sequences: no zero-length mark, no vanished dark gap, no ON at/after its own OFF due, no lost final mark.
- **Standing invariants re-verified green:** master clock anchored ONCE per GO on the audio clock (`clockSource:'audio'`, anchor = start-clock + `START_PAD_MS` 145, constant through a run, fresh anchor on replay); screen/audio at exact `tMs` ("EE"@BRISK screen stream `[0,100,400,500]`, tones exactly `[0,100],[400,500]`); torch unpaused = the exact stream shifted −120 wholesale, first command at exactly `anchor − 120`, no gap bleed; replay reproduces the identical relative stream from a fresh anchor with zero leaks; cancel mid-mark → last torch command OFF, nothing after, no onComplete; `TORCH_LEAD_MS=120` with `[0,200]` RangeError-enforced (−1/201/200.5 reject, 0/200 accept); DOM-free (the only `document` reference is the lazy typeof-guarded visibility default at js/conductor.js:997–1004; no window/navigator/storage/frame tokens); no state-machine import (imports: `eventIndexAt` from schedule.js, `LOOKAHEAD_MS`/`TICK_MS` from audio.js, `TORCH_EVENTS`/`TORCH_STATES` from torch.js); scope exact — mtime window holds only `js/conductor.js` + `test/conductor.test.mjs` (04:00–04:01) plus the three bookkeeping docs; `harness.html` and all prior-task files untouched (≤ 02:02, F8-era).
- **Floor sanity:** `TORCH_SPACING_FLOOR_MS = 33` = ⌈1000/30⌉ is justified directly by R2 §3.1c ("~33 ms frame-quantization" of torch requests at the default 30 fps camera preview, per the Android camera2 per-request-settings source) and §3.1b (W3C #275 rapid-toggle class: commands must be spaced; committed steps naturally space ≥ 100 ms > one 33 ms frame — so resume catch-up is genuinely the only compressing path, which is where the floor is enforced). It is injectable (`torchSpacingFloorMs`, positive-validated at construction) and reported in `status().torchSpacingFloorMs`; the knob verifiably drives the derivation (injected floor 60 on "I"@STANDARD@90 → `on@90, off@120, on@180, off@330` — 60 ms commanded dark).
- **Non-gating observation (recorded for a future hardening pass, NOT a defect introduced by attempt 3):** an exact-boundary tie — `pause()` called at EXACTLY an OFF edge's `tMs` while the wake timer at that same instant has not yet run (user/visibility handler wins the microsecond race) — yields a zero-remainder restore pair `on@P, off@P` (probed with an exclusive-boundary clock: "I"@BRISK@300 → `on@300, off@300`; "EE"@BRISK@100 → `on@100, off@100, on@280, off@380`). Assessment: measure-zero in real time (requires the position read to equal `offT` to the microsecond with the due timer unfired); no mark information is lost — the mark's ON and OFF lead commands were both already dispatched pre-pause (their dues precede `offT`) and the LED was already commanded dark, so this is a same-instant blip pair at a gap boundary, and the following stream still holds the floor (`on@133` next); both prior verification rounds canonized the dispatched-first ordering at exact edges (round 2's pinned "edge never re-fired" case reproduced byte-exact here) and the attempt-2 shaping produces the identical pair (catch-up OFF at its exact edge = P). If ever hardened: skip the restore when `events[resumeIdx].tMs === pausedAtMs` (a mark ending exactly at the freeze point has zero remainder to complete).
- **Evidence:** verifier probe battery — 53,298 assertions across the exact repros, the four 7 ms sweep families, ≈990 double-pause sequences, ≈290 takeover/paused-loss sequences, and the standing-invariants battery; 0 failures; probe scripts deleted after the run (zero repo residue).

## T-F7 — Share hash · 2026-08-28

- **Status:** `awaiting-approval` (plan.md updated).
- **Changed files:** `js/hash.js` (new, 338 lines), `test/hash.test.mjs` (new, 429 lines); doc updates for this entry's bookkeeping (plan.md status, state.md phase note, this log). No other source files touched; `harness.html` untouched (F7 has no UI).
- **What was built:** the plan's committed fragment format `#m=<base64url(UTF-8 message)>&s=<step id>` (unpadded RFC 4648 §5) as four exports plus one pure pair: `encode(message, stepId)` → hash string (leading `#` included; pure/total/stable — re-encoding a decode result is a fixed point); `decode(input)` → `{message, stepId}` accepting a hash string, a bare fragment, or a FULL URL (fragment = everything after the FIRST `#`, matching how browsers define `location.hash`); `readHash(loc)` — the on-load auto-fill helper (decode of `loc.hash`, or **null** when the fragment is absent entirely, `''`/`'#'`, so I2 can tell "no shared link" from "link with garbage"); `applyHash(hash, env)` — rewrites the fragment via `history.replaceState(pathname + search + '#' + fragment)` with `history.state` passed through by identity; `shareUrl(hash, loc)` — the copy-link helper returning the page URL with the fragment swapped. Speed-step ids are F2's vocabulary: `SPEED_STEP_ORDER`/`DEFAULT_STEP_ID` IMPORTED from `js/schedule.js`, never redeclared (membership via the ORDER array, so prototype keys like `constructor`/`toString` can never pass as step ids — pinned by test).
- **Output contract for consumers** (documented in the module header):
  - **decode NEVER THROWS; defaults are per part:** `m` missing / not base64url / not valid UTF-8 → message `''`; `s` missing / unknown / case-mismatch → `DEFAULT_STEP_ID` (LEARNER 5 — F2's photosafety default). A mangled link still opens a working app (empty composer, safe speed). `encode` throws TypeError only for wiring bugs (non-string message, unknown step — the encoder must never EMIT an unshareable hash; F3/F4/F5's sync-throw discipline).
  - **applyHash = replaceState, not navigation:** no history entry (Back never walks keystrokes), no scroll jump, **no hashchange event** — I2 re-renders from its own state and must NOT listen for hashchange. The share button copies `shareUrl(encode(message, stepId))`; the clipboard write stays in I2 (needs the gesture).
  - **DOM-optional:** every global (TextEncoder/TextDecoder/btoa/atob/location/history) resolves lazily behind a `typeof` guard; `encode`/`decode` never touch location/history at all (the whole suite runs in bare Node, where those globals don't exist — asserted). The location/history helpers take injectable stand-ins (`readHash({hash})`, `applyHash(hash, {history, location})`, `shareUrl(hash, {href})`); missing page globals → descriptive TypeError (wiring bug), except `decode`'s never-throw rule.
  - **Malformed-shape rules:** base64url charset `A-Za-z0-9_-` only (standard-base64 `+`/`/`, `%`-escapes, raw unicode → default message); impossible byte counts (length %4 === 1, i.e. truncated sequences) rejected; trailing `=` padding TOLERATED on decode (Python's `urlsafe_b64encode` pads) though never emitted; invalid UTF-8 bytes (lone 0xFF, truncated multi-byte, surrogate range) → default message via `TextDecoder('utf-8', {fatal:true})`; duplicate keys → first occurrence wins (mirrors `URLSearchParams.get`); stray unknown params ignored; wrong key (`#message=`) → default for that part.
- **UTF-8 path:** primary = `TextEncoder`/`TextDecoder` (global in Node ≥ 11 and every ES-module browser — the plan's plain-static stack). A compact fallback exists for pre-TextEncoder embeddings: encoder = encodeURIComponent's percent-escaped UTF-8 unrolled into bytes; decoder = manual length-scanning decoder with structural validation (truncation/stray-continuation → default message, never throws). Verified byte-identical to the primary path across the whole corpus (tests delete `globalThis.TextEncoder/TextDecoder` and re-run the round trips). One documented asymmetry: a LONE surrogate (invalid Unicode scalar, pathological input) becomes U+FFFD via TextEncoder but round-trips via the fallback — pinned only as "never throws" (asserted), not as a specific value.
- **Decisions made in F7** (within the plan's outcome, flagged for consumers):
  1. **encode output includes the leading `#`** — it is exactly what `applyHash`/`shareUrl` consume (both also accept a `#`-less fragment).
  2. **First-`#`-wins fragment extraction** — matches the browser's own fragment definition, so `decode(location.hash)` and `decode(location.href)` always agree (probed: `https://x.test/p?a=1#2#m=…` takes `2#m=…` as the fragment → defaults, same as a browser would hand over).
  3. **Manual fragment parsing, no URLSearchParams** — base64url and step ids never need fragment escaping, so values are taken verbatim and validated by charset/whitelist; no form-encoding surprises (`+` staying `+` matters: standard-base64 `+` must be REJECTED, not read as space).
  4. **`readHash` distinguishes null (no link) from defaults (garbage link)** — the task-briefed contract; I2 auto-fills only on non-null.
  5. **`shareUrl` added** (plan outcome names copy-link): pure URL construction from an injectable `{href}`; the clipboard write is I2's.
- **Validation evidence:** `node --test test/hash.test.mjs` → `ℹ tests 107 · pass 107 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, exit 0. Full suite `node --test 'test/*.test.mjs'` → `ℹ tests 438 · pass 438 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, **exit 0** (331 pre-existing all green — F1 74 + F2 38 + F4 30 + F5 32 + F3 43 + F8 49 + F6 65 — plus F7 107). `node --check` clean on module and test. Coverage (107 tests, 7 suites): encode — exact hand literals (`encode('SOS','STANDARD') === '#m=U09T&s=STANDARD'`, `'hi' → '#m=aGk&s=LEARNER'`, `'' → '#m=&s=LEARNER'`), **independent oracle: the `m` segment equals Node `Buffer.from(msg,'utf8').toString('base64url')` for the whole corpus × every step**, shape regex (unpadded URL-safe alphabet, fixed m-then-s param order), default step + `SPEED_STEPS` entry objects + explicit null/undefined, purity (repeat-call identity), wiring-bug throws (non-string message ×4; unknown step incl. `FAST`, bare `12`, case-mismatch `learner`, prototype keys `constructor`/`toString`, `{id:'FAST'}`); round trips — **22-message corpus × 3 steps deep-equal** (empty, SOS/PARIS/HI YOU/multi-word, the FULL 18-char ITU punctuation set `. , ? ' ! / ( ) & : ; = + - _ " $ @`, CJK `你好，摩尔斯电码`, Japanese, Cyrillic, RTL Arabic, emoji `👋`, `👋📡`, skin-tone `👍🏽`, ZWJ family `👨‍👩‍👧‍👦`, mixed `SOS 你好 👋 73!`, newline/tab/multi-space, leading/trailing whitespace, message content containing `+ = /`, quotes, the 44-char pangram+digits, and a 10,000-char message), full-URL decode (https with query, `file://`, localhost), bare-fragment acceptance, idempotence (re-encode = fixed point, 12 messages), fresh-object output shape; fallbacks — unknown step (5 shapes, message preserved), missing parts (per-part defaults ×5), malformed base64url (truncated ×2, mid-`=`, standard `+` and `/`, percent-escapes, raw-unicode, whitespace/braces), **invalid UTF-8 byte sequences** (0xFF, truncated 3-byte, surrogate range ED A0 80), padding tolerance (`aGk=` → `hi`), wrong/stray params (`#x=`, `#message=`, trailing/leading/bare strays, only-strays), duplicate keys first-wins (m ×2, s ×2), URL forms without a usable fragment (no `#`, `#section`, `#/m=…`, `###`), non-string inputs (undefined/null/number/object/array/Symbol/boolean), lone-surrogate never-throws, every nasty input still names a known step id; readHash — `''`/`'#'` → null, present → decode result, garbage → defaults non-null, unicode round trip, `{}` → null, bare-Node no-`location` descriptive throw; applyHash — replaceState exactly once with `pathname+search+fragment` and `title ''`, **pushState count 0 and `location.hash` setter spy 0** (no history pollution / no scroll-jump path / no hashchange by construction), `history.state` identity passthrough, `#`-optional + empty-search variants, non-string TypeError, no-page-globals descriptive throw; shareUrl — fragment replacement keeping scheme/host/path/search, append-when-absent, `#`-optional, non-string TypeError, no-location throw; environment independence — **fallback codecs: whole corpus round-trips byte-identically with `globalThis.TextEncoder/TextDecoder` deleted** (restored in `finally`), fallback still rejects invalid UTF-8 ×3 shapes, encode/decode proven location/history-free in bare Node, **source-hygiene scan** (comment-stripped `js/hash.js` has zero `window/document/navigator/localStorage/sessionStorage/querySelector/getElementById/innerHTML/addEventListener`), and step-vocabulary proven imported (decode accepts exactly `SPEED_STEP_ORDER`, rejects `LEARNERX`/`standard`/`''`/`FAST`/`'5'`/`'8'`/`'12'`).
- **Adversarial probes beyond the suite** (scratch script, deleted after the run — zero repo residue): chunk-boundary messages at 32,767 / 32,768 / 32,769 / 98,304 / 100,000 bytes round-trip exactly (the 0x8000 binary-string chunking path); a 35,000-char unicode-dense message (`'你好 👋 SOS '.repeat(5000)`) round-trips; `btoa` deleted from globals → `encode` throws the documented TypeError naming btoa, `atob` deleted → `decode` falls back to defaults without throwing; `aGk==` (double padding) still decodes `hi`; first-`#`-wins confirmed live (`…#2#m=U09T` → defaults, matching what a browser's `location.hash` would hand over).
- **Headless limits (honest):** real-browser `history.replaceState` behavior (no entry / no scroll / no hashchange on an actual page) and clipboard copy are not observable in Node — they are guaranteed by the API contract replaceState is NOT a navigation, and the suite pins that only `replaceState` is touched (pushState 0, `location.hash` setter 0). The device-level confirmation is Q3's script; the wiring is I2's (auto-fill via `readHash`, update via `applyHash(encode(...))` throttled with typing, share via `shareUrl` + `navigator.clipboard` inside the gesture).
- **Deviations:** none from plan (format, parse-on-load, replaceState, copy-link all exactly as committed). Three scope notes: (a) `shareUrl` exported alongside the briefed encode/decode/readHash/apply because the plan's F7 outcome line names "copy-link" (the clipboard write itself stays in I2); (b) trailing-`=` padding tolerance on decode is an F7-owned leniency (documented above) — strict unpadded is what `encode` emits and what the shape test enforces; (c) the fallback UTF-8 codecs exist because the task brief asked for a Node+browser-compatible fallback path; both runtimes in play have TextEncoder, so they are belt-and-braces, but they are FULLY tested via global deletion, not dead code.
- **Delegation record:** none — task executed directly by the production worker. Research consumed: none (F7's deps are F1 + the plan's committed hash format). Consumer inputs: F2's T-entry (`SPEED_STEPS`/`SPEED_STEP_ORDER`/`DEFAULT_STEP_ID` as the single source of step ids) and F1's T-entry (message semantics — any string in, unknowns flagged downstream by `translate`, never dropped; F7 preserves message text byte-exactly and leaves translatability to F1).

- **Status:** PASS → F7 set to `completed` in plan.md; `auto-approved (ultron-supreme)` recorded in state.md. Verifier did not author F7; all checks re-run from scratch (Node 24.8.0) with a verifier-OWNED oracle, not the suite's `Buffer` oracle.

- **Verifier's own oracle round trips:** a hand-rolled base64url decoder (bit-level RFC 4648 §5 reverse map, padding stripped, dangling-symbol rejection) plus a hand-rolled strict UTF-8 decoder — zero use of `Buffer`, TextEncoder/TextDecoder, or module internals. 5 verifier-chosen messages × all 3 step ids (hand-coded `LEARNER/STANDARD/BRISK`, not read from schedule.js): plain ASCII `CQ CQ DX DE VERIFIER`; ITU punctuation colliders `AT&T COST=73 & ?MORE=!` (`&` `=` `?` never leak into the payload — the emitted `m` segment matches `/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]*$/`, so query-syntax collisions are structurally impossible); emoji with surrogate pairs incl. skin-tone 👍🏽 and flag 🇯🇵; an exact 500-char message; mixed CJK/Arabic/emoji/`&`/`=`/tabs/newlines. For every combination: the oracle recovers the message EXACTLY from `encode`'s payload; `decode` round-trips the hash string, the bare fragment, AND the full URL `https://v.test/app?x=1&y=%3D#m=…&s=…` identically; `encode(decode(h)) === h` (idempotent fixed point) and repeated `encode` calls byte-identical (pure). 120/120 round-trip assertions.
- **6 malformed inputs of the verifier's own invention (distinct from the suite's battery):** `#M=U09T&s=BRISK` (case-mismatched KEY — m defaults, s preserved); `https://x.test/p?x=<script>#m=U09T&s=BRISK#frag2` (script in the query + second `#` — first-# fragment semantics make `s` = `BRISK#frag2` → unknown → LEARNER while message `SOS` survives); `#m=//////&s=BRISK` (full-length standard-base64 `/` alphabet → default message); `#m=` + `A`×10001 (impossible length %4===1 at scale → default); `#s=BRISK&m=%3Cscript%3E…` (percent-escaped injection, params reversed → default message, step preserved); `#m=U09T&s=BRISK&m=<img onerror>` (duplicate `m` with HTML injection second — FIRST wins → `SOS`). **None threw; every fallback matched the verifier's hand-derived expectation exactly (per-part defaults, valid parts preserved).**
- **Code audit (confirmed in source, not via tests):** step vocabulary is genuinely imported — line 47 `import { SPEED_STEP_ORDER, DEFAULT_STEP_ID } from './schedule.js'` is the ONLY source; grep finds no local redeclaration (`LEARNER/STANDARD/BRISK` literals appear only in comments), and membership via `SPEED_STEP_ORDER.includes()` keeps prototype keys out. `decode` accepts hash string / bare fragment / full URL (`fragmentOf` = everything after the FIRST `#`, whole input when no `#`). `readHash` returns null only for absent fragments (`''`, `#`, missing `hash` property) and a defaults object for garbage. `applyHash` (js/hash.js:298–306) performs exactly one navigation op — `env.history.replaceState(env.history.state, '', pathname+search+fragment)`; the file contains ZERO `pushState` calls and ZERO `location.hash` assignments (grep: all other `window`/`document`/`replaceState` matches are comments). Proven live on the verifier's own Proxy-recording location stub: one `replaceState(state-identity, '', '/p.html?a=1#m=U09T&s=STANDARD')`, the Proxy's set trap NEVER fired (no location write → no hashchange/scroll path), no pushState/go/back/forward. No DOM globals at module top level: `TextEncoder/TextDecoder/btoa/atob/location/history` all resolve lazily behind `typeof` guards inside function bodies (module top level = import, one regex const, function declarations); the module imported and operated in bare Node where none of those globals exist.
- **Injection safety (for I2's DOM insertion):** `decode` returns a plain `Object` (prototype = `Object.prototype`) with EXACTLY two own keys `message, stepId`, both primitive strings — no object/HTML smuggling is representable. Probed with `<img src=x onerror="alert(1)"><script>alert(1)</script>&=<iframe>`: survives as the exact plain string, flags none. I2 must still insert it as text (textContent), which the plain-string contract makes natural.
- **Scope:** full tree = pre-existing artifacts + the two declared F7 files (`js/hash.js`, `test/hash.test.mjs` — the only source files in the task's mtime window, 04:29–04:32) + the three bookkeeping docs (plan.md, state.md, this log). `harness.html` untouched (no hash.js reference; mtime predates F7 work — F7 has no UI, as claimed). Only consumer of `js/hash.js` is the test. Verifier probe script deleted after the run (zero repo residue).
