## T-U1 — Copy pack · 2026-08-28

- **Status:** `awaiting-approval` (plan.md updated).
- **Changed files:** `js/copy.js` (new), `test/copy.test.mjs` (new); doc updates for this entry's bookkeeping (plan.md status, state.md phase note, this log). No other source files touched — `harness.html` deliberately not extended (U1 is data-only; the harness is Q3's instrument and stays leaf-disposable).
- **What was built:** `js/copy.js` — EVERY user-facing string of the instrument in one reviewable, deep-frozen, data-only module: `COPY` (frozen tree of stable keys) + four tiny pure helpers for parameterized lines (`speedLine(stepId)`, `progressLine(sent, total)`, `unknownCharsLine(chars)`, `fallbackLine(reason)` — the last never returns undefined, unknown reasons get the generic line). Zero DOM access (source-scan test, the F4–F8 hygiene stance); imports are vocabulary/numbers only: `SPEED_STEPS/SPEED_STEP_ORDER/WPM_HARD_CAP/PHOTOSAFETY_THRESHOLD_HZ/flashGuard` from js/schedule.js, `TORCH_FALLBACK_REASONS` from js/torch.js, `STATES/ARM_FRAMINGS` from js/states.js — so the pack loads anywhere those DOM-free modules do (browser or bare Node).
- **The register voice, as enforced laws** (test-pinned, not just prose): telegraphic machine-plate lines (uppercase legends/printed-state lines, sentence-case for longer explanatory statements), **no exclamation mark anywhere in the pack**, no apology tokens (sorry/unfortunate/apolog), no marketing hype (amazing/awesome/wow/magic), screen mode framed as a first-class light source ("THE SCREEN IS THE LAMP. HOLD IT UP FACING THE ROOM." — no consolation), **no string over 120 characters** (walked over every leaf of the tree).
- **What the pack contains** (keys stable for I1/I2):
  - `instrument` — name plate (title THE REGISTER / subtitle / ITU foot legend).
  - `input` — composer label, placeholder, empty-message line, unknown-char lead + note (F1 semantics: flagged, never dropped, "stay on the tape, unspoken").
  - `transport` — all control keys incl. the manual channel-switch control + its R1 §5.6 hint ("LAMP NOT FLASHING? THE SCREEN CAN TAKE OVER MID-MESSAGE.").
  - `speed`/`steps` — legend, ceiling note (`CEILING 12 WPM. NO FASTER STEP EXISTS.`, 12 derived from `WPM_HARD_CAP`), and per-step `{label, description, caution}` for LEARNER 5 / STANDARD 8 / BRISK 12 (committed R2 values). **BRISK alone carries the rapid-flash caution** ("RAPID FLASH: REPEATED DOTS REACH 5.0 PER SECOND — ABOVE THE 3-HERTZ SAFETY LINE." — both numbers derived via `flashGuard`, never retyped); LEARNER's description names it the default and states its flashes stay under 3 per second; STANDARD's honestly names its 3.3-per-second rate (F2's guard flags STANDARD above-threshold too — the copy follows the numbers).
  - `notice` — **first-visit vs returning distinction** (states.js's `hazardAcknowledged`/`noticePending`): first run leads with the hazard plate; later preflight entries get the short re-arm beat. Distinct strings, test-pinned.
  - `hazard` — the photosensitivity plate: title, **pictogram alt text**, **one imperative line** ("DO NOT WATCH IF FLASHING LIGHT HAS EVER TRIGGERED A SEIZURE."), advisory (who should not watch: anyone photosensitive, lamp or screen), the **speed steps by name and WPM** (derived), the honest per-step flash rates 2.1/3.3/5.0 per second (derived from `flashGuard`), the 3-per-second safety line (derived from `PHOTOSAFETY_THRESHOLD_HZ`), the default-stays-below note, ACKNOWLEDGE/decline keys.
  - `whyCamera` — the pre-permission explainer (rendered BEFORE the browser camera prompt; `framings.why-camera` + `states.awaiting-permission` carry the sequencing). **The two honesty facts survive verbatim and are test-pinned:** "Browsers expose the torch only through a live camera stream." and "No video is recorded. No video is sent anywhere." — plus the release fact ("The camera closes the moment the broadcast ends.").
  - `states` — `{legend, line}` for **all 15 F8 states** (broadcasting-on-screen and paused-on-screen distinct; unsupported's line positive: "NO LAMP TO DRIVE. THE SCREEN CARRIES THE SIGNAL.").
  - `denied` — the denied block with the **screen OFFER** ("BROADCAST BY SCREEN LIGHT" + offer line, no dead end), retry key, and a regrant hint pointing at browser site settings.
  - `screenMode`/`framings` — positive screen framing + one line per `ARM_FRAMINGS` value (total against `ARM_MATRIX`, test-enumerated).
  - `fallback` — one machine line per **all 10 `TORCH_FALLBACK_REASONS`** values (permission-denied included) + `generic` for future/unknown reasons.
  - `choreography` — the standby → caution → GO beats (`{legend, line}` each).
  - `broadcast` — channel status lines + the takeover notes: **"LIGHT SOURCE LOST — CONTINUING ON SCREEN."** (the committed wording), the manual-switch variant, and "POSITION KEPT. THE MESSAGE LOSES NOTHING."
  - `done` (by doneReason complete/stopped) + offer line; `error` (arm/broadcast/internal + recovery: the message survives); `share` (copied/copy-failed/what-a-link-carries — "No server, no record."); `shared` (hash auto-fill: loaded vs unreadable — F7's readHash null-vs-garbage distinction).
- **Completeness is structural, not aspirational:** `COPY.states` keys use the `STATES` constants, `COPY.fallback` keys use `TORCH_FALLBACK_REASONS` constants, and the suite enumerates the LIVE exports — `STATE_LIST`, every `ARM_MATRIX` cell (framing copy + the offerScreen cell), every fallback reason, every speed step — asserting exact key-set equality and string-ness, so a future state/reason/step CANNOT ship copyless and a typo'd key cannot pass as coverage.
- **Validation evidence:** `node --test test/copy.test.mjs` → `ℹ tests 25 · pass 25 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, exit 0. Full suite `node --test 'test/*.test.mjs'` → `ℹ tests 463 · pass 463 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, **exit 0** (438 pre-existing all green — F1 74 + F2 38 + F4 30 + F5 32 + F3 43 + F8 49 + F6 65 + F7 107 — plus U1 25; 438+25 = 463). `node --check` clean on module and test. Coverage (25 tests): deep-frozen at every level + leaf-type walk (sole legal nulls: LEARNER/STANDARD cautions); state completeness vs `STATE_LIST` (key-set equality, legend+line strings, no undefined access); doneReason/error-kind/takeover-cause carriers; ARM_MATRIX framing totality + the denied offer; fallback completeness vs `TORCH_FALLBACK_REASONS` (key-set equality incl. the generic) + `fallbackLine` never-undefined; speed-step completeness vs `SPEED_STEP_ORDER` with WPM values asserted against `SPEED_STEPS` and flash rates against `flashGuard` (derived, not trusted); the BRISK-only caution + threshold naming + LEARNER-default mention; the 120-char plate-line cap over every string; the voice laws (no `!`, no apology, no hype); the hazard imperative+advisory+steps+rates content (each rate re-derived from `flashGuard`); the why-camera facts (camera-stream fact + NO VIDEO IS RECORDED + NO VIDEO IS SENT, verbatim, case-insensitive); first-visit ≠ returning; screen-positive framing (hold-it-up energy on three surfaces); the takeover committed wording; choreography beats; block-presence sweep (instrument/input/transport/share/shared); helper formatting + wiring-bug TypeErrors (unknown step, out-of-range/float progress, malformed char arrays); data-only source scan (10 DOM/timer tokens, comment-stripped).

## T-Q1 — Unit suite complete · 2026-08-28

- **Status:** `awaiting-approval` (plan.md updated).
- **Changed files:** `package.json` (new, 10 lines), `test/suite-integrity.test.mjs` (new, 3 tests); doc updates for this entry's bookkeeping (plan.md status, state.md phase note, this log). No other source files touched; all nine logic suites and `harness.html` byte-identical to their own tasks' entries.
- **What was built:** the single CI-runnable command. `package.json` is scripts-only and dependency-free by construction — `private: true`, `type: module` (the js/ modules are ESM), NO dependencies/devDependencies/peerDependencies/optionalDependencies (verified), and exactly one script: `"test": "node --test 'test/*.test.mjs'"` — the quoted-glob form, because the plan's literal validate line (`node --test test/`) fails to resolve the bare directory on this repo's Node 24.8.0 (the T-F2 note; re-confirmed live during this task: exit 1). Quoting keeps glob expansion inside the runner's own matcher, which is the form every task since T-F2 has actually validated against (217/266/309/320/331/438/463).
- **Test-integrity guard** (`test/suite-integrity.test.mjs`, 3 tests): the silent-skip failure mode of a glob-driven suite is a test file that never runs and never fails. Three independent guards, each proven non-vacuous by mutation during this task:
  1. **Script/shape guard** — package.json's test script normalizes (shell quotes stripped) to exactly `node --test test/*.test.mjs`, and the artifact stays private + ESM + dependency-free, so the command cannot regress to the broken directory form or become a dependency-introducing artifact. Mutation check: script changed to `node --test test/` → this guard fails (exit 1).
  2. **Stray-file guard** — a recursive walk of test/ fails loudly on any test-LIKE file (`*.test.js|mjs|cjs`, `*.spec.*`) NOT matched by the glob (i.e. not a direct `test/*.test.mjs` child — the glob's `*` does not cross `/`), catching subdirectory or extension drift. Mutation check: `test/subdir/foo.test.mjs` planted → this guard fails naming the file (exit 1).
  3. **Live-discovery guard** — spawns the REAL runner (`node --test --test-name-pattern <never-matches> --test-reporter=tap 'test/*.test.mjs'`): test bodies are filtered out so only module loads + per-file reporting happen (~1 s), then the TAP top-level `# Subtest:` file list is deep-equaled against the on-disk `*.test.mjs` set — same count, same names, this file included. The meta-test executing at all is itself proof for one file; this guard extends it to every file, and exercises the runner's own glob engine so a Node upgrade that changes discovery semantics (exactly the T-F2 class) cannot pass silently. Mutation check: glob swapped to the `test/` form → guard fails (probe exit nonzero; a hypothetically-silent empty discovery is backstopped by the deepEqual against the non-empty on-disk set).
  - **Two traps found and handled while building guard 3** (recorded so verifiers don't re-trip): (a) the runner marks its file-children with env `NODE_TEST_CONTEXT=child-v8` — a spawned `node --test` inheriting it detects recursion, prints "skipping running files", and exits 0 with EMPTY output (a fake-pass!); the probe strips the variable from the child env and asserts the recursion warning never appears. (b) `fileURLToPath(new URL('.', import.meta.url))` keeps a trailing slash, breaking `path.dirname` equality in the stray-file walk — fixed by deriving TEST_DIR via `path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url))`.
  - Recursion is double-guarded: the never-matching `--test-name-pattern` means the probe child executes zero test bodies (including this file's), and an independent env guard (`MORSE_SUITE_INTEGRITY_PROBE=1`) skips the spawn test if it ever ran there anyway.
- **Flakiness investigation:** none found — the full suite is deterministic. 4 baseline consecutive runs of `node --test 'test/*.test.mjs'` plus 5 more of `npm test` (post-meta-test): every run 463/463 then 466/466, fail 0, cancelled 0, skipped 0, exit 0. Conductor and audio suites are already fully fake-clock/stub-driven (per T-F6/T-F5); no real timers anywhere in test/ — no stabilization changes were needed (and none made).
- **Validation evidence (final counts per suite, each run individually, exit 0):** morse 74 · schedule 38 · screen 30 · audio 32 · torch 43 · states 49 · conductor 65 · hash 107 · copy 25 (= 463, matching U1's committed baseline exactly) + suite-integrity 3 = **466 total**. `npm test` → `ℹ tests 466 · pass 466 · fail 0 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0`, **exit 0**; `node --test 'test/*.test.mjs'` → identical 466/466, exit 0 (~6.6 s either way; the discovery probe adds <0.1 s). No `node_modules/`, no `package-lock.json` (nothing to install — `npm test` on a scripts-only manifest runs no install step). `node --check` clean on the meta-test.
- **Deviations:** one, documented in plan.md's Q1 entry — the validate line's directory form (`node --test test/`) does not resolve on Node 24.8.0, so the committed single command is the T-F2-noted glob form; the plan's AC intent ("exits 0, one command") is met by `npm test`. The meta-test's 3 tests are an intentional addition on top of the 463 (count only grew), per the task brief.
- **Delegation record:** none — task executed directly by the production worker. Inputs consumed: T-F2's Node 24.8.0 directory-resolution note; the per-suite committed counts from T-F1 through T-U1 entries (re-derived live, not trusted: every suite re-run individually this task).

- **What was built:** the committed Register world as a fully static instrument. `index.html` carries the direction contract comment as the FIRST CHILD OF BODY (THESIS / OWN-WORLD / STORY / FIRST VIEWPORT / FORM — punched-tape telegraph register, position 4 of 7, seed key 53cae9af, grep-verified in the emitted markup — / FINISH line verbatim), then the machine: nameplate, MESSAGE composer, the full-bleed paper tape band as hero (punch-hole dots 14px / dashes 42px showing the ground through the paper, per-letter printed characters, sprocket feed holes, fixed read-head bridge at center — the tape moves, not the head), the machine-dialogue status station (aria-live), standby/caution/GO choreography zones, contextual plates (hazard / why-camera / denied+screen-offer / screen-framing), transport key rows, named speed steps (native radio detents — functional pre-I2 via `:checked`), share row, footplate. `css/main.css` (≈1000 lines) carries the tokens (dark warm ground #171008, paper #e2d3a8, ONE lamp accent #ffb454 + derived dims, Saira Stencil One legends / Barlow plates / Spline Sans Mono tape data via the one permitted Google Fonts request, system-adjacent fallbacks) and the STATE BLOCK: `body[data-state="…"]` renders **all 15 F8 states** as distinct complete static snapshots — per-state status line (verbatim `COPY.states`), strip shift (which letter sits under the head: idle→H, broadcasting→A @7/38, on-screen→K @9/38, paused→R @8/38, error→D @6/38, done→M all-spoken), spoken/live group styling, lamp modes (off/dim/lit+halo), key-row mounting, plate visibility, choreography-zone lighting, progress counters, takeover note.
- **Fidelity mechanism (translate()-true statics):** the tape markup, group positions (px, absolute — no layout drift), and every mock number were GENERATED from the real modules (`node /tmp/i1-gen/gen.mjs`, since deleted): `translate('HELLO DARK ROOM')` → 13 letter groups, 38 marks, word gaps 58px / letter gaps 26px / symbol gaps 10px; progress lines = `progressLine` semantics against the real mark count; every user-facing string rendered verbatim from `js/copy.js` (zero new prose — two invented micro-labels caught and removed during the build; aria-labels reuse copy strings only). Unknown-char mechanism ships (`.tg-unknown` dashed void slot + `COPY.input` flag lines, hidden until I2 drives content).
- **Raises honored as laws:** capability-layer glow law — every glow/halo rides a custom property (`--lamp-halo/--zone-halo/--punch-glow`) that flattens to crisp static under `prefers-reduced-motion` (the lit ring carries the information, the blur is the layer); named speed steps law — three detent keys, never a slider; corridor-focus law — the message's own letters at full legibility on the tape (no alphabet chart shrunk to a miniature; none needed on this surface).
- **Validation evidence (30 captures in `.impeccable/review/`, all opened and judged):** all 15 states @390×844 (full content height), the six named states (idle / torch-ready / preflight / broadcasting / broadcasting-on-screen / permission-denied) also @768×1024 and @1280×800, plus idle/preflight/broadcasting @320 (320px does not break; steps stack 1-col, no overflow). Rendered via headless Chrome/CDP against a served origin (`python3 -m http.server 8123`) with a temporary content-fitted iframe probe (`i1-probe.html`, same-origin `data-state` injection — DELETED after validation; zero repo residue). Each capture was vision-inspected and cross-checked against DOM ground truth (CDP evaluate) where the analyzers disagreed with the CSS: three analyzer claims were disproven by DOM measurement (390 "two-column" hallucination, "empty region", letter/hole misalignment — charCx === marksCx === headCx = 195/195/195), and TWO real defects were found and fixed in one batch, then re-verified in DOM + fresh captures: (1) plate key rows (`#hazard/#why-camera/#denied .keys`) inherited `.keys{display:none}` and were invisible in their states — now mounted with the plate; (2) at ≥900px with share hidden (live corridor states) the speed bay left the right deck column empty — speed now spans full width unless share is mounted beside it. Also tightened: head cap raised off the printed-letter row (cap bottom 263 vs char top 271, 8px clear), status sentence wraps as its own printed line under the legend at narrow widths.

One warning: "side-tab accent border — `.tape-window` inset box-shadow 3px stripe (bottom)". Assessed and PASSED TO THE REVIEWER rather than mechanically changed: the flagged shadow is the paper band's symmetric material edge (top highlight + bottom shade of the SAME paper hue, both inset) — physical tape shading, not a one-side colored accent on a card; the craft floor bans left/right color accents on cards, which this is not.

- **FAIL — criterion "320px no-break": corridor states break below 385px.** `#corridor` uses `repeat(3, 1fr)` but `.zone` grid items keep `min-width:auto`, so the tracks cannot shrink below min-content (zones measure 117/113/93px at EVERY viewport). Measured with real viewports: at 320 the corridor + deck track is forced to 385px — `documentElement.scrollWidth` 385 > viewport 320, `body{overflow-x:clip}` hard-cuts ~65px: the GO card shows 41px of 93 (it is the LIT card mid-broadcast), the MESSAGE input's right border, the tape band's right end (window right = 360 > 320), transport and speed bay are clipped, and the read-head sits ~32px off viewport center. At 360/375 the clip is 25/10px; at 390 corridor states still overflow (scrollWidth 395; tape-window right = 395; deck right padding lost — bays end at 385 instead of 374). Confirmed visually in the verifier's `v-broadcasting-320.png` AND in the worker's own `.impeccable/review/i1-broadcasting-320.png` (same clipping). **The T-I1 claim "320px does not break; steps stack 1-col, no overflow" is therefore false as evidence** — root cause of the miss: the content-fitted-iframe probe sizes the frame to the content, so it can never observe viewport overflow; all width-labeled captures below 385px are effectively mislabeled. Fix direction (for the worker): `grid-template-columns: repeat(3, minmax(0,1fr))` on `#corridor` (or equivalent shrink), then re-verify at 320/360/375/390 with an IMPOSED viewport, not a fitted one.
- **FINDING (minor) — copy drift, one comma:** `index.html` hazard plate reads "Repeated dots flash 2.1, 3.3, and 5.0 times per second…" but `COPY.hazard.rates` renders "2.1, 3.3 and 5.0" (the RATE_LIST join has no Oxford comma). The "every string verbatim from copy.js" claim does not hold for this line; it will silently change when I2 wires live strings. Align (either the static text or the join).
- **OBSERVATION — mock progress numbers:** the static counters use the GROUP index (MARK 7/9/8/6 OF 38) while the depicted head positions imply 19/26/22/16 MARKS spoken. Acceptable as static placeholders (I2 drives `progressLine` live), but "progressLine semantics against the real mark count" only holds for the total (38).
- **Disposition:** I1 → `in-progress`. Unmet criteria to close: (1) the ≤385px corridor overflow fix + re-verification at imposed widths; (2) the `hazard.rates` comma alignment. Everything else verified green by this pass and need not be re-proven.

## T-I2 — Wire the instrument (js/app.js) · 2026-08-28

- **Status:** `awaiting-approval` (plan.md updated).
- **Changed files:** `js/app.js` (new, ~560 lines — the only new JS file), `index.html` (wiring edits: `data-action` hooks on every control + the `<script type="module">` include), `css/main.css` (three wiring-scoped edits: the I1 static per-state tape overrides replaced by the live `.tg-spoken`/`.tg-live` class rules + paused/error soft-ring variants; the `body[data-state="ready"][data-beat="caution"]` choreography block; `.message-input:disabled`), `harness.html` **DELETED** (per plan — index.html now runs end-to-end; grep confirms zero references in shipped files), `.impeccable/review/i2-*.png` (14 validation captures @390). No logic module touched; no test touched.
- **What was built:** the DOM glue that makes The Register an instrument. One `createTorchChannel`/`createScreenChannel`/`createAudioChannel`/`createConductor`/`createStateMachine` per page; every decision delegated — the machine owns state, copy.js owns every string, the conductor owns timing, F7 owns the URL. The journey wired exactly per the F8 contract: BROADCAST → preflight (hazard plate on first visit — `notice-required` — re-arm beat on same-session returns) → ACK → arming → why-camera plate (`awaiting-permission`, via the T-F8 prompt-expectation heuristic: `torch.status().supported` + `permissions.query({name:'camera'})` granted/denied → skip; anything else → show why-camera BEFORE the prompt) → `torch.acquire()` always inside a click → `eventFromTorchResult()` → torch-ready / permission-denied (screen offer) / unsupported (screen-positive) / error; screen arms via ACCEPT_SCREEN. **The test flash:** one dot fired on the lamp at torch-ready entry (on → `dotMsOf(step)` → off, 300 ms after the probe's own blink) so the user can verify the light before the message. **The choreography:** GO from the armed standby dispatches GO → `ready`, where standby (650 ms) → caution (650 ms, `body[data-beat]`) auto-fires the second GO → broadcasting + `conductor.start()`; CANCEL is the mounted escape. Progress events drive the read-head traverse (`--strip-shift` = live group center inline on body, `.tg-spoken`/`.tg-live` classes, `progressLine`+`speedLine` in the status row); takeover (`TORCH_LOST` from the conductor, manual `SWITCH_CHANNEL` cause-tagged) prints the copy.js takeover line; pause/resume/stop/replay per machine events; done → REPLAY (torch released at done → preflight re-arm; screen held → straight to the choreography) + SHARE.
- **Laws held (validated, not claimed):**
  - **Live tape:** typing re-punches `translate().letters` as per-letter groups (unknown chars = flagged void groups, `unknownCharsLine` flag text, empty-tape note) via `requestAnimationFrame` coalescing — the input handler itself does no DOM work; geometry reproduces I1's static markup byte-for-byte (dot 14 / dash 42 / symbol gap 10 / letter 26 / word 58, lead/trail 44 — verified: 'HELLO DARK ROOM' → 13 groups at the exact static lefts/widths, strip 1778 px). The tape FREEZES at `ready` (composer disabled in the five committed states) so head indices always match the schedule being spoken.
  - **Speed + hash:** the three radio detents wire `SPEED_STEP_ORDER` ids; message+step persist ONLY in the URL — `applyHash(encode(...))` throttled 250 ms behind typing (replaceState only; zero pushState, zero hashchange listening — F7's contract); on load `readHash()` auto-fills (shared-link 2-tap journey: message present → GO immediately available).
  - **Privacy/gesture:** zero `getUserMedia` in app code (torch.acquire inside the click chain is the only door — grep-verified); audio `unlock()` fires inside the GO gesture (the conductor's own unlock is then an idempotent check); visibilitychange pause arrives through the conductor's document port → `onPause` → `PAUSE`; **no storage API anywhere** (grep 0), memory-only mute; **no console output** (grep 0 — and the walkthrough's CDP console capture stayed empty across all three scenarios).
  - **The 15 states:** `body[data-state]` set from the machine on every change; every rendered string traced to copy.js (first-visit vs returning gate, takeover cause wording, denied offer, screen-positive, shared loaded/unreadable); `release-torch` effect → `torch.release()` (verified: track-stop count ≥1 at done, 0 while armed).
- **CSS wiring deltas (I1's world preserved):** the static per-state tape rules (`--strip-shift` px values + `:nth-child`/`data-g` stylings for the demo message) are REPLACED by live-class rules — app.js owns the shift/classes now, and the old `data-g="7"`-style selectors would have lit wrong groups under live content; the base `.tg-spoken`/`.tg-live` rules (I1 lines 291–304) carry the inks unchanged, paused/error keep I1's softer 0.45 ring via new state-scoped variants, broadcasting keeps the 0.7 ring + punch glow. Nothing else in the visual world changed (all 12 sweep measurements overflow-clean; corridor/plates/keys untouched).
- **Validation evidence (all re-runnable from the T-I2 rig class: local `http.server` + headless Chrome 152/CDP with `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` IMPOSED viewports + a pre-navigation fake injected via `Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument` — a canvas-captureStream track with `getCapabilities(){torch:true}` and a recording `applyConstraints` — zero shipped debug hooks; rig scripts live in /tmp, zero repo residue):**
  - **`npm test` 466/466 exit 0** before and after (app.js is DOM glue; no new tests — count unchanged per the task's allowance).
  - **Fake-torch walkthrough 76/76 checks green** across three scenarios (torch / deny / no-media). Torch scenario @390: cold open idle with the prefilled demo punched (13 groups) and ZERO camera access at load; typing "SOS #" → 4 groups incl. the flagged void + "NO MORSE SIGN FOR" line, retype "SOS" → clean; hash `#m=U09T&s=LEARNER` with replaceState>0, pushState=0; BROADCAST → preflight (FIRST RUN beat) → ACK → why-camera (privacy facts verbatim, still zero gUM) → CONTINUE → torch-ready with the probe's both-direction applies + the TEST DOT (`on/off ×2` at +300 ms) and the track held (stops=0); GO → ready (STANDBY beat → CAUTION beat) → auto-GO → broadcasting with "MARK 1→3 OF 9 · LEARNER — 5 WPM" advancing, strip shift 75→205 px, hot-path torch applies growing, 4 oscillators started (audio scheduled on the master clock), composer frozen; PAUSE → paused → RESUME → broadcasting; clean completion → done (all 3 groups spoken, head on the last letter, REPLAY+SHARE mounted, track stopped); same-session REPLAY → preflight re-arm with the RETURNING beat (no hazard plate again); re-arm → torch-ready; second run with the fake's apply rejections → TORCH_LOST → broadcasting-on-screen, takeover note verbatim, screen overlay lit/dark transitions observed in-DOM, completion → done. SHARE: clipboard write verified by read-back (`http://127.0.0.1:8111/index.html#m=U09T&s=LEARNER`); fresh TRUE page load (query-bumped — a fragment-only navigate is same-document) auto-fills message+step, prints "MESSAGE AND SPEED LOADED FROM THE LINK.", GO immediately available; a FRESH session shows the hazard notice again (no persistence — by scope); CANCEL escapes the choreography. **Deny scenario:** fake NotAllowedError → permission-denied with the screen-offer plate (no dead end) → ACCEPT_SCREEN → screen-mode → GO → broadcasting-on-screen with overlay lit/dark observed → done. **No-media scenario:** `navigator.mediaDevices` removed → ACK lands straight in unsupported (why-camera NEVER shown — no prompt class), screen-positive line, BROADCAST arms the screen, E @ LEARNER runs to done.
  - **Overflow:** `documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth === viewport` at **320/390/768/1280** for idle, broadcasting, and done (12/12 measurements, real imposed viewports on the served page).
  - **Zero console noise:** CDP `Runtime.consoleAPICalled` + `exceptionThrown` captured across all three runs — 0 events.
  - **14 captures** in `.impeccable/review/` (all vision-inspected): idle, typing-unknown, preflight, awaiting-permission, torch-ready, broadcasting, paused, done, takeover-flash (the takeover moment is a full-screen WHITE frame — the screen light literally speaking mid-mark; the screen channel covers the viewport by design, so the state's DOM assertions + the done capture carry the state evidence), share-outcome, shared-autofill, shared-loaded-line (scrolled), permission-denied, unsupported — all @390×844.
- **Detector:** run once on the changed targets (`index.html css/main.css`, regex-degraded mode as in T-I1): the same single pre-assessed warning — `.tape-window`'s inset bottom stripe = the paper band's symmetric material shading (top highlight + bottom shade of the same paper hue), untouched by I2 and already passed to the I5 reviewer with rationale in T-I1.
- **Deviations:** none from plan. Four conscious wiring notes for the record: (a) the ready-state choreography auto-fires GO after the two beats — the machine's own design ("COMMITTED. STANDBY, CAUTION, THEN GO." with CANCEL mounted as the escape), which keeps one user gesture per journey; the conductor start rides the beat timer (~1.3 s inside Chrome's transient-activation window, and F4/F5 degrade silently where an engine disagrees); (b) the test flash fires on the TORCH channel at torch-ready (the probe's blink already exists but is not guaranteed physical — the deliberate dot is); screen arming needs no test flash (the screen is self-evidently visible); (c) `PERMISSION_GRANTED` is deliberately unused — every TORCH_* outcome is legal from both arming states (T-F8), so acquire's single result is the whole story; (d) the input is disabled in the five committed states so the head can never desync from the schedule (REPLAY rebuilds from the composer, honoring edits made in done/error).
- **Delegation record:** none — task executed directly by the production worker. Inputs consumed: plan.md I2 entry + the I2 production brief; T-entries F1 (letters contract), F2 (SPEED_STEPS/dotMsOf), F3 (acquire/release/lost), F4 (attach/on/off/hide + pointer policy), F5 (unlock-in-gesture/toggleMuted), F6 (start/pause/resume/cancel + the six callbacks + progress shape), F7 (encode/readHash/applyHash/shareUrl + no-hashchange law), F8 (the ten-line wiring sketch, eventFromTorchResult, effects, ARM_MATRIX semantics), U1 (COPY + helpers), I1 (the body/data-state world + tape geometry), craft-floor.md (no new visual patterns introduced — wiring only).

## T-I3 — Responsiveness, motion, capability layers · 2026-08-29

- **Status:** `awaiting-approval` (plan.md updated).
- **Changed files:** `css/main.css` (the motion grammar + capability layers + responsiveness completion), `js/app.js` (three minimal hooks: the `.tg-new` punch diff, the fire pulse, the `.cap-low` mirror), `index.html` (one line: `viewport-fit=cover`); `.impeccable/review/i3-*.png` (10 validation captures); doc updates for this entry's bookkeeping (plan.md status, state.md phase note, this log). No logic module touched; no test touched; `npm test` 466/466 exit 0 before and after.
- **What was built — one authored motion grammar, not scattered effects.** Two curves defined once in `:root` (`--drive` for driven parts, `--snap` for seats) and EVERY moving part reads one of them, bounded ≤200ms, nothing bounces/floats/loops (every keyframe is iteration-count 1; there are no infinite animations at all, inside or outside broadcasting):
  1. **Tape advance** — `.tape-strip` transitions `transform 190ms var(--drive)` (slack taken up, then seated). The strip now moves like paper on a sprocket feed; the traverse TARGETS remain progress-driven from I2's `--strip-shift` (the one motion exempt from the stroke budget — it arrives on the broadcast's clock; the easing is the register's).
  2. **Punch press** — a genuinely new punched group settles under the die (`@keyframes punch-press`, 140ms, scaleY 1.55→1 + opacity 0.35→1 — no fade-in, a press). app.js diffs group identity (`charIdx:char:marks`) between re-punches and marks only-new groups `.tg-new`: typing "SOS" over the prefill presses all 3, appending presses exactly 1, deleting presses 0. The boot render seeds the identity set (the machine presents its tape; it does not punch one).
  3. **Read-head lamp pulse + ground echo** — per mark, `onProgress` alternates `body.fire-a`/`body.fire-b` (swapping the ANIMATION NAME is the restart mechanism — no reflow tricks): the head lamp fires (`lamp-fire-a/b`, 180ms) at every width, and at ≥720px the ground echoes the light faintly (`body::before` radial wash, 190ms, peak 9% alpha, pointer-transparent) — the desktop broadcast echo. Leaving the broadcasting states clears both classes (verified: paused → 0 running lamp-fire animations).
  4. **State-plate snap** — mounting bays (#hazard/#why-camera/#denied/#screen-note/#corridor/#share), transport rows swapping inside the bay, and the status station's printed lines seat with `plate-snap` (130ms, translateY −2px, no opacity anywhere) — a mechanical reconfiguration, not a carousel fade.
  5. **Light state changes** — the standby→caution→GO beat lighting and the idle→armed→live→held lamp states now carry their changes as transitions on the snap (zone border/background/halo 160ms; lamps 140ms; zone legend/line/status-legend ink 140ms).
- **Capability layers (Arcade's law, brief §3).** `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` and `html.cap-low` flatten EXACTLY the same layers: glow custom properties → zero-alpha, all transitions/animations → none, with the crisp live ring kept (`0 0 0 2px rgba(255,164,60,.7)` — the ring carries the information). Save-Data is a request header CSS cannot see, so app.js mirrors `navigator.connection?.saveData` onto `<html class="cap-low">` at boot (guarded `typeof` read; Chrome-default `saveData:false` boots without the class — both directions verified). **The broadcast flash is NOT one of these layers** — documented in the CSS capability block as F4's law: the light is the message; the layers flatten decoration, never signal (the screen overlay's own no-transition CSS re-verified present).
- **Responsiveness completion.** `viewport-fit=cover` + `@supports (padding: max(0px))` safe-area insets on the deck/nameplate/footplate (the tape band stays full-bleed by design; insets floor at 16px on notch-less devices — mechanism verified); `min-height: 100vh` fallback ahead of the existing `100dvh`; a landscape short-viewport block (`max-height:480px` + landscape: band 152→128px with proportionate char/marks/slot metrics, tighter nameplate) so 740×360 reads whole with vertical scroll only; `:focus-visible` no longer mutates the focused element's border-radius (I1's lamp outline kept — the outline follows each element's own corners).
- **Validation evidence** (rig of the T-I2 class: own http.server + headless Chrome 152/CDP with `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` IMPOSED viewports; the fake torch injected pre-navigation via `Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument`; journey clicks through the REAL input pipeline — `Input.dispatchMouseEvent`, which grants the user activation the AudioContext master clock needs: synthetic `el.click()` leaves `resume()` pending and the conductor never starts, a rig finding, not an app defect; rig scripts in /tmp/i3-rig, deleted after the run; zero repo residue). **102 checks, 0 failures** across four passes:
  - **`npm test` 466/466 exit 0** (before and after the build; no JS logic touched).
  - **Pass A 67/67 — responsiveness + polish:** overflow sweep 320/360/390/768/1024/1280 × idle/ready/broadcasting/broadcasting-on-screen/paused/done/permission-denied — 42/42 cells with `documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth === viewport`, `body.scrollWidth ≤ viewport`, ZERO interactive controls outside the viewport; landscape 740×360 all 7 states no-break with the 128px short band engaged, input clear of the band, `100dvh` tracking the viewport; touch targets ≥44px at 320+390 (all keys/step-keys/input ≥48px); plate legends 13px / input 16px at both widths; safe-area padding live; tab order sane (`message → broadcast → tone → step-learner → copy-link`, wraps, no trap) with the lamp ring on real Tab keypresses; `:active` mechanical press caught mid-hold (`matrix(1,0,0,1,0,0.85)` → `none` at rest); zero console events.

Three conscious notes: (a) the ground echo is width-gated at ≥720px (the brief's "desktop broadcast echo") while the head-lamp pulse reports at every width — on desktop the torch path never occurs (R1: desktop resolves to screen), so the echo's live scenarios are wide torch devices (landscape phone/tablet) and windowed screen broadcasts, exactly where the instrument is visible while the light speaks; (b) `viewport-fit=cover` is the one index.html line — without it the safe-area insets can never be non-zero on iOS, so the insets and the cover go together; (c) all motion is bounded single-iteration — the brief allows bounded looping DURING broadcasting, but per-mark restarts make looping unnecessary (the pulse marks marks, not time).
- **Delegation record:** none — task executed directly by the production worker. Browser tool unavailable in this worker (as in T-I1/T-I2) — rendered inspection via the substitute CDP path, disclosed here.

- **Judgment (does the motion strengthen the Register?):** Yes — mechanical, not costume. Every stroke reads as an instrument part: driven paper (tape 190ms drive), a die settling (punch press on genuinely-new groups only — diff-proven), a mark spoken (per-mark lamp fire), the light echoing on the ground while it speaks (≥720, honest width gate), plates SEATING (130ms translate, no opacity fades), light states riding the snap. Nothing bounces, floats, loops, or decorates: glows exist only where the instrument is live, and each flattens with information parity (ring/progress/position). Two non-gating wording nits recorded for I5: the CSS comment "every moving part reads one of these two curves" overstates — `.key` (120ms) and `.message-input` (150ms) micro-transitions and the ground echo use generic `ease`/`ease-out` rather than `--drive`/`--snap` (all within budget, mechanical in feel, no overshoot); and the punch-press keyframe includes opacity 0.35→1 alongside scaleY (a die-press, not a fade-in — the "no opacity anywhere" claim applies to plate-snap, where it holds).
- **Disposition:** I3 → `completed` in plan.md; `auto-approved (ultron-supreme)` recorded in state.md. Next per plan: Q2 (privacy/photosafety/a11y audit — now unblocked), then I4/I5, Q3/D0.

### 3 — A11Y

- **GREEN · keyboard operability (I3-swept, re-confirmed):** fresh-load real key events — Tab order `message → BROADCAST → TONE → speed group` (matches I3's full sweep incl. wrap/no-trap); **Enter on the focused BROADCAST key operates it (idle → preflight)**; `:focus-visible` computed on the focused key: `outline: 2px solid rgb(255, 180, 84)` — the amber lamp ring, radius unmutated (I3).
- **GREEN · aria-live status line actually announces:** `.station-status` carries `role="status" aria-live="polite"` (index.html:91; role=status implies aria-atomic — the whole rendered region is the announcement). Exactly ONE of the 15 state lines renders per state (the other 14 are `display:none`, therefore excluded from the accessibility tree); CDP `Accessibility.getFullAXTree` confirms the `role=status` node with `live=polite` exists and the tree carries **only the current state's line** (idle load → only "TYPE A MESSAGE…"; after Enter → only "THE GATE BEFORE THE FIRST BROADCAST.") — the live region's payload is always exactly the current line; the progress lines additionally mutate `textContent` inside the region on every mark/pause/error (the reliable announcement trigger).
- **GREEN · contrast on tape/ground primary surfaces — T-I1's numbers re-verified, THE SPOKEN-INK FINDING re-measured, classified, and FIXED:** calibration first — my WCAG math reproduces T-I1 exactly (tape ink #2b2013/#e2d3a8 = **10.70:1**; cream/ground ≈ 14.4–14.9:1; secondary 7.5:1 — all pass, unchanged). The finding: **`--ink-sent` (css/main.css:41, applied to `.tg-spoken .tg-char` at :313)** — the printed letter of every already-spoken group. Font 15px/600 Spline Sans Mono → normal text → **4.5:1 required**. Re-measured against the ACTUAL letter-row background (the band's vertical gradient interpolated at `--char-top`: **#dfcfa5** phone / #e0cfa5 wide / #dfcea4 landscape): **2.70:1** (2.15–2.91 across stops; T-I1's 2.8 was vs the flat #e2d3a8). **Classification: INFORMATIONAL, not decorative** — in the `done` state `applyHeadForState` marks ALL groups spoken (`setHead(last, length)`), so the tape's complete message record — the done copy's "THE TAPE KEEPS THE MESSAGE" — rendered at 2.8:1; redundancy (head position, amber ring, numeric progress) does not exempt text under WCAG 1.4.3. The same precise measurement exposed the sibling token: **`--ink-current` #8a5410** (`.tg-live .tg-char` :322 + `.tg-unknown .tg-char` :325 — the live letter AND the persistent unknown-char letters) at **4.02–4.06:1** — also under. **FIXED NOW (mechanical: two token values, hue families preserved, visual hierarchy preserved):** `--ink-sent` → **#645637** (4.61–4.79:1 across the actual glyph spans; corrected at retry — the first attempt #6a5a3c was justified against #e2d3a8 and #e6d7b0, which are NOT letter-row backgrounds, and measures only 4.30–4.47:1 at the glyph spans; see the T-Q2 verification + retry entries), `--ink-current` → **#7a4a0c** (4.80–4.99:1 at the glyph spans — above the 4.5:1 requirement; the first report's "4.92–5.23:1" range was the same light-anchor error). The Register's de-emphasis grammar survives: unspoken 10.3 > live 4.80–4.99 > spoken 4.61–4.79 at the glyph rows (corrected at retry) — spoken remains the lightest tape ink, still less than half the unspoken contrast, still a paper-family hue. Re-validated: pixel differential on the captures (letter-row luma bins 115–135: 343→179; 80–104: 344→522 — the letters moved into the darker range while paper/hole pixel counts stayed identical), vision pass on the before capture ("very faint… subtle and easy to overlook") vs the after (letters clearly legible, still visibly softer than near-black), and the FULL 41-check rig re-run green with the fix in. **Recorded for I5:** the two values are the Q2 a11y floor, not a design judgment — the finish reviewer should re-judge the paper-hue de-emphasis aesthetic (css/main.css:40–46) with the constraint that retuning must keep ≥4.5:1 on the letter rows and the live>spoken>nothing ordering.
- **GREEN · labels on all controls:** `label[for=message]` on the composer; `label[for=step-learner|standard|brisk]` on all three speed radios inside a `fieldset` with `legend`; every button carries its text — rig-verified across the page.
- **GREEN · hazard plate readable by a screen reader:** DOM order is title → pictogram → imperative/advisory → facts → first-run line → keys; the aside carries `aria-label="CAUTION — FLASHING LIGHT"`; the pictogram is `svg role="img" aria-label="Warning pictogram: a lamp emitting rapid flashes."` with `focusable="false"`; the fact list includes the 3-flashes safety line and the LEARNER-default-under-it line — rig-verified end to end.

- **GREEN · no color-only signaling:** all 15 state lines carry legend + copy text (state is always named in text), the corridor zones carry legend + line text, spoken/live/unspoken is backed by the numeric progress line and head position — rig-verified.

Rig in /tmp, deleted after the run.

### ONE unmet criterion — the spoken-ink fix does NOT reach 4.5:1 at the actual letter-row backgrounds

- **The audit's own methodology, applied to its own fix, fails.** T-Q2 measured the FINDING at the interpolated letter-row background (`#dfcfa5` phone / `#e0cfa5` wide / `#dfcea4` landscape — from `.tape-window`'s gradient `#cbb98b 0% / #ddcca1 10% / #e6d7b0 42% / #e2d3a8 58% / #d9c89d 88% / #cbb98b 100%` at `--char-top`), but justified the FIX against two anchors that are NOT letter-row backgrounds: "4.50:1 vs #e2d3a8, 4.68:1 vs the worst-case lightest stop #e6d7b0". For a DARK ink on lighter paper the LIGHTEST stop is the BEST case, not the worst — the worst case is the darkest background the glyph sits on. **The verifier reproduced the audit's interpolated backgrounds exactly** (same three hex values, same 2.70:1 for the old `--ink-sent` #8d7a55, same 4.50/4.68 for the anchors, same 10.70:1 tape-ink calibration — the math pipelines agree), then measured the FIXED token at the actual glyph spans (`.tg-char` top `--char-top`, heights 15px phone / 16px wide, band 152/176/128px):
  - phone 390: glyph y∈[27,42]px → 17.8–27.6% of the gradient → **#dfcfa5…#e2d2a9** → **#6a5a3c `--ink-sent` = 4.33:1 (worst, glyph top) … 4.47:1 (best, glyph bottom)**
  - wide ≥720: y∈[34,50]/176 → 19.3–28.4% → #e0cfa5…#e2d2aa → **4.34…4.47:1**
  - landscape ≤480: y∈[22,37]/128 → 17.2–28.9% → #dfcea4…#e2d2aa → **4.30…4.47:1**
  - No glyph row at ANY geometry reaches the 42% stop (#e6d7b0); the flat #e2d3a8 (58% stop) is never the letter-row background either. The `.tape-window` inset shadows only darken further at the row (top highlight is y<3px). **All values 4.30–4.47:1 < 4.5:1** (WCAG 1.4.3 normal text — 15px/600 mono, the audit's own classification). The done-state message record — "THE TAPE KEEPS THE MESSAGE", all 13 groups `.tg-spoken`, verifier-confirmed in DOM — renders below threshold.
  - Corroboration: the verifier's vision pass on the audit's own `q2-contrast-done-after-390.png` capture reads the spoken letters as "legible but faint … blends somewhat into the beige tape" — consistent with ~4.3:1, not a comfortable ≥4.5:1.
- **Sibling token (non-gating):** `--ink-current` #7a4a0c at the same actual glyph spans = **4.80–4.99:1** — PASSES 4.5:1 (the audit's "4.92–5.23:1" range again overstates by anchoring light; the CSS comment's "≥5:1" fails marginally at the glyph top, 4.80). No action forced; re-state honestly at retry.
- **Fix direction (mechanical, one token value):** darken `--ink-sent` within the same paper-family hue until it clears 4.5:1 at the DARKEST actual glyph background (#dfcfa5): `#665638` → 4.61 (worst) / 4.75 / 4.78 vs flat; `#645637` → 4.65/4.79/4.82. Keep the live>spoken ordering constraint the audit itself set: at #665638 spoken 4.61 vs live #7a4a0c 4.84 (same bg) — ordering preserved with ~0.2 margin; #625436 (4.79) leaves only ~0.05. Recommend `#665638` or `#645637`, then re-measure at the glyph spans (top = worst) and refresh the four captures + the pixel-differential evidence. Nothing else in the fix chain needs to move.

## T-Q2 retry — the spoken-ink fix re-anchored at the actual glyph backgrounds · 2026-08-29

- **Status:** `awaiting-approval` (plan.md updated).
- **Changed files:** `css/main.css` (ONE token value — `--ink-sent` #6a5a3c → **#645637** — plus both tape-ink comments restated at the actual letter-row measurements), the 4 evidence captures `.impeccable/review/q2-contrast-{done,broadcasting}-{before,after}-390.png` (refreshed), and bookkeeping (the T-Q2 entry's wrong anchors corrected in place above; plan.md status; state.md). No other source, test, or asset touched.

### The fix, chosen by re-measuring at the glyph spans myself

- **Arithmetic re-derived from the CSS values, independently (not copied from the verifier).** Relative luminance per WCAG 2.x — linearization `C/12.92` below 0.04045, else `((C+0.055)/1.055)^2.4`, `Y = 0.2126R+0.7152G+0.0722B`, ratio `(Y_lighter+0.05)/(Y_darker+0.05)`. The `.tape-window` gradient (css/main.css:229–231) is `#cbb98b 0% / #ddcca1 10% / #e6d7b0 42% / #e2d3a8 58% / #d9c89d 88% / #cbb98b 100%`; the glyph spans interpolate it at `.tg-char` top `--char-top` + heights 15/16px: **phone 390** y∈[27,42]/152 → 17.8–27.6% → **#dfcfa5…#e2d2a9**; **wide ≥720** y∈[34,50]/176 → 19.3–28.4% → **#e0cfa5…#e2d2aa**; **landscape ≤480h** y∈[22,37]/128 → 17.2–28.9% → **#dfcea4…#e2d2aa** — every digit reproduces the verifier's backgrounds exactly. Calibration reproduces the shared pipeline: old `--ink-sent` #8d7a55 = **2.70**:1 at #dfcfa5; the failed fix #6a5a3c = **4.33/4.34/4.30** at the three glyph-top rows (the verifier's 4.30–4.47 range, worst = landscape top); #6a5a3c vs #e2d3a8 = 4.50 and vs #e6d7b0 = 4.68 — the wrong anchors, reproduced and thereby confirmed wrong (for dark ink on lighter paper the lightest stop is the BEST case; neither anchor is a letter-row background).
- **Candidates measured at the actual glyph-top rows (#dfcfa5 / #e0cfa5 / #dfcea4):** #665638 → **4.61 / 4.62 / 4.57**; #645637 → **4.65 / 4.66 / 4.61**; (reference #625436 → 4.79/4.80/4.75; live `--ink-current` #7a4a0c → **4.84 / 4.85 / 4.80**). Full glyph-span ranges: #665638 4.57–4.75, #645637 **4.61–4.79**, live 4.80–4.99, unspoken `--ink` #2b2013 10.3.
- **Choice: #645637.** The verifier's quoted "worst 4.61" for #665638 anchored #dfcfa5, but the darkest glyph row is the LANDSCAPE top #dfcea4, where #665638 reaches only **4.57** (cushion 0.07 over 4.5) — and the verifier itself noted the inset shadows darken the row further (my rendered-pixel check below confirms: the measured phone glyph-top row is #decea4, one count darker than the interpolation). #645637 keeps **4.61 at the true worst row (cushion 0.11)**, still the same paper-family hue one value darker, and the audit's ordering constraint holds with MORE margin than #665638 at every geometry: live 4.80–4.99 > spoken 4.61–4.79, worst-case gap **0.19–0.20** (live 4.80 vs spoken 4.61 at #dfcea4; #665638 would have left 0.23 at that row but only 4.57 compliance). Both verifier-suggested values pass; the darker one buys compliance headroom against the rendered-reality margin the verifier flagged.
- **Rendered-pixel confirmation (not just the math):** modal background colors sampled per device row of the refreshed done capture: cssY 27 → **#decea4**, 30 → #e0cfa6, 33 → #e0d0a6, 36 → #e1d1a7, 39 → #e1d1a8, 42 → #e2d2a9 — the actual rasterized band tracks the pure interpolation within one 8-bit count (the top row measurably darker = the inset-shadow contribution). **Token #645637 vs the darkest RENDERED letter-row background #decea4 = 4.60:1** — the fix passes against reality, not only the model.
- **The non-gating `--ink-current` comment marginality — resolved without moving the value:** the comment's "≥5:1" claim was marginally false at the glyph top (4.80); the honest range **4.80–4.99:1 at the glyph spans** now stands in the comment (above the 4.5:1 requirement, ordering intact). The VALUE is untouched — the verifier's retry scope was one token, and darkening live ink for a non-gating comment would destabilize the visual hierarchy the audit set.

The BROADCASTING journeys were real broadcasts, not static snapshots: each ran idle → BROADCAST → preflight (first-run plate) → ACK → why-camera → CONTINUE (exactly **1 gUM** in-gesture, fake constraints deep-equal shape) → probe BOTH directions → torch-ready → GO → beats → broadcasting, captured at MARK 8 with the read-head over the second L of HELLO (H, E, first L spoken-dim, current group ringed); the rig recorded alternating `{"advanced":[{"torch":true}]}` / `torch:false` applies per mark (10 on/off pairs by MARK 8) and **zero console events/exceptions** in every scenario.

**Every capture opened and vision-verified (the validity gate):** all six show the section their name claims — nameplate, composer (frozen + disabled-look mid-broadcast), the full-bleed paper band with punch holes + printed letters + sprocket feed + read-head bridge, the exact state line (IDLE copy / SENDING + "TORCH LIGHT SPEAKS THE TAPE." + live MARK line), corridor zones with GO lit in the broadcasting frames, the correct transport row (STOP/PAUSE/SWITCH TO SCREEN/TONE), speed detents (LEARNER selected), share row, footplate — no black or blank regions, no half-loaded state, no wrong-section-behind-a-right-filename file, no clipped or truncated button labels at the 320 floor, no horizontal overflow.

### The detector (run ONCE)

`node …/impeccable/scripts/detect.mjs --json index.html css/main.css js/app.js` — **regex-degraded mode** (its HTML parser deps — htmlparser2/css-select/css-tree/domutils — are not installed on this machine; the tool says so itself: custom properties, selector matching and computed contrast are NOT evaluated, findings are an undercount — disclosed here). Result: **exactly one warning**, the same single pre-assessed `.tape-window` inset bottom-stripe ("side-tab accent border", css/main.css:226) carried since T-I1: it is the paper band's SYMMETRIC material shading — top highlight `inset 0 3px 0 rgba(255,250,230,.45)` + bottom shade `inset 0 -3px 0 rgba(66,50,26,.4)` of the same paper hue, both inset — a physical paper edge, not a UI accent border on a card; the regex sees only the bottom stripe. Unchanged by I2/I3/Q2 (re-run now over the current targets confirms: no new findings). **Passed to I5 with the standing T-I1 rationale; nothing mechanical to fix.**

### Direction-contract critique (the shipped render vs the contract in index.html)

- **THESIS** — holds: the tape IS the translation (typing punches; the broadcasting captures show the traversing head over the live group, earlier letters spoken-dim), and the category defaults are refused — no centered card (machine plates full-width or composed), no textarea (the composer is an inset mono slot), no blue button (the one amber lamp key). Vision pass: "reads as a custom machine instrument, not stock web chrome."
- **OWN-WORLD** — holds at all three widths: dark warm ground, the full-bleed paper band (the only full-bleed element), punch holes showing ground through paper, sprocket feed along the band's bottom edge, stencil legends, ONE amber accent family for live states. Recognizable with content removed.
- **STORY** — holds: nameplate subtitle + tape + BROADCAST make the offer legible in seconds; the broadcasting frame demonstrates the mechanism mid-act (SENDING, live MARK line, GO lit, frozen composer).
- **FIRST VIEWPORT** — verified by measurement, not just by eye: at 390×844 nameplate (0–105) → composer (121–229) → tape band (243–395, 152px full-bleed) → status (395–452) → BROADCAST key (483–541 = **57% of the viewport — thumb height, inside the first viewport**); at 320×690 BROADCAST at **72%** (inside the 690px fold); at 1280×800 at **62%** with the composed instrument. `scrollWidth === clientWidth` at all three widths (no overflow). One process note: an early vision query mis-framed the 390 fold as 422 CSS px (my prompt error — the analyzer honestly read raw 2× pixels as CSS px) and reported BROADCAST below the fold; the geometry probe above is the correction of record — the key is inside the first viewport at every inspected width.
- **FORM** — Register grammar everywhere: transport keys are machine keys, speed is named detent keys (not a slider), plates seat; no stock chrome found in any of the six vision passes.
- **Material gaps found: none.** Two design-level observations join the I5 inputs below as re-judgment points (not defects, no action by I4): (a) the read-head bridge visually crosses the top of the current letter's glyph as the tape traverses — by design (the head reads the tape from above; the letter stays readable under the bridge in every capture, and the live ring marks the group); (b) dot/dash punch differentiation is size-only (14 vs 42px — unambiguous in fact, softened only in down-scaled analyses).

3. **The `.tape-window` paper-edge detector warning** (T-I1 → unchanged through T-I4's detector run): symmetric inset paper-edge shading, rationale above.

## T-Q3 — Real-device test script + checklist (handover to user hardware) · 2026-08-29

- **Status:** `awaiting-approval (handover — awaiting user real-device results)` (plan.md updated). Q3's validate line is "user-reported results logged" — the deliverable is the script; the completion evidence arrives when the user reports back and a T-Q3 results entry is logged. No verifier dispatch: there is nothing for a verifier to independently re-run (the whole point of Q3 is hardware this harness does not have).
- **Changed files:** `docs/DEVICE-TEST.md` (new — the handover document; the only substantive write), plus this entry's bookkeeping (plan.md Q3 status, state.md). **Zero source/test/css/html changes** — Q3 is a documentation deliverable by plan.

### The deliverable

`docs/DEVICE-TEST.md` — a human-facing script the user runs on their own phones. Structure:

1. **Setup (§1):** three real paths to a secure context, ordered by genuine simplicity: (A, recommended) `python3 -m http.server 8080` / `npx serve -l 8080` + `cloudflared tunnel --url http://localhost:8080` → a real `https://*.trycloudflare.com` URL (no account, no interstitial; zero-install variant `ssh -R 80:localhost:8080 localhost.run` [corrected at retry 2026-08-29 — this spot originally claimed `npx localhost.run 8080`, a command that cannot run: no npm package exists, and localhost.run documents SSH-only usage]; localtunnel's loca.lt mobile interstitial explicitly warned against); (B) deploy-first — any static host (the D0 path), the most realistic option if deploying anyway; (C) desktop needs no tunnel (`localhost` is a secure context). The honest LAN caveat is stated up front: `http://192.168.x.x` on a phone is NOT a secure context → `navigator.mediaDevices` undefined → the app lands in screen mode, so LAN-HTTP cannot exercise the torch path (with the Android-only `chrome://flags/#unsafely-treat-insecure-origin-as-secure` power-user escape hatch).
2. **Android/Chrome script (§2, A1–A13):** the full journey against the real UI vocabulary — type HELLO → BROADCAST → hazard plate (first-visit vs returning beat) → **why-camera plate verified BEFORE the permission prompt** (A3) → CONTINUE-to-the-prompt gesture → ARMING (probe blink disclosed) → TORCH READY + **test flash = exactly one blink** (A5) → LEARNER broadcast (A6: objective anchors — HELLO = 16 marks, `MARK 16 OF 16`, ≈11.8 s at LEARNER vs 7.4/4.9 s at STANDARD/BRISK; subjective legibility of …. . .-.. .-.. ---) → all three speed steps (A7) → **pause/resume mid-broadcast incl. BRISK — marks must stay distinct (the F6 fix meeting real hardware; the 120 ms lead-window defect class called out)** (A8) → backgrounding the tab → auto-pause + return + resume + camera-indicator-released-at-DONE check (A9) → torch-loss path (A10: manual SWITCH TO SCREEN always; true loss via built-in Camera app or mid-run site-settings revoke — whichever is physically doable, both offered) → replay (A11) → denied path with screen offer (A12) → share-link round-trip on a second device with a non-default speed (A13).
3. **iOS/Safari script (§3, i1–i8):** same core journey compressed; iOS 17.4+ torch support framed honestly as new-ish — the run RECORDS which channel each iOS device took (torch vs screen fallback + the exact fallback line), per R1's medium-confidence iOS finding; background/lock expected to hard-suspend → auto-pause.
4. **Desktop spot-check (§4, D1–D2):** screen-mode positive framing; the no-camera-prompt expectation stated with the honest live-detection nuance (a webcam-equipped desktop may legitimately show why-camera + prompt once — no UA sniffing per R1 §8 — before landing in screen mode; a camera-less desktop never prompts). Both pre-paths marked correct; the requirement is positive framing + no torch.
5. **Results table (§5):** one row per device — model / OS / browser / torch-or-screen+reason / test-flash=1-blink / rhythm legible per LEARNER-STANDARD-BRISK / pause-resume distinct marks / background auto-pause / sync feel (aligned-lead-lag) / camera released at DONE / share round-trip / notes — plus the free-text prompts (per-speed sync wording, BRISK-specific feel, copy confusions).
6. **Pass criteria (§6):** 8 objective must-holds (copy-order, one-blink, three-speed completion with correct totals/durations, pause/resume distinctness incl. BRISK, hidden-tab auto-pause, camera release at DONE, positive screen landing on every fallback path, share round-trip); subjective judgments explicitly not self-graded; the known-acceptable outcomes listed (probe blink, R1 quirk-class fallbacks with printed reasons, webcam-desktop prompt, iOS screen fallback) so the user doesn't file working-matrix behavior as bugs.
7. **Report-back + TORCH_LEAD_MS retune (§7):** the R2 honesty carried through — the 40–130 ms LED latency was **cited, never measured**; Q3 is the measurement. The user reports per-torch-device sync feel per speed, start-vs-mid-message consistency, BRISK legibility, stuck-on LED occurrences; the retune rule is written down (light lags → raise toward 200 in 30–40 ms steps; light leads → lower toward 0; aligned with readable BRISK → keep 120; a lag surviving 200 ms lead = slow-HAL class → screen channel, also a valid result). Range 0–200 and the 45 ms BT.1359 tolerance named so the retune stays inside the researched envelope.
8. **Honesty scaffolding throughout:** every check tagged [objective] or [subjective]; the photosafety note leads (LEARNER under 3 Hz, BRISK ~5/s, every subjective check answerable by ear); expected vs acceptable-vs-defect outcomes separated before the user ever opens the app.

### Accuracy of the script against the built app

Every step was written from the shipped code, not from memory of the plan: the journey order (why-camera plate rendered before the prompt via `promptExpected()`, the prompt riding CONTINUE's gesture — app.js `beginArm()`), the test dot (one dot-length blink ~300 ms after TORCH READY at the current step's `dotMsOf` — app.js `fireTestDot()`), the choreography beats (STANDBY/CAUTION 650 ms each, auto-GO), the exact status-line vocabulary per state (copy.js verbatim: "LAMP ARMED. STANDING BY FOR THE WORD." / "SCREEN ARMED. HOLD IT UP — IT IS THE LAMP." / "TORCH LIGHT SPEAKS THE TAPE." / "LIGHT SOURCE LOST — CONTINUING ON SCREEN." etc.), the progress composition ("MARK x OF 16 · LEARNER — 5 WPM", replay restarting at MARK 1 — `renderProgressLine()` clamps to ≥1), HELLO's mark count and durations re-derived from the timing law (16 marks; 49 dot-units → 11.76/7.35/4.9 s at 5/8/12 WPM), the transport keys per state incl. mid-broadcast SWITCH TO SCREEN, the DONE-state camera release (states.js `release-torch` effect), and the desktop matrix nuance (live detection only — states.js ARM_MATRIX comments). Two desktop/iOS expectations were corrected against source during writing rather than assumed from the plan text.

### ONE unmet criterion — the "zero-install" localhost.run variant is a command that cannot run

`docs/DEVICE-TEST.md` §1 Option A offers: *"No-cloudflared variant, zero install: `npx localhost.run 8080` also prints a real HTTPS URL."* This is not a real command:

- **No npm package named `localhost.run` exists** — `npm view localhost.run` returns registry **E404** (verified directly against registry.npmjs.org on 2026-08-29). `npx localhost.run 8080` therefore fails with an npm package-not-found error before doing anything. The promised "real HTTPS URL" can never print.
- **localhost.run's own documentation is SSH-only** — the service describes itself as "a client-less tool" and documents exactly `ssh -R 80:localhost:8080 localhost.run` (docs fetched 2026-08-29); no npx/npm usage appears anywhere on the site.
- The false claim propagates into the bookkeeping: the T-Q3 entry ("zero-install variant `npx localhost.run 8080`") and state.md's Q3-built record ("localhost.run zero-install") both assert it.

This fails the setup-path criterion ("commands… real, correctly spelled, and would actually serve this repo") and grazes the nothing-impossible judgment criterion — a handover doc whose entire value is that the user can run its commands verbatim must not contain one that errors out. Fix is one line: replace with `ssh -R 80:localhost:8080 localhost.run` (genuinely zero-install on macOS — ssh ships with the OS) or drop the variant; the adjacent localtunnel warning is accurate and can stand. Returned from `awaiting-approval` once per the T-Q2/T-I2 convention.

### The deliverable

`docs/DEPLOY.md` — a human-facing deploy handover. Structure:

1. **What ships (§1):** the bundle inventory taken from the actual repo tree, not guessed — 12 files, all load-bearing: `index.html`, `css/main.css`, and the full `js/` set of ten modules (`app.js` + the nine it imports, verified via `grep '^import' js/app.js` → morse/schedule/torch/screen/audio/conductor/states/hash/copy). Stated properties each verified locally: all asset references relative (`index.html` links: `css/main.css` line 11, `js/app.js` line 273 — so any host or subpath serves it unchanged, zero config); the only third-party request is the Google Fonts stylesheet pair (lines 8–10, matching the Q2 network audit); PRODUCT.md/DESIGN.md/docs/ marked optional-to-publish; `test/`, `package.json`, `.impeccable/` marked never-publish (keeps dev artifacts private when the host account is free-public, e.g. GitHub Pages). One-time prep command given verbatim and **executed during writing**: `mkdir -p /tmp/register-bundle && cp -R index.html css js /tmp/register-bundle/` → counted output = exactly the 12 files the doc claims; probe folder deleted afterward (zero residue).
2. **Host options (§2)** — three genuinely simplest, each with exact steps, HTTPS-automatic confirmation (the hard product requirement: getUserMedia/torch is secure-context-only, stated up front with the honest screen-mode degradation if HTTP ever appears), cost (free tier), and caveats:
   - **Netlify Drop** (fastest, no account): drag the folder at `app.netlify.com/drop` → live in seconds at `https://<random>.netlify.app`. Honest caveats verified against current sources: anonymous (unclaimed) sites are auto-deleted — Netlify's own blog only says the site "expires on its own" with no timeframe, community references cite 1–24 h, so the doc says exactly that and prescribes claim-to-keep rather than asserting a number; a spurious login wall is a known extension/ad-blocker glitch (incognito workaround), per the Netlify support thread. **[corrected at retry 2026-08-29 — both the "no account" pitch and the no-timeframe framing were wrong per the T-D0 verification: anonymous drops are password-protected until claimed (Drop quickstart + the 13-year blog), the claim window is ~1 h (cut from 24 h in Jan 2022), and unclaimed sites are suspended then deleted ~a week later; Option A now states all of this and positions the host as fastest-with-an-account — see the T-D0 retry entry.]**
   - **GitHub Pages** (permanent, free): exact repo → `git init -b main` … `git push` command block → Settings → Pages → Deploy from a branch (main /(root)) → `https://<user>.github.io/<repo>/`, Enforce HTTPS once the cert issues. Free-plan **public-repo-only** requirement stated (per GitHub's official docs) with the reassurance that the bundle contains no secrets. **`.nojekyll` judged:** Jekyll drops underscore-prefixed paths; this bundle has none (verified live: `find -name '_*'` over the repo → zero matches, files and dirs), so it is NOT required and `js/`+`css/` serve untouched — the doc says so and adds that an empty `.nojekyll` is harmless belt-and-braces for future wider publishes.
   - **Cloudflare Pages** (permanent, free account): free account → dash.cloudflare.com → Workers & Pages → Create application → Pages → "Drag and drop your files" Get started → name → drag folder → Deploy → `https://<name>.pages.dev`. Caveats from the current official docs: direct-upload projects cannot switch to Git integration later; updates = new atomic deployments; drag-and-drop limits 1,000 files / 25 MiB each (this bundle: 12 small files).
3. **Post-deploy verification checklist (§3):** HTTPS padlock on each device (with the why — getUserMedia secure context); Android Chrome → torch path (`TORCH READY` + exactly one test blink + one LEARNER broadcast); iOS 17.4+ Safari → **record which channel** (torch or screen — both valid per R1); desktop → screen mode (with the honest webcam-desktop may-prompt-once nuance); share round-trip from a real phone with a non-default speed. Each step cross-referenced to DEVICE-TEST.md (§1 Option B is the deploy-first path; the full A1–A13 / i1–i8 / D1–D2 script doubles the smoke test as Q3 evidence).
4. **What deliberately does NOT exist (§4):** no server, no analytics, no cookies/storage (Q2-audited: zero storage APIs, no request beyond the Google Fonts pair ever, no request carries the message), no secrets/env/keys → literally nothing to configure before or after; deploying = copying 12 files.

## T-D0 verification — 2026-08-29 — FAILED, returned to `in-progress`

### ONE unmet criterion — Option A (Netlify Drop) describes account-free behavior that no longer exists: anonymous drops are password-gated until claimed

`docs/DEPLOY.md` §2 Option A promises: *"**Option A — Netlify Drop (fastest: no account at all)** For an instant link — trying the app from a real phone in under a minute… 3. Done: the site is live within seconds at `https://<random-name>.netlify.app` — HTTPS automatic, zero configuration."* Against **current (2026-08-29) primary sources, the anonymous drop is not publicly viewable**:

- **Netlify's official Drop quickstart** (docs.netlify.com/start/quickstarts/netlify-drop-quickstart/, fetched 2026-08-29), under "Is your project public?": *"If you drop without signing in, your project URL is protected with a temporary password until you claim it. After you claim it by logging in or signing up, the outcome depends on your plan."* — On credit-based plans (new accounts since 2025-09): *"if your team has private by default enabled, your project stays private until you make it public."*
- **Netlify's own "The 13-year story of Netlify Drop" blog** (netlify.com/blog/thirteen-years-of-netlify-drop/ — the same source the T-D0 entry cites; page metadata last-updated 2026-08-29, present tense): after a 2024 fraud wave *"the team password-protected anonymous sites until someone claimed them"* — *"Your drop is available to you as soon as it uploads, but Netlify does not make it public until someone attaches an identity to it."*
- Consequence for the handover: a non-dev user who follows Option A verbatim, then opens the URL **on the phone** (the option's stated purpose), hits a password wall the document never mentions. "No account at all" is false for any publicly-viewable outcome — public access requires claiming, claiming requires signup. This is the Q3 class of failure (a step whose described outcome cannot occur as written); the deliverable's own stated standard is verbatim-runnable.
- **Secondary, same option — the auto-deletion caveat timeframe is stale:** DEPLOY.md says *"their own docs don't promise a timeframe (community references range from 1 to 24 hours)."* The blog the T-D0 entry itself cites states specifics: the claim window was cut **from 24 hours to one** (January 2022; the current CLI docs likewise say *"claim it within one hour"* for anonymous deploys), and the early-2025 retention policy *"suspended unclaimed sites, then deleted them a week later."* The doc's prescription (sign up while the tab is still open, claim, URL stays the same) is directionally safe advice — but the "no timeframe anywhere" framing understates the 1-hour claim urgency, and "the URL stays the same" omits the credit-plan private-until-made-public wrinkle above.

Fix direction (one option block rewrite): either re-title and re-step Option A honestly — anonymous drop deploys in seconds **but the URL is password-protected until you sign up (free) and claim it, within about an hour; unclaimed sites are suspended and deleted about a week later; on new accounts check the project is public after claiming** — or drop Option A and lead with Cloudflare Pages / GitHub Pages. The `app.netlify.com/drop` landing-page marketing ("no account required… live, shareable link") is what the current doc tracks; the quickstart's "Is your project public?" section is the operative fact. Informational only, not a D0 criterion: DEVICE-TEST.md §1 Option B's loose "drag the folder in, get a permanent HTTPS URL" (Q3's text, points users here for the real steps) inherits the same Netlify imprecision and should be touched only if the retry edits it.

## T-D0 retry — 2026-08-29 — the one-block fix delivered, back to `awaiting-approval (handover)`

The T-D0 verification's single unmet criterion fixed exactly as directed — **rewrite chosen over removal** (Netlify Drop stays visible as the fastest path to a working link, now described honestly), plus the stale deletion-timeframe caveat in the same option block. Nothing else in the deliverable changed.

### The fix (`docs/DEPLOY.md` §2 — the only user-facing write)

1. **Option A repositioned from zero-account to fastest-with-an-account:** title "(fastest: no account at all)" → **"(fastest: live in seconds; free account to make the link public)"**; the lead sentence "For an instant link — trying the app from a real phone in under a minute" → the honest framing (deploy is drag-and-drop in seconds; making the link publicly viewable takes a free signup).
2. **The password-gate reality is now the option's spine (step 2):** a signed-out drop deploys within seconds at `https://<random-name>.netlify.app` (HTTPS automatic, zero configuration) **but the URL is protected by a temporary password until the site is claimed** — the drop is available to the dropper as soon as it uploads; anyone else opening the link, including a phone, hits the password page (quickstart "Is your project public?"; 13-year blog: "Netlify does not make it public until someone attaches an identity to it").
3. **Claim step with the real clock (step 3):** sign up (free) and claim **while the Drop tab is still open — the claim window is about one hour** (cut 24 h → 1 h in Jan 2022; the CLI docs say "claim it within one hour"); the URL stays the same when claimed.
4. **Check-public-after-claim step (step 4, new):** accounts created since 2025-09 are on credit-based plans where projects can be private by default — after claiming, make the site public if needed and **confirm by opening the URL in a private/incognito window (no password page = public)**. This is the operational answer to the credit-plan wrinkle the verifier flagged.
5. **Stale deletion caveat replaced (the secondary finding):** "their own docs don't promise a timeframe (community references range from 1 to 24 hours)" → **unclaimed sites are suspended, then deleted about a week later; the claim window itself is ~1 hour** (early-2025 retention policy per Netlify's own blog).
6. **The zero-account question answered plainly (one line, new §2 intro note):** none of the three options is truly account-free — every path to a publicly viewable HTTPS URL requires a free account (Netlify to claim the drop, GitHub, or Cloudflare); Netlify Drop is still the fastest, the account step just comes after the drag-and-drop. No genuinely-zero-account path exists among the verified options, so the doc says that in one line instead of implying one.
7. **Footer updated:** host-facts line now notes the retry correction and points at both the T-D0 and T-D0 retry entries for sources.

Untouched inside the option: the extensions/ad-blocker login-wall glitch note and the update-later note (Deploys → drag the folder again) — both still accurate.

The URL stays the same when you claim." **Suspend-then-delete retention is the first caveat:** "unclaimed sites are suspended, then **deleted about a week later** (the claim window itself is ~1 hour)." **Check-public-after-claim is step 4 (new):** credit-plan private-by-default since 2025-09, make public in settings, confirm "in a private/incognito window — no password page means public."
- **The zero-account question answered plainly:** §2 opens with "none of these three options is truly account-free — every path to a publicly viewable HTTPS URL below requires a free account somewhere (Netlify to claim the drop, GitHub, or Cloudflare)".

"Live in seconds" survives only where it describes the deploy itself and is immediately paired with the public-viewability caveat.

### 2. Operative Netlify facts re-verified by this verifier against primary sources (fetched 2026-08-29)

- **Drop quickstart** (`docs.netlify.com/start/quickstarts/netlify-drop-quickstart/`): *"If you drop without signing in, your project URL is protected with a temporary password until you claim it."* and *"After you claim it by logging in or signing up, the outcome depends on your plan… On Credit-based plans (Free, Personal, Pro): if your team has private by default enabled, your project stays private until you make it public."* → matches DEPLOY.md steps 2 and 4 exactly. (The quickstart states no claim-window duration.)
- **Netlify CLI docs** (`docs.netlify.com/cli/get-started/`, "Anonymous deploys"): *"You have one hour to claim ownership of the deployed project by logging in or creating a Netlify account. After one hour, unclaimed projects are removed. When you claim the project, it follows your team's default project visibility."* → the ~1-hour window (step 3) is Netlify's own stated clock, and the claim-follows-team-visibility line corroborates step 4.
- **"The 13-year story of Netlify Drop"** (`netlify.com/blog/thirteen-years-of-netlify-drop/`): *"the team password-protected anonymous sites until someone claimed them… Netlify does not make it public until someone attaches an identity to it"*; *"In January 2022, the team cut the claim window from 24 hours to one"*; *"a retention policy that suspended unclaimed sites, then deleted them a week later."* → the password gate, the 1-hour window, and the suspend-then-delete-~a-week retention (the caveat block) all trace verbatim. Minor tension noted and judged immaterial: the CLI docs say unclaimed CLI deploys are "removed" after one hour — DEPLOY.md is about the Drop UI and tracks the blog's fuller policy; if anything the stricter CLI reading reinforces the claim-now advice.
